A section of `status` that reads anything beyond the manifest, the registry, the store and what the repository and the pinned tree hold on this machine, that reads the tracker or the network or starts a build or a run, or a section list typed back into §4.6, is this paragraph wrong.

**78. Revision 78 (2026-10-04).** A frontier emptied only by held tickets ends a `--fill` run, settled by Mitchell on #1855 and #1862, built by #1857 and landed by #1861. #1850 counted a blocker as open while the branch that records it is not yet in `main` (revision 75), so a `track work` run over a chain closed the first link and found the frontier empty, the rest of the chain held until /specs merges that branch; with `--fill` that empty frontier started a `/frontier` session, which filed the next batch as if the track had nothing left. Mitchell ruled on #1855 to hold rather than plan, and #1857 built it, `track_next::Frontier::Held` the state: §5's A track's plan keeps its sentence on `--fill` and carries the ruling after it, word for word, "With `--fill`, a frontier emptied only by held tickets ends the run, naming each held ticket and the branch it waits on." `CONTEXT.md`'s **Frontier** named no held state and ended "which is when `/frontier work <track>` fills it again" (revision 77), which said more than the code does; as Mitchell settled on #1862, the clause is replaced and the entry reads "…is empty when `next` says `none`. A ticket waiting only on a blocker's branch that `main` does not contain yet is held: the frontier it empties is not filled again, but waits on /specs to merge that branch." Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test track_work` runs `a_frontier_emptied_by_a_held_ticket_ends_the_fill_run_naming_it`, in which a `--fill` run whose frontier is empty but for a held ticket starts no `/frontier` session and ends naming the ticket and its branch, and `a_frontier_empty_with_nothing_held_starts_the_fill_session`; `grep` finds no "fills it again" in `CONTEXT.md`. A `--fill` run that starts a `/frontier` session while a ticket of the track is held, or ends without naming a held ticket's branch, is this paragraph wrong.

**79. Revision 79 (2026-10-04).** What `status` reads is bounded by kind, not by list, settled by Mitchell on #1880 and landed by #1913. Revision 77 widened §4.6 to "the pinned tree's layout where it is present (the `oracle` and `parity` sections)", and the sentence was still narrower than the code by one section: `status::git_section` runs `git log` and `git status` over the checkout, and §5 names `git` as one of the local file's regions. Mitchell ruled on #1880 to bound the reads by kind rather than list them again, having checked them against the code: no section reads the tracker, `ticket` rendering the store's readings, and the repository reads are `git`, `phpt`'s first-parent chain and the pin `oracle` and `corpus` read. §4.6 now reads, word for word, "It reads the manifest, the registry and the store, and what the repository and the pinned tree hold on this machine (the `git`, `oracle` and `parity` sections), only ever reading them: never the tracker or the network, never a build or a run. It computes nothing else; a section that needs a command of its own is a missing instrument, not a shell one-liner in a region." Revision 77's check sentence reads by kind as the new sentence does; the `Status` rustdoc in `xtask/src/cli.rs` and `status.rs`'s module doc say the same, and the help line `status` carries in the map grows by nothing. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep` finds no "pinned tree's layout" in §4.6, `xtask/src/cli.rs` or `xtask/src/status.rs`; `cargo test -p xtask --test map` holds the map under its room. A section of `status` that reads the tracker or the network, or starts a build or a run, is this paragraph wrong.

**80. Revision 80 (2026-10-05).** §4.3 states how a crate row's oracle variant is read, settled by Mitchell on #1948. #1782 ruled that a crate may be read under a build of the pinned tag with configure flags of its own, pinned per platform, a Linux build by "an image digest and the binary's md5", and #1890 built the grammar with two facts the ruling did not name: every platform's table carries a `path`, since a digest names where a binary came from and not where it is, and a row naming a variant the pin lacks fails that crate and not the manifest. #1889's recipe raised a third, what a Linux table's `image` names: the base image the build is made from, from which a runner rebuilds the binary and holds its md5 with no registry, or the image built, from which a runner could copy the binary only once it is pushed to a registry, whose choice and credentials are a person's. Mitchell ruled that the grammar stands as #1890 built it: every platform's table carries `path`, the install prefix #1889 builds to (`/opt/dax-php/oracle/<variant>/bin/php`); a Linux table's `image` names the base image's manifest digest, which #1889 pins and rebuilds from, and the lane checks the binary by its md5; an unknown variant fails that crate's readings, not the manifest. §4.3 carries the ruling in **What a crate row's oracle variant is**, after the recipe's environment, and `[variant.zend-test]` stands in `oracle.toml` under it (e6219e9e). The paragraph says a variant's regimes are the main oracle's and nothing of what a regime carries, which is #1933's. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask` runs `oracle::variant`'s `a_linux_build_names_its_image_and_the_macos_build_names_none` and `a_relative_path_is_the_pins_directorys_and_an_absolute_one_is_kept`, and `--test variant` runs `the_fixture_whose_row_names_a_variant_the_pin_lacks_fails_its_crate` and `the_fixture_whose_variant_binary_differs_from_its_md5_refuses_the_run`; each Linux `image` in `oracle.toml` is the base image's digest `scripts/oracle-build.py` names for its platform. A platform's table read without a `path`, a Linux `image` naming the built image rather than the base image's manifest digest, or a manifest refused over one row's variant, is this paragraph wrong.

**81. Revision 81 (2026-10-05).** The bundle's order is held as features against requirements, settled by Mitchell on #1778 and landed by #1821: the edge example of §4.1 and the "No second registry" row read `order = "registration"`, checked as features against requirements. Two more sentences say the bundle order is held as topological, §3's "Dependency direction" row and §4.1's paragraph on what `api-check` reads; the ruling names neither, so they stand until a person settles them (#2039). `api-check` learned the value in #1882 and the manifest carries it since 0a3809e5; the order itself is php's registration order sorted as `zend_sort_modules` sorts it (design revision 85), held by a witness against the oracle rather than by the crate graph. Rejected, as in the design: the oracle's literal list as data, and topological order with a divergence row. Nothing that an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: the edge example and the "No second registry" row read `order = "registration"`, and `api-check` fails `xtask/fixtures/api-check/registration`, a bundle whose feature omits a requirement.

**82. Revision 82 (2026-10-05).** The correctness lanes run release with debug assertions, settled by Mitchell on #1980 and landed by #1994. No lane ran what `cfg(debug_assertions)` guards over the corpus: the nightly witness and the phpt lanes ran release builds, and the per-commit witness skips without the pinned php; #1980's first candidate puts the freed-use guard and the request leak gate behind those assertions, and a lane that never compiles them measures nothing of them. §7 carries the ruling in **Which build a correctness lane runs**, word for word: "Every correctness lane (witness, the per-commit phpt lane, Zend/tests, `tests/*` and the nightly full run) runs the `release-da` profile: release with debug assertions and overflow checks, without thin LTO or a single codegen unit. The bench lane and the shipped binaries are plain release." The profile is a table of `Cargo.toml`, and `xtask/src/oracle/profile.rs` the one statement of it the harness reads, where the engine's binary's profile had been the strings `release` and `debug` in each instrument that looked for it; the oracle job's comment carries the cost measured when it landed, before the gate and the quarantine. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: the oracle job stays under its timeout; `release-da` costs at most 1.3× release on the Zend/tests subset. `cargo test -p xtask` runs `oracle::profile`'s `the_oracle_job_builds_its_binaries_in_the_lanes_profile`, which fails on a cargo line of the oracle job that names `--release` or another profile, and `the_lanes_build_is_the_engine_before_release_and_passed_over_when_behind`.

**83. Revision 83 (2026-10-06).** The store's log is one file per instrument, and an index of one file per key answers what the log was read whole for. Settled by Mitchell in the architecture review of 2026-10-06, which found the instruments spending their time outside their work, and landed by #2164. The one `log.jsonl` was read whole by every question asked of it: `status --section phpt` parsed 234,588 lines to keep 1,338, `done` and each `--staged` instrument scanned it for the latest reading over a key, and it grew by about 45 MB a day. §4.2's **Store** now names three things. The scope's file is its last run, as before. The index, `<instrument>/<scope>/<key>.toml`, is the latest reading of the scope over each key, the newest 32 keys of a scope kept and a reading under an oracle pin kept per pin; "the latest reading over a key" is one file read. The log, `log/<instrument>.jsonl`, is history: the permits section, usage and the dashboard read it, and no question about a key does, save `permit`'s look for evidence of a key the index has let go. A store written before keeps its old log as `log.migrated.jsonl` once the first record or read splits it, the rename that claims the file deciding which process does. §5's retire lands a worktree's log in the main checkout's log and index. §7's telemetry and §10's rule 1 read the log where they read `log.jsonl`. The dashboard and the console read the log's directory. `CONTEXT.md` gains no entry, and nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: a store whose only log is another instrument's gives `status --section phpt` no rows, and `done` over a crate whose file holds a reading over another key finds its pass over the key now in the index with no log in the store.

**84. Revision 84 (2026-10-07).** The harness reaches the engine through a process, and links none. Settled by Mitchell in the architecture review of 2026-10-06, which found every hook relinking the engine after any edit under `crates/` or `ext/`: the in-process engine was a convenience and not a principle. Landed by #2166. `xtask` linked `php_bundle` with `linked`, so its binary carried 89 workspace crates, and the Stop hook, `permit`'s fallback and pre-commit relinked them before judging anything. `php-linked`, `sapi/cli` built with the bundle's `linked` set, now serves what the linked engine served: the witness lane's snippets and crosscheck's renderings, each with a fresh engine, its streams, status and entered natives written back as files. As many binaries run as threads serve, and they outlive one crate's pool into the next. A snippet that ends its binary, on a signal or an abort, is that witness's failure alone, and the next is served by a binary started again. `compile-identity` loses its linked rung and dumps through the engine's binary, which honours php's dump flags. §4.5's condition 1, §7's witness rows and §8 say so. `php-linked` carries `pcntl`, as the CLI does, so a snippet that forks forks it and never the harness; the mark `process` still sends such a fence to `php-witness`. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo tree -p xtask -e normal` names no crate under `crates/`, `ext/` or `sapi/`, and a fence that sends `SIGABRT` to its server fails alone while the fence after it passes (`xtask/tests/witness.rs`).

**85. Revision 85 (2026-10-07).** The witness trace is a runtime switch, and the witness lane's binaries are the hosts' own. Settled by Mitchell in the architecture review of 2026-10-06 (Q17, its own ticket) and landed by #2168. `witness-trace` was a cargo feature of `php_engine`, carried up through `php_ext`, `php_bundle`, `php_sapi` and each host, so `php-witness`, `php-cgi-witness`, `php-fpm-witness` and `phpdbg-witness` were second builds of the engine beside `php`, `php-cgi`, `php-fpm` and `phpdbg`, and a witness run after a phpt run compiled the engine again. The feature is gone: the request holds its trace as an `Option` that is `None` unless its engine was built with `trace_natives_to` or its test served it traced, and the entry a `php_macros` expansion builds tests that field on its way in, one branch per native entered. `php_sapi::prepare` reads `PHP_WITNESS_TRACE` in every host, so the witness lane serves a `process` fence through `php` and a `request` fence through `php-cgi`, `php-fpm` or `phpdbg`, built as every other lane builds them; `oracle::hosts` names one binary per host. The branch's cost is a bench measurement on #2168 for a person to read. §4.5's condition 2 and §8 say so, and §8's sentence that the linked engine carries no `pcntl`, which revision 84 made untrue, now says what keeps the harness unforked. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: no `Cargo.toml` in the workspace names `witness-trace`, `PHP_WITNESS_TRACE=<file> php -r 'json_encode(1);'` appends `php_ext_json`'s `json_encode` to the file, and the witness readings of every crate are what they were with the feature.

**86. Revision 86 (2026-10-07).** §4.5 condition 2 says what a kernel is and that a crate the attributes anchor counts its kernels beside its natives, settled by Mitchell on #1711 (2026-10-03) with the amendment, in the ruling's words: "A kernel is a function of a crate's `ops` module, as the manifest's witness.coverage scope names it (design §8, `php_engine::ops`), `pub` or `pub(crate)` at indentation zero, covered by a fence in its own doc block; a crate the attributes anchor is counted by its kernels beside its natives." Condition 2 said what a native is and that a crate declaring no attribute keeps the subject count it had, and said nothing of a crate with both, `php_engine`, whose natives the attributes anchor and whose kernels the `ops` module holds; the witness instrument counts both, and the condition now says so. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask witness -p php_engine` names a kernel without a fence of its own as missing, beside a native without one.

**87. Revision 87 (2026-10-07).** §8 says what a witness fence's `locale=<name>` does, the counted skip #1678 built, settled by Mitchell on #1701 (2026-10-03), in the ruling's words: "An `expect` line may name `locale=<name>`, repeatable, for a snippet that needs a locale. Before the fence runs, the oracle and then the engine each try `setlocale(LC_ALL, <name>)`; where neither host takes any named locale, the witness is a `skip` naming it, counted as skipped in the crate's reading and never a pass or a fail, as a `.phpt`'s `--SKIPIF--` dies on `!setlocale(…)`; where either host takes one, the fence runs and is held as any other." A snippet whose output depends on a locale could otherwise pass on a machine that has it and fail on one that does not, with nothing in the reading to tell the two apart. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: a fence naming a locale neither host has reads `skip` naming it, and the crate's reading counts it among the skipped.

**88. Revision 88 (2026-10-07).** §4.6 says which sections fold their passing rows, and revision 52's sentence says where every row is rendered, settled by Mitchell on #1617 (2026-10-03): one shared paragraph, "`budget`, `phpt`, `witness` and `mutants` render the rows a session acts on (a fail, a skip, no reading, not yet created, a budget within a tenth of its ceiling, a phpt subset whose comparison fails) and count the passing rest in one line naming `--uncut`, which renders every row; `--format json` renders every row.", and revision 52's "`xtask status --section crosscheck` and `--section phpt` render every row with its numbers whatever the key says" gains "under `--uncut`, and for a failing row of a `porting = true` crate without it". #1608 made the four sections fold, which keeps the local facts block under the engine's cap as crates land, and §4.6 still described each as one row per crate or subset until revision 77 replaced the per-section clauses with `--help`'s list, so the fold was stated nowhere. A later folded section only adds its name to the paragraph. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask status --section budget` without `--uncut` ends in the line counting the passing crates and naming `--uncut`, and with it lists every crate.

**89. Revision 89 (2026-10-07).** §7 says how a `--ri` row naming a linked library's version is met, settled by Mitchell on #624 (2026-09-28): the first of the ticket's three shapes, the engine links the version the pin's build links. Rendering this build's own fact honestly does not reach such a row: `PCRE Library Version`, `PCRE Unicode Version`, `iconv library version`, `SQLite Library` and `libxml Version` name which build of a library this engine happened to link, against which build the oracle linked, and differed between the keg and the linux image when #624 measured them. So the engine links, or vendors, the version the pinned keg links, as #717 ruled for PCRE2 10.48, and an oracle bump moves those versions with it; each library that differs is its own build ticket, filed as it is reached. Rejected, by the ruling: a registry key per rendered row (`crosscheck:<name>:<line>`), which needs a key kind and a matcher the registry does not have and a reason per machine; and the row standing red under `porting = true`, a key that would never come off. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask status --section crosscheck` reads pass for `ext/pcre/ri`, `ext/iconv/ri`, `ext/dom/ri`, `ext/libxml/ri` and `ext/pdo_sqlite/ri`, measured so on main at 2c13f612.

**90. Revision 90 (2026-10-08).** §4.1's grammar sentence cites the code and counts nothing, settled by Mitchell on #1909 (2026-10-07) and landed by #2250. It said the predicates were ten and the one qualifier was `unless`, while the code had eleven predicates and four qualifiers (`unless` and `unless_pattern` on `deny_pattern`, `with` on `deny_argv` and `require_argv`, `deny_macros` on `deny_types`): the list's third drift. It now names `manifest::Predicates::NAMES` and `Predicates::orphaned_qualifier`, and keeps the words for `check`, `avoid_from`, `generated` and `unless` that a name does not say. The example manifest's `unsafe.homes` text and §6's table row cite the homes the rule names in place of "four", as ruled on #1935, and so do the `new-ext` and `new-sapi` fixtures. `xtask status` gains the `crates` and `edges` sections, which the design's §4.1 and §4.2 and the map render as regions (#1921): both read the manifest alone, so a region over either is held by `inputs=seams.toml`, and only the crates section's every row, under `--uncut` and in JSON, also says whether the crate is created. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test status` holds both sections to the manifest, `cargo test -p xtask --test map` finds the map's `crates` region, and `grep -rnE 'predicates are (ten|eleven)|(three|four|seven) (places|homes)' harness-design.md xtask/fixtures` matches ledger rows and §14's record of revision 7 alone.

**91. Revision 91 (2026-10-08).** A person's approval in the session lets an agent type a change to the harness's own files, settled by Mitchell on #2247 on 2026-10-07: "as long as I have given permission (clearly and understandably through AskUserQuestion tool) I want you to be able to edit harness files, loosen, tighten, commit, merge, whatever." §4.4 gains **What a person's approval admits** and §11's Human-only paragraph the sentence the ticket states: what only a person does stays the person's decision, and typing it becomes an agent's act once the person approved it in the session. The approval is the answer Claude Code records for an `AskUserQuestion` call whose chosen option's preview is the change as a diff; it admits the write that realizes that diff exactly, never one the model could have authored, and never a subagent's. `harness.self-shell`, ruled by Mitchell on #625 on 2026-09-28, is named beside `harness.self` in §4.4's list of rules, §6's table and §11: a `Bash` line writing one of the six paths is denied at the call, and the same approval lifts it. Nothing is reversed: `steer` still lands an addition settled on a ticket without asking, and a person's hand on the files is unchanged, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test permit_approval` admits the `admit` case of `xtask/fixtures/approval/` and denies every `deny-*` case, and `permit_after`'s `a_write_the_person_approved_stands_and_one_that_differs_is_put_back` keeps the approved `Bash` write and puts back the other; a forged answer admitted, or an approved change denied, is this paragraph wrong.

**92. Revision 92 (2026-10-08).** The harness builds what it serves through in the lanes' profile, settled by Mitchell on #2217 (option 2) and landed by #2265. `oracle::profile::build` built `php-linked` and the hosts' binaries in the profile `xtask` itself was built in, `Profile::this_build` read off `build.rs`'s `XTASK_PROFILE_DIR`: dev for `cargo xtask` and pre-commit, release-da where CI built a release-da harness. Revision 82 already put every correctness lane, witness among them, on release-da, and the reason for the coupling, the harness linking the engine and sharing its compiled crates, went with revision 84. A witness reading's key covers the source and not the profile, so under the coupling one key could be read off two builds, a deep-recursion fence overflowing in dev and passing in release-da. §7's **Which build a correctness lane runs** carries the settled sentence, word for word: "Every binary the harness builds, `php-linked` and the hosts' own, is built in the lanes' profile, whatever profile the harness itself was built in." `Profile::this_build`, `Profile::of_dir` and `XTASK_PROFILE_DIR` have no reader left and go. #2167 measured the trade on this machine: a rebuild of `php-linked` after a one-line engine edit costs 40 s in release-da against 16 s in dev, and the lane over `php_engine` takes 61 s against 119 s; release-da's directory is about 2 GB a checkout against dev's 9. Nothing that an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `profile::build` under a dev harness puts its binary under `release-da/` (`oracle::profile`'s test), and `grep -rn this_build xtask/src` finds nothing.

**93. Revision 93 (2026-10-08).** §3's **Dependency direction** row and §4.1's paragraph on what `api-check` reads cite the manifest's bundle order instead of restating it, settled by Mitchell on #2039 (2026-10-07) and landed by #2257. Revision 81 left both sentences saying the bundle order is held as topological, a check `api-check` no longer runs: the manifest's `[[edge]] php_bundle` row reads `order = "registration"`, held as features against requirements (#1882). Both now say, in the ruling's words, that `api-check` "checks the bundle against the `order` its `[[edge]]` names", which stays true when the value changes. `Order::Topological` in `api_check/bundle.rs` is dead code, filed as #2255. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n opological harness-design.md` matches ledger rows alone, and `api-check` fails `xtask/fixtures/api-check/registration`, a bundle whose feature omits a requirement.

**94. Revision 94 (2026-10-08).** §4.3 and §8 say a regime carries args as well as ini, settled by Mitchell on #1933 (2026-10-07) and landed by #2257. Since #1887 a regime of `oracle.toml` carries `args`, passed after the recipe's on a binary's command line, and since #1822 an `Invocation` carries a list of regimes, `regimes`, and the linked engine takes `-e`, building with extended info as a host's `-e` does, and refuses any other argument (`oracle::Linked`); both sections still said a regime is ini. As ruled, the post-#1822 proposal lands, worded for `php-linked` (revision 84): §4.3's Interface names "the `[regime.<name>]`s of the pin it stands under, in order, each one's args after the recipe's and each one's ini between the recipe's and the test's", and §8's revision 55 paragraph says a regime's args "stand after the recipe's on a binary's command line; `php-linked` has none and takes `-e` as a host does, building with extended info (#1822), so a fence under a regime of any other argument is marked `process` (#1780, #1887)". A fence still names one regime (`witness/fence.rs`). The first proposal on #1933, under which a regime of args was always marked `process` and compile-identity's linked compiler refused the run, is superseded. §4.3's reason `-l` is not in `[regime.dump]` still says "a regime is ini"; the settled proposal gave it no words, so it waits on a person (#2295). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask` runs `oracle`'s `a_regimes_args_stand_after_the_recipes_and_ahead_of_every_ini`, `witness`'s `a_fence_under_a_regime_of_args_is_served_where_they_reach_or_not_at_all` and `fence`'s `a_regime_attribute_names_one_block_of_the_pin_and_carries_no_ini`.

**95. Revision 95 (2026-10-08).** A reading's `tree` is its `key`, under the reading's version 2, settled by Mitchell on #1719 (2026-10-07) and landed by #2257. Since #1318 a reading is stamped with the key of its scope's files and of every workspace crate in the scope's declared dependency closure, and §4.2's field comment, its **Store** sentence and `CONTEXT.md`'s **Reading** still called it the scope's tree hash. As ruled, the field comment carries the declared-graph wording of the coordinating session's comment of 2026-10-03, word for word; the Store sentence says a reading is fresh while the scope's key still matches and that the closure is the graph the manifests declare (`cargo metadata --no-deps`, ADR-0011); `CONTEXT.md`'s **Reading** says "the scope's key (§4.2)" and leaves the definition to one place; and D.18's title is "The key as freshness". The field no longer holds a tree hash, so it is renamed `key` under a new `v`, as revision 76 requires of a renamed field: every reading is written with `v = 2` and `key`, and a reading with no `v` is version 1, whose key is spelled `tree`, which the store still reads from a scope's file, the index, the log and a log handed in, so the readings a worktree took before the rename stand. A `v` the harness does not know is refused. `Reading::tree()` is `Reading::key()` in `xtask.lock`, and §4.4's `unless` and §4.5's condition 1, which named the field or the hash, name the key. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test store` runs `a_reading_is_written_under_its_version_with_its_key`, `a_reading_of_the_first_version_spelling_its_key_tree_still_reads` and `a_reading_of_a_version_this_harness_does_not_know_is_refused`, and `reading::tests` runs `a_key_the_index_let_go_is_found_in_a_line_of_either_version`. A reading written without `v = 2`, or a version 1 reading the store no longer reads, is this paragraph wrong.

**96. Revision 96 (2026-10-08).** §3's **Lock-free extensions** row names the once types and `thread_local!`, and D.19 states the package-wildcard gap, settled by Mitchell on #1909 (2026-10-07) and landed by #2257. Since #1781's row and #1888, `ext.no-locks` also denies `Once`, `OnceLock`, `LazyLock`, `OnceCell`, `LazyCell` and `once_cell::*`, with `deny_macros = ["std::thread_local"]`, and the generated `ext/clippy.toml` renders `disallowed-macros`; the row named the locks, cells and atomics alone. It now names the once types under `clippy::disallowed-types` and `thread_local!` under `clippy::disallowed-macros`, as proposed on #1909. D.19 says what #1888 measured: the generator reads a package's `lib.rs` line by line, so a package wildcard reaches its root items only, and `once_cell::*` renders to paths that do not exist, which clippy only warns about, so that wildcard denies nothing at clippy until #1910 lands. §4.1's grammar sentence, the ruling's third part, landed as revision 90. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask budget` reads `ext/clippy.toml matches its rendering from rule ext.no-locks`, and the file carries `disallowed-macros` naming `std::thread_local`.

**97. Revision 97 (2026-10-08).** §4.4's **Implementation** says what `permit --after` does with a `harness.self` file that moved across a `Bash` call, settled by Mitchell on #1931 (2026-10-05) and landed by #1972: #1895's narrowing stands, plus a `done` backstop. The sentence is the ruling's, word for word: "After the call, a harness.self file whose blob moved is put back only where the call's line can be read to write it: a redirection into it, a writer handed its path, an interpreter whose program names it, or a program run by its path whose bytes name it. A change the line cannot write is kept and named, since a person's edit made while a call runs is lost by putting it back; until a person commits or restores it, that change is one of `done`'s unmet conditions (#1931)." The sensor records each change it keeps in the store of the checkout the file is in, the file, the blob it was before and the call it moved under (`permit::kept`), and `done` lists every record that stands as unmet: while the file is not at `HEAD`'s blob, so a commit of the file or a restore to `HEAD` meets it, and the record is then spent. A write the person approved in the session (revision 91), one `steer` made and one put back leave no record, so `done` tells an approved change from an unattributed one by the record alone. §4.5's condition 6 still says a change to these files in the diff is a note; the backstop is read from the store and not from the diff, and whether §4.5's list names it is a person's to settle (#2309). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test permit_after` runs `a_kept_change_is_unmet_in_done_until_a_person_commits_or_restores_it`, and the approved case of `a_write_the_person_approved_stands_and_one_that_differs_is_put_back` leaves no record; a kept change `done` does not list, or an approved one it does, is this paragraph wrong.

**98. Revision 98 (2026-10-08).** A ticket closes when its work is on `main`, settled by Mitchell on #2270 (option 1) and landed by #2311 with #2249, in the ruling's words: "A closed ticket means its work is on `main`; there is no *held* state. #2249's refusal lands as built. `/build` ends at "ready to merge" (boxes ticked, `done` met, a ready record written); the close happens at the merge, and the merge commit's hook runs `xtask ticket close` for each `#n` its subject names, so no merger can forget it. `xtask track work` reads the ready record instead of the close. CONTEXT.md's Frontier entry loses *held*." #2163 to #2168 were closed on a stack no coordinator merged, so the tracker said done while `main` lacked the work, and nothing but a person noticed. `xtask ticket ready <n>` holds a ticket as a close does and writes `branch.<name>.ready`, the successors' numbers or `nothing`, in the configuration every worktree shares; `xtask ticket close` refuses a close of work done at a commit `main` does not contain (#2249), and a withdrawal stands as before, since it says nothing of the work is in the tree. The post-commit hook, on a commit with two parents, and the post-merge hook, which a fast-forward of `main` runs, call `xtask ticket merged`, which closes each open ticket the merge commits' subjects name with its ready record's successors, names one with none, and closes nothing off `main`. `track work` reads a session's end off the ready record, or a withdrawal at the tracker. §5 says so, and the build and specs skills end and integrate in that order. Nothing that an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test ticket` holds the ready record and the close at the merge, `--test hooks` holds the two hooks reaching `ticket merged`, and `--test track_work` holds a ready branch retired and its record kept.

**99. Revision 99 (2026-10-08).** `permit` owns the whole approval path, settled by Mitchell on #2281, #2282 and #2283 on 2026-10-08 and built by #2314. The audit record revision 91 gave a function hook is written by `permit` itself (#2281, the `[budget] harness` ceiling not raised): one entry per admitted change in the main worktree's store, an `Edit` or a `Write` at the call and a `Bash` line from `--after` once what it wrote stands, the store chosen over a committed `docs/approvals/` file because an entry written at the call stands before the person has let the call run, and a committed file for a call they then refused would be an untracked file in a checkout every session shares. `harness.transcript` is added (#2282): a shell write under `~/.claude/projects/`, where the command hook reads the person's answers, is denied in the shapes `harness.self-shell` reads, with the text "A session's transcript is Claude Code's record of what the person said; no agent writes it." The function-hook modules join `harness.self`'s scope and `harness.self-shell`'s alternations (#2283), a widened value landed by a person's hand or under their approval in the session, since `steer` refuses it; §4.4, §6, §11 and the layout say so. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test permit_approval` runs `each_adapter_reads_its_own_evidence_and_an_admitted_change_is_one_audit_entry`, `permit_after`'s `a_write_the_person_approved_stands_and_one_that_differs_is_put_back` finds one entry for the approved line and none for the other, `cargo test -p xtask --test permit` fires `harness.transcript` on each `deny*.json` of its fixtures and on no `allow*.json`, and `the_hook_modules_are_a_persons_files_under_the_widened_rows` denies an `Edit` and a `sed -i` of `harness-plugins/hooks/harness.ts` and lets a `cat` of it through; an approved change with no entry or two, a transcript a shell line writes, or a hook module an agent edits unapproved once the widening stands, is this paragraph wrong.

**100. Revision 100 (2026-10-08).** §4.1's account of what `api-check` reads says how it holds design §4.10's list of surfaces since #2020's ruling, built by #2251 (design revision 111): it fails an `ext/*` crate's library dependency on another that no row of the list names with both crates, and writes each extension's `pub` items beyond `extension()` to `ext/<name>/surface.lock`, diffed as an item lock is, where it printed them and held each against the names a row typed. The manifest's `ext.pub-beyond-extension` text still says the second, and its rewording is a person's (#2352).

**101. Revision 101 (2026-10-08).** §4.4 states the after-half of `permit`, settled by Mitchell on #1671 (2026-10-03) as the code stands and landed by #1732 once #1972 had built the sixth point's backstop (revision 97). #1516 landed the sensor #625 ruled: `permit --after` over the `PostToolUse` hook puts back and denies a `harness.self` file a `Bash` call moved, against the blobs `permit` recorded before the call, and §4.4 described `permit` as the judge at the call only. The five points are the ruling's: the record keyed by `session_id` and `tool_use_id` and taken before any build decides whether it judges, so a call with no record is never put back; tracked files only; the hook's checkout and the call's, not every worktree; what the call wrote kept as a named blob before the file is put back; and `steer`'s write standing when the line runs `xtask steer` and its reading names every addition, the one edge, a comment written before steer in the same call, accepted because a comment binds nothing. The ruling of 2026-10-07 adds the rule's `exempt` from revision 70 to the same paragraph; #1931's sixth point, with #1895's narrowing to the four routes `writes.rs` reads, already stands in the paragraph after it (revision 97). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `xtask/tests/permit_after.rs` holds each point: a call with no record left as it stands, an untracked file left, a write the rule exempts standing, concurrent sessions keeping their own records, a moved file put back and denied with its blob named, and a `steer` call that adds a row standing while a write beside it is put back.

**102. Revision 102 (2026-10-08).** A reading under the oracle's recipe is fresh only under the pin it records, settled by Mitchell on #1408 (2026-10-01) as the rule in prose, with no list of instruments: the design names the constant `reading::UNDER_RECIPE`, not its three current members, so the next instrument that runs under the recipe does not make the sentence stale. Since #1387 a reading of an instrument under the recipe records `recipe`, `oracle::Pin::digest` over every section of `oracle.toml`'s parsed values, and `Reading::fresh_for` holds it to the pin now beside the key; §4.2's shape gains the `recipe` line, its **Store** sentence and §4.5's condition 1 say the reading is fresh only while the pin's digest matches too, "the pin" and not `[recipe.env]` alone, since the digest covers every section. The settled sentences named the scope's tree hash, which revision 95 renamed the reading's `key`; they are fitted onto `key`, their words otherwise as settled. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test done` runs `a_reading_under_the_recipe_is_stale_once_the_recipe_changes`, an entry laid under `oracle.toml`'s `[recipe.env]` staling the touched crate's readings of each instrument of `reading::UNDER_RECIPE` and no other; a reading of one of them that stays fresh across a moved pin, or one of another instrument that a moved pin stales, is this paragraph wrong.

**103. Revision 103 (2026-10-08).** A port renders the pinned Mac keg, settled by Mitchell on #635 (2026-09-28): with #809 ruled for one platform, §7 gains **Which build a port renders**, in the ruling's words, for the six extensions whose `--re` and `--ri` columns #424 measured to differ between the keg and a Linux build of the same version (glibc's locale constants, PCRE2 and Unicode, PDO's drivers and SQLite, libxml, iconv). The ruling named "CI's macos-15 job", the hosted runner #809 chose; #2253 has since moved CI's oracle job to the self-hosted Mac mini, `dax-oracle`, which pours the same keg by the pin's md5 (`.github/actions/pinned-php`), so the sentence names that job and runner, its words otherwise as ruled. The answer is a sentence and no `[recipe.normalise]` key, so `oracle.toml` is unchanged. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `xtask/tests/crosscheck.rs`'s `FLOOR` says in its rustdoc that it rises onto one of the six only under this sentence, and `oracle.toml`'s `[recipe.normalise]` carries no key for a library version or a driver list; a floor raised onto a rendering normalised for either, or a `--ri` row's library version met by a rewrite rather than by linking that version, is this paragraph wrong.

**105. Revision 105 (2026-10-09).** §4.3 states what an `Invocation` carries over the recipe as a rule, and its fields as a region rendered from the type, settled by Mitchell on #1305 (2026-09-30) and built by #1319. #1186 replaced two of the fields the sentence named, what the program reads on stdin with `oracle::Stdin` and whether the second stream is the first with `oracle::Streams`, and #952 added the working directory, `cwd`, which with `raw` neither the sentence nor the draft put to a person on #1305 named: the enumeration had drifted three times, each fix a person's revision of a fact the type's rustdoc already states. The sentence keeps the two groups, what a `.phpt` section adds and what no section gives, and the fields are the region `invocation` below the paragraph, `xtask status --section invocation`, which reads `oracle::Invocation` from its source: each field past the binary and the program with its type, its group and the first sentence of its rustdoc, the group being the rustdoc's last paragraph, one of two sentences, so a field that names neither fails the render. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: revision 23's check, a field of `Invocation` this paragraph does not name, becomes `computed check`, since the region's `inputs=` name `xtask/src/oracle/mod.rs` and a field the type gains without a re-render fails CI; `cargo test -p xtask --test map` runs `the_invocation_region_is_the_types_and_a_field_it_gains_stales_it`, which asks `computed affected` for the region and runs `computed check` over a copy of the design, fresh, then failing once a copy of the type gains a field, and `status::invocation`'s tests fail a field whose rustdoc names no group.

**106. Revision 106 (2026-10-09).** §8 says a fence's streams may be captured as its status is, settled by Mitchell on 2026-09-24 in the coordinating session ("let's do it", on the proposal from #783's builder) and built by #801, the sentence word for word: "A fence's streams may be captured as its status is: `xtask witness --accept --streams` writes the oracle's stdout and stderr, after the recipe's normalisation, into each fence that asks for it, and leaves every other fence as its author wrote it; a value that differs between machines is a wildcard the author writes, and the capture refuses to write one it can see differs." #783 held the oracle's run to each fence and found 14 fences whose text php does not print, typed by hand and wrong or bound to one machine; a fence written from the oracle's run cannot be typed wrong. A fence asks with the word `capture` on its `expect` line, not with an empty `expect` fence, which is what a snippet that prints nothing pins; the word stays once the fence is written, so a reader knows the text is the oracle's and the next capture, after an oracle bump, writes it again. The capture runs the oracle a second time from a scratch tree whose path differs from the first's in length as well as name (`oracle::Place::Apart`), so a number the path moves differs too, and writes nothing for a fence whose two runs differ in a line or the status, or whose text the fence grammar would not read back as written, naming the line. Whether a rule should deny a hand-written body under `capture` is proposed in the commit, not written into the manifest. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test witness` runs `accept_streams_writes_the_marked_fences_alone`, which fails if a fence not marked moves or a marked one is not the oracle's streams, and `xtask/fixtures/witness/capture-moves`, a fence printing `string(N)` over its own path, fails the crate with nothing written.

**107. Revision 107 (2026-10-09).** §4.2's list of the instruments that write a reading names `miri`, built by #1916. §7's Miri row is design §9's "Miri on `php_heap`'s test suite per commit", and no instrument or workflow ran it. `xtask miri` runs `cargo miri test` over `php_heap`'s library and integration tests on the nightly `xtask::miri::TOOLCHAIN` names. A test that ends the binary in undefined behaviour or an unsupported operation is skipped and the run goes again, so one such test hides none after it. The instrument writes one reading per crate, with `ran`, `failed` and `ignored` among its numbers, and its message names the aliasing model and provenance it ran under. CI's `miri` job runs it per commit on `dax-build`. Which model and provenance the lane runs is a person's (#2415), and so is whether `done` reads the reading (#2416); §4.5 is unchanged. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test miri` fails `xtask/fixtures/miri/use-after-free`, a read after a free, as undefined behaviour, with the test beside it still run, and fails a workflow that installs a nightly other than the constant's.

**108. Revision 108 (2026-10-09).** §4.4's Interface says which of `permit`'s two refusals is a skip and which a failure, settled by Mitchell on #125 (2026-09-28) and landed by #1930's branch, the sentence word for word: "A build whose digest is not the tree's writes a `skip` under `permit.stale-build` naming the candidate it hands the call to, since that candidate judges it; a call no candidate can judge writes a `fail` under `permit.unjudged-call`." #118 had changed what `permit` records when a build refuses to judge, and the Interface still said only that every refusal is a reading with the rule id: a stale build hands the call on to a candidate that judges it, a walk, and a call no candidate judges is an outage. Revision 13's entry, which names "the two rules `permit` judges by that are its own" where there are three now, `computed.region-body`, `permit.stale-build` and `permit.unjudged-call`, keeps its words, the ledger being a record (revision 22). The settlement's second half, §4.6's `permits` section naming the three and the `handed on` column beside `denials`, does not land: revision 77 removed §4.6's list of sections after it was settled, so there is no `permits` sentence to carry it, and where it goes is a person's (#2420). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `xtask status --section permits` renders `permit.stale-build` with a `skip` reading and its count under `handed on`, and `permit.unjudged-call` with a `fail` reading and its count under `denials`.

**109. Revision 109 (2026-10-09).** §4.1's grammar names `where` and `unless_pattern`, settled by Mitchell on #1154 (2026-09-28) and landed by #1930's branch, the sentence word for word before "That is the whole grammar": "A rule `permit` judges at the call may carry `where = "ticketed"`, which limits it to a checkout whose branch records a ticket, the call's own or the one a leading `cd` names; a `deny_pattern` may carry `unless_pattern`, which lets a hit through when the command around it, the text between the shell separators on either side, also matches." #1149 added both keys to the manifest's typed rule, `manifest::Where` and `Predicates::unless_pattern`, which `permit` judges, and the design of record named neither. Revision 90 since made the paragraph cite the predicate and qualifier lists rather than restate them, keeping words only for what a name does not say; what `where` limits and what text `unless_pattern` is matched against are of that kind, so the sentence stands beside `unless`'s. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'where = "ticketed"' seams.toml` finds the row it describes, and `permit::rules::runs_in` and `command_around` are the leading `cd` and the text between separators the sentence names.

**110. Revision 110 (2026-10-09).** §8 says how a host test that holds the oracle's bytes is held, settled by Mitchell on #2468 (2026-10-09, option C) and built by #1975. Host tests under `sapi/*/tests` carried the oracle's bytes as typed literals that no instrument captured or checked, so an oracle bump left each stale for a hand to retype. The ruling makes each such test data that an xtask instrument runs against the engine's host and the oracle's host, asserting the md5 first, and gives the format and the instrument's name to the builder: a host crate's `tests/lines.toml`, judged by `witness` among the crate's fences. The pin stays xtask's alone (§4.3) and `sapi/*` gains no edge, since a dev-dependency on xtask would enter every host crate's reading key (ADR-0011). Check: wrong if a file under `sapi/*/tests` carries a typed copy of the oracle's bytes, or if a line whose pinned host is absent or drifted reads as anything but a failure (`xtask/fixtures/witness/host-line-unmet`).

**111. Revision 111 (2026-10-09).** §4.3 and §8 say a `trace:` row may cover the fences that pin the engine's side of it, settled by Mitchell on #1650 (2026-10-03) and built by #1728, the sentence word for word: "A `trace:` row's `covers` may name `witness:` ids. A covered fence is held to its pinned `expect` against the engine alone: the oracle still runs and its signal, status or bytes are recorded in the reading, but they are not judged. This is how a witness pins the engine's side of a row where the oracle crashes or prints bytes php does not define." Before it, `Id::of` held every id in a row's `covers` to the row's kind, so a fence whose oracle ends on a signal was a bork whatever the registry said, and a `witness:` row of its own would have accepted the engine's side as a difference rather than pinned it; #1514's and #1556's fences had nothing to stand on. A `trace:` row's `covers` now admits a `witness:` id; `Registry::pinning` answers the accepted `trace:` row covering such an id and `Registry::accepted` answers none, so `oracle::verdict` reads the engine's miss as `Differs`. The judge serves a pinned fence by the engine alone and its verdict is the variant `covered`, whose note is the oracle's status or signal and each stream's length and blob id; the crate's reading counts it under `covered` and carries the note in its message. What the ruling leaves to the code, and the code decides: `--accept` captures a bare `exit` from the engine's run, the one the fence is held to, since an oracle that ends on a signal has no status to give, and a `capture` on a pinned fence writes nothing, since what a capture writes is the oracle's streams. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test witness` runs `a_fence_a_trace_row_covers_records_the_oracle_and_the_one_it_does_not_fails_the_crate` over `xtask/fixtures/witness/oracle-signal`, whose two fences end the oracle on `SIGKILL`: the one the fixture's row covers is `covered` with `signal 9` in the reading's message, and the one nothing covers borks and fails the crate; without the row both bork (`witness::tests::a_fence_a_trace_row_covers_is_held_to_the_engine_alone_and_one_it_does_not_borks`).

**112. Revision 112 (2026-10-09).** §4.5 condition 1 and §7's **What the per-commit lane measures** say a crate whose row names an oracle variant is in the per-commit phpt lane on the engine built with its feature, settled by Mitchell on #2421 (2026-10-09: the drafted wording accepted as written) and built by #2029, the two sentences word for word as drafted there. #2029 has the per-commit lane run a crate whose `[[crate]]` row names an oracle variant on the engine the harness builds with the row's feature, in a target directory of its own (`oracle::profile::featured`, `target/featured/<feature>`), where the variant's php is on the machine, and has `done` ask such a crate for its `phpt` reading as it asks a carried one (`done::measured_by_the_lane`: a crate that owns a subset and that the binary carries or whose row names a variant); condition 1 still said a `feature =` crate's corpus was the nightly lane's and §7 that a `feature` row the binary does not turn on stays out of the lane. The two sentences now carry the exception, and the code is unchanged. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `done::measured_by_the_lane` reads `carried.carries(krate) || krate.oracle.is_some()` beside a subset, and `phpt::featured` builds into `profile::featured(root, feature)` only for a row with a variant whose php is here; a sentence that names neither the variant nor the target directory of its own is this revision undone.

**113. Revision 113 (2026-10-09).** D.19 says how the package wildcard expands now that #1910 has landed, settled by Mitchell on #2406 (2026-10-09: the drafted wording accepted as written), the sentence word for word: "The expansion walks the package's public modules from its `lib.rs` with syn, inline and file modules alike, and names each type at the path a public module makes it reachable at; a `pub use` glob or a `pub mod` at a `#[path]` is refused naming where, and a type behind a `#[cfg]` is rendered `allow-invalid`, denied where the build has it (#1910). Unverified that the expansion is complete for a type a macro defines, or for a crate that re-exports a lock under another name; the `permit` regex and the review of `api.lock` are the twins." It replaces what revision 96 had D.19 say of the gap #1888 measured, that the generator read a package's `lib.rs` line by line, reached its root items only and rendered `once_cell::*` to paths that do not exist, which denied nothing at clippy until #1910 landed; revision 96's entry keeps its words, the ledger being a record (revision 22). #1910 (bd07a12d) replaced that reading with `lock::public_types`, which parses the package with syn and walks each `pub mod`, inline by its module path and a file one as rustc finds it, names each `pub` struct, enum, union and type alias and each CamelCase name a `pub use` brings in at the path of the module holding it, refuses a `pub use` glob and a `pub mod` at a `#[path]` naming where, and marks a type gated where a `#[cfg]` stands on it or on a module above it, which `generated` renders with `allow-invalid = true`. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: the generated `ext/clippy.toml` names `once_cell::unsync::{Lazy, OnceCell}` without `allow-invalid` and `once_cell::sync::{Lazy, OnceCell}` and `once_cell::race::{OnceBool, OnceBox, OnceNonZeroUsize, OnceRef}` with it, and `cargo xtask budget` reads `ext/clippy.toml matches its rendering from rule ext.no-locks`.

**114. Revision 114 (2026-10-09).** §4.1's grammar sentence for `generated` reads "`generated` the files the manifest renders, each from every rule whose scope reaches its directory (`generated::reaches`)". It replaces "`generated` a file and the rule it is rendered from". Mitchell settled it on #2474 (2026-10-09), and #2473's branch lands it beside the code. Since #1950 the generator has rendered into each `clippy.toml` every rule whose scope reaches its directory, so the rule the old map named for a file was one of several and decided nothing. #2473 made the predicate a list of files (`manifest::Generated`), with each file's rules read off the scopes alone. The sentence cites the function and counts nothing, as revision 90 asks of that paragraph. The `seams.toml` row moves to the list form when Mitchell approves its diff on #2474; until then the map decodes to its files. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'fn reaches' xtask/src/generated.rs` finds the function the sentence names, and `a_rule_added_under_ext_lands_in_ext_clippy_toml_without_naming_the_file` passes.

**115. Revision 115 (2026-10-10).** §5's A track's plan says `xtask track next` passes over a ticket while an open ticket blocks it, and for nothing else: "those no open ticket blocks" replaces "those no ticket blocks while it is open or while the branch that records it is not yet in `main`". Mitchell ruled on #2270 that a closed ticket means its work is on `main` and that there is no *held* state (revision 98), and #2326 lands it. Since #2311 a ticket closes at the merge that puts its work on `main`, so a blocker whose branch waits on that merge is still open and blocks, and the branch test #1850 added (revision 75) could only fire for a ticket closed on its branch before #2311 landed. On 2026-10-10 no open ticket had a closed blocker recorded by a branch `main` does not contain: 189 closed blocking edges, two unmerged branches, no match. The mechanism therefore guarded nothing and is gone with its tests: the unmerged-branch records (`Records::unmerged`), the frontier's held state (`Frontier::Held`, `Worked::Held`, revision 78's `--fill` ending, #1857) and the `Wait` that named a branch. What blocks is now one function, `BlockedBy::open`, which the frontier, `track chains` and a merge's partial landing (#2394) all read. CONTEXT.md's **Frontier** already said so. Nothing is reversed that revision 98 did not already reverse, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -rn 'unmerged\|Frontier::Held' xtask/src/track*.rs` finds nothing, and `only_an_open_blocker_blocks` and `a_run_that_readies_a_blocker_does_not_lay_down_its_dependent` pass.

**116. Revision 116 (2026-10-10).** §12 item 16 and §14 item 3 count the rows whose cause is `debt` against the day-30 target, and every other cause on its own line, settled by Mitchell on #2429 (2026-10-09, restated for #2460's field). #2460 gave a divergence row a `cause` beside its `kind`, and #2462 built it at 57f46b7b. The `cause` is `debt` by default, or `php-ub`, `by-design` or `oracle-build` (`Cause::ALL`, `xtask/src/divergence.rs:313-318`). `kind` now says only which lane reads a row, and `status --section divergence` writes the total from `debt` rows alone against `seams.toml`'s `[divergence] target` and a line for each other cause after it. Item 16 said the registry counts every kind against one target, and item 3 said the target counts all kinds. Design revision 124 carries §8's sentence and CONTEXT.md's **Divergence** names the cause. §4.3's sentence is not here. The restated words have an `oracle-build` row's reason name what it retires at, but Mitchell ruled later the same day on #2462 that the retirement is a structured field, `retires`. `divergence-reason` reads that field for it and never the reason (`divergence.rs:569`, `:582`), so #2535 asks a person for §4.3's words, and §4.3 still says the target counts every kind until it lands. Rejected, by #2460's ruling: a kind per cause, lanes times causes. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'counts all kinds\|counts every kind against' harness-design.md` matches ledger rows alone; `cargo xtask status --section divergence` prints the `debt` total and then a line for each other cause.

**117. Revision 117 (2026-10-10).** §4.1, §6 and §3 say what the generated clippy configuration and the re-entry rule are since #1950 and #2464, settled by Mitchell on #2465 (2026-10-09, a session), the sentences word for word. Since branch `t1917` (644fe4ab, c71f8c03), a `clippy.toml` is rendered at each directory that a `deny_types` or `deny_methods` rule's scope names. Each file carries every such rule whose scope reaches it: `crates/clippy.toml` and `sapi/clippy.toml` hold `fork.threads`, and `ext/clippy.toml` holds `ext.no-locks` and `fork.threads`. Yet §4.1's derived bullet still said that `ext/clippy.toml` was generated from `ext.no-locks` alone and covered nothing under `crates/`. It now says each such rule renders into the file at each directory its scope names, and it names no file list, so a fourth directory does not stale it. §4.1's readers name the generator of each `clippy.toml`, and its deletion test names the generated clippy.toml files. §6 gains a row for `fork.threads`, the fork home and the engine's counted threads, and its generated-config row names each generated clippy.toml. #2464's third diff, approved by Mitchell and landed at e27db22f, deleted `engine.reentry-signature`. Measured on #2464 at a1299fa1, over php_engine and php_streams, 298 of the 300 functions that call `execute`, `reenter` or a `release` take `&mut Request`, and the other two hold one or make the request they serve. So §6's re-entry row cites the type and design principle 9, not a lint, with compile as its cadence. §3's row is now **Re-entry in signatures**, and its last column says this revision retires the lint. §4.1's excerpt shows `lints.no-panic` at its scope since e27db22f, and the `engine.reentry-signature` row is struck. Rejected, by the ruling: listing the three files in the prose, §6 keeping "the parameter" (it claims more than the types enforce, e.g. `api::syntax::InRequest`), and reversing the deletion. Design revision 125 carries principle 9 and strikes §10's lint bullet, and #2475 asks what stops a crate-level clippy.toml from hiding the generated rules. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'reentry-signature\|Re-entry lint' harness-design.md` matches ledger rows alone, and `cargo xtask budget` reads each of `crates/clippy.toml`, `ext/clippy.toml` and `sapi/clippy.toml` as matching its rendering.

**118. Revision 118 (2026-10-10).** §4.1's excerpt of `seams.toml` takes its `[[rule]]` rows from the file. Mitchell ruled on #2465 (2026-10-09, a session) that those rows become a computed region rendered from the manifest by id, and #2472 builds it. The excerpt had been typed by hand and had drifted: after #2464 it still showed `lints.no-panic`'s old scope and the deleted `engine.reentry-signature`, which revision 117 struck by hand. The region `manifest-rules` runs `scripts/manifest-rules.sh` over the ids the excerpt showed, less the deleted one: `ext.no-locks`, `unsafe.homes`, `lints.no-panic`, `witness.exit-authored`, `git.no-verify` and `harness.self`. It prints each row verbatim, from its `[[rule]]` line to the blank line that ends it, comments included, so the excerpt now shows what the file carries: `ext.no-locks`' once types, `Rc` and `deny_macros`, and `harness.self`'s function-hook modules. An id the manifest carries no row for fails the render, naming the id, so a deleted rule cannot drop out unseen. The renderer is a script beside `scripts/adr-index.sh`, the other region that prints a file's own text, and adds no line to `xtask`. The non-rule tables (`[oracle]`, `[budget]`, `[[crate]]`, `[[seam]]`, `[[edge]]`) stay hand-typed above it, since their comments explain keys the file states bare and a rendering would lose that. That was the builder's call, which the ticket left open. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test map` runs `the_rule_excerpt_fails_naming_an_id_the_manifest_has_no_row_for`, whose manifest lacks `c.deleted` and has `a.first`, and `the_rule_excerpt_prints_each_named_row_verbatim_in_the_order_named`. `computed check harness-design.md` holds the region to `seams.toml`.

**119. Revision 119 (2026-10-10).** §4.5's list of conditions is a region rendered from `done`'s own conditions. Mitchell ruled it on #2309 and #2365 (2026-10-09, a session): the list becomes one rustdoc line per condition, read from the code, the kept-change backstop and the stranded merge become numbered conditions in it, and the deletion test's "seven sentences" becomes the count the region renders. The list had seven conditions by hand, while `done::judge` read two more. One was the backstop #1972 built for #1931: a change `permit --after` kept is unmet until a person commits or restores it (§4.4's sentence since revision 97). The other was the stranded merge #2358 built: a ticket a merge names and `xtask ticket merged` left open for want of a ready record, which #2394 tells apart from a partial landing. Neither was in the list. `done::Condition` now has one variant per condition, and `judge` walks `Condition::ALL` with an arm for each. `xtask status --section conditions` renders each variant's first rustdoc paragraph, numbered in declaration order, and a variant without one fails the render, naming it. The rest of a variant's rustdoc is the code's account and stays out. Conditions 1 to 7 keep their numbers. The eighth is #2309's drafted sentence, word for word. The ninth says the stranded merge as `done.rs`'s rustdoc and #2394's builder do: an `unready` refusal is named, and a partial landing, a `skip` counting `partial`, is not. Condition 1's sentence keeps revision 112's meaning, and its crosscheck clause says "an extension the binary carries", which is what `instruments_of` asks. Where an item had more than a sentence, the rest stays as prose under the list (**Condition 1 at length**, **2**, **7**), its words unchanged. The deletion test says "as many sentences as the list above renders". `done.rs`'s module rustdoc stops restating the list and points at the enum. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask status --section conditions` prints nine items, the eighth naming #1931 and the ninth #2358 and #2394, and `conditions::tests::a_condition_without_rustdoc_fails_naming_it` passes.

**120. Revision 120 (2026-10-10).** §4.6, §5, §6 and §10 say how tracked regions are rendered and held, the state #630, #631 and #2236 left (branch `t630`). Mitchell ruled on #2377 (2026-10-09, a session): the drafted wording is accepted where a draft exists, an agent words the rest, and a tracked status region drops its `reading` column, since there it can only say `no reading`. The live readings stay in the volatile `.claude/harness.local.md`. The code landed under revision 6's sentence with no revision of its own, so these lines lagged it. §5's region table gave the `seams` and `rules` commands without `--tracked`, with inputs that stopped short of `xtask/xtask.lock` and `xtask/src/status.rs`, and it had no row for `crates` or `layout-sapi`. §5's `bundle-order` command had no `--tracked`. §4.6's synopsis had no `--tracked`, and it called `locks` "the `api-check` readings' section", where its `items` are what the tree's lock lists. §6's account of `xtask regions` had no `--staged` refusal (#2236). §10 rule 4 named four region files where pre-commit and CI's `computed check` name six, `harness-design.md` and `third-engine-design.md` among them. §5's "Design documents get no regions" was untrue of both. Each line now says what the code does. `status::Against::omitted` leaves `reading` out of a table rendered against the tree, and the tracked regions of `AGENTS.md`, `ext/AGENTS.md` and `third-engine-design.md` are re-rendered without it. `bundle-order`'s `extensions` and `message` cells, which are empty against the tree, keep their columns, since the ruling names `reading` alone. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test status` passes `tracked_renders_no_reading_whatever_the_store_holds`, whose tracked `rules`, `locks` and `bundle` tables have no `reading` column, and `grep -n '| reading |' AGENTS.md ext/AGENTS.md third-engine-design.md` finds nothing.

**121. Revision 121 (2026-10-10).** The second half of #125's settlement lands where revision 77 sends a section's meaning, in `cli::Section::Permits`'s rustdoc, which `xtask status --help` renders, and not in §4.6. Mitchell chose option 2 on #2420 (2026-10-09, a session). The settlement (2026-09-28) put two facts in §4.6's `permits` sentence. One is the three rules `permit` owns, `computed.region-body`, `permit.stale-build` and `permit.unjudged-call`. The other is the `handed on` column beside `denials`. Revision 77 (#1854) had removed §4.6's list of sections, so revision 108 landed only the §4.4 half and left this one to a person. The rustdoc now names the three as the rows that follow the manifest's, which no manifest row names, and `handed on` as the calls a stale build handed to the candidate that rebuilds, never counted as a refusal (#118). #125's second and third boxes are reworded to say where the two facts are, as ruled. §4.6 is unchanged, and revisions 13's and 108's entries keep their words, the ledger being a record (revision 22). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask status --help` prints the `permits` line naming `computed.region-body`, `permit.stale-build`, `permit.unjudged-call` and `handed on`, and `status::permit_rows` renders permit's three rows after the manifest's, with `handed_on` beside `denials`.

**122. Revision 122 (2026-10-10).** §4.6 names what t1319 (#1157, #1319) gave `xtask status`, the sentence drafted on #2404, which Mitchell accepted (2026-10-09, a session). Since t1319, `--session` appends `## tickets` after the local facts: the week's closes, withdrawals, successors opened and opened per close. `status --section tickets` reads the `ticket` log of both the main worktree's store and the checkout's, since a merge writes a close into the main worktree's store (#2311) and a withdrawal stands in the checkout's until `worktree retire`. `status --section invocation` reads `oracle::Invocation`'s source for §4.3's region (#1305). §4.6 named none of the three. **123. Revision 123 (2026-10-10).** §4.2's **Store** says what CI uploads, the sentence drafted on #2363, which Mitchell accepted (2026-10-09, a session): "CI uploads the store: each per-commit job its own, joined for `done` and kept as the run's `harness` artefact, and the dashboard's jobs the log (#2253)." **124. Revision 124 (2026-10-10).** §4.3's reason for keeping `-l` on the invocation and out of the pin's `[regime.dump]` is worded for a regime that carries arguments as well as ini. Each now says the copy and the type-check. **126. Revision 126 (2026-10-10).** §6 and §4.4 name `witness.capture-authored` beside its twin `witness.exit-authored`, and §4.4 says what a `deny_pattern` over a write is asked of. #2413 was ruled by Mitchell (2026-10-09, accepted as proposed), and #1728's branch landed the rule (c01beb4e). It is a `deny_pattern` over `**/*.rs` on a block, from an `expect … capture` fence line to its close plus the `stderr` fence after it, with `unless = "xtask witness --accept"`. e7b94f35 made such a pattern read the file the write leaves. The ledger numbers on t1728 were reserved for that bundle's own tickets, so its prose did not land there. #2508 asks for it under the delegation Mitchell ruled on #2439 (2026-10-09, a session), which makes it an agent's to word. The delegation's own sentence waits on a person's hand (#2543). §6 gains a row for streams in a fence marked `capture`, whose only writer is `witness --accept --streams` (revision 106) and whose twin is `permit` at the call and over the staged diff. §4.4's list of the rows `permit` evaluates names the rule after `witness.exit-authored`, in manifest order. Before that change, an edit that finished a hit outside its own text went through, and a block pattern could not fire on an edit inside the block. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'witness.capture-authored' seams.toml` finds the row §6 names, and `cargo test -p xtask --test permit` runs `an_edit_that_finishes_a_hit_outside_its_own_text_is_denied`.

**127. Revision 127 (2026-10-10).** §4.5 condition 1 says `done` reads the Miri lane's reading, settled by Mitchell on #2416 (2026-10-09, through AskUserQuestion: "yes, once #2414 is fixed"), the sentence word for word as ruled there: "For a change whose dependency closure includes `php_heap`, `done` reads a fresh `miri` reading of `php_heap` and holds `undefined` to zero (`unsupported` is reported, not held, under the permissive provenance #2415 ruled)." Revision 107 built `xtask miri` and left §4.5 unchanged, the reading a person's to make a condition. #2414 is fixed, and #2417 moved the lane to a nightly of the workspace's own Rust under Tree Borrows, where `php_heap` reads 96 passed, 0 undefined, 0 unsupported. The sentence is the first paragraph of `done::Condition::Fresh`, which §4.5's conditions region renders (revision 119's region, #2309), so it reaches the text from the code. `done::FRESH` gains `miri`, and `done::instruments_of` asks it of the crates `miri::CRATES` names, `php_heap`, when a change touches it. `php_heap` depends on no workspace crate, so a change that moves its key is a change to it. `done::miri_unsupported` meets a fail whose every failed test ended in an unsupported operation and holds everything else: undefined behaviour, a test libtest failed, a fail with nothing failed (the tests did not build, or Miri spoke after the last of them) and a skip. **128. Revision 128 (2026-10-10).** §4.5's **Callers** says what CI's `done` passes while the oracle job is skipped. Mitchell ruled on #2477 (2026-10-09, a session): until `dax-build` is online, the Linux jobs run on a GitHub-hosted image through one repository variable, `DAX_BUILD_RUNNER`, and a job that reads the oracle's readings neither fails red for a missing oracle job nor passes as if its lane had run. While the variable is set, both `oracle` jobs are skipped, since a job queued for an absent runner waits GitHub's 24 hours and holds every job that needs it. `done` then takes `--not-run witness --not-run crosscheck`. A missing reading of either is a note, the head line reads "every condition of done is met over what ran; witness and crosscheck did not run in this run", and the reading counts `not_run`. **129. **130. Revision 130 (2026-10-10).** §4.4's after-half holds one checkout, the one the call ran in, and never puts back a file an operation in progress there has staged, as #2597 built. On 2026-10-10 a builder's `Bash` call in its worktree, `t2426`, ran through `permit --after` while the coordinator's merge of t2463 stood in the main checkout with Mitchell's approved `docs/divergence.toml` staged. A subagent's shell starts in the main checkout and `cd`s into its worktree, and the record held the hook's own checkout beside the one the call stood in, read from the `cwd` alone, so both were the main one. The line named the registry by a relative path, read there, so the main checkout's working copy was put back to blob 4e35c03c under the merge, and three of Mitchell's approved merges (t2463, t2474, t2512) aborted. Revision 101's sentence, "the checkouts held are the hook's own and the one the call stands in", is narrowed: the checkout is the call's `cwd` moved by a leading `cd`, at its worktree's top, the resolution `where = "operating"` reads (#2476), so a call in one checkout never compares or restores another's files. And a harness file whose index entry a merge, cherry-pick, revert or rebase in progress has moved off `HEAD`'s is kept and named, never put back, since the operation's commit carries it. A person's approved change rides a merge that way. Every other rule of the after-half stands. The sentence is an agent's to word under the delegation Mitchell ruled on #2439 (2026-10-09, a session), whose own sentence waits on #2543. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test permit_after` runs `a_call_in_a_worktree_leaves_the_main_checkout_s_merge_as_it_stands`, `a_harness_file_a_merge_staged_is_not_put_back` and `a_call_that_writes_its_own_worktree_s_pin_is_put_back_there`.

**131. Revision 131 (2026-10-10).** §11's *Human-only* paragraph gains #2439's delegation sentence, ruled by Mitchell on 2026-10-09 (a session) and settled word for word on #2543: a revision that states what a ruled ticket built, and reverses, widens or adds to no ruling, is an agent's to word and land, with its ledger row naming the ruling and the ticket. The builder of #2439 tried the edit on 2026-10-10 and Claude Code's auto-mode classifier refused it as an agent writing its own permission, so Mitchell authorized it in a session (#2543), and the agent typed it on his instruction. Revision 130 already worded a sentence under this delegation before the delegation was written down; this revision closes that gap.
