**132. Revision 132 (2026-10-10).** §4.4's after-half reads where a call ran past three more shapes of a line, as #2607 built on #2597's resolution. Revision 130 held the checkout the call's `cwd` moved by a leading `cd` names, and three shapes a builder writes still left the call held in its workspace, which for a subagent is the main checkout: `git -C <worktree> checkout -- seams.toml` with no `cd`, which writes the worktree's file while `--after` compared the main checkout's, and put back a person's edit there where the line's words named the file relatively; `W=<worktree> && cd "$W"`, whose operand was read as the directory `$W`; and `pushd <worktree>`. The sentence names them: the line's leading `cd`, `pushd` and `popd`, a variable the line assigned before them read as its value, and git's `-C` where every command past them is git and names one directory. A `-C` moves one command and not the shell, so a line any of whose other commands runs where the shell stands (`git -C /wt status && git checkout -- seams.toml`) is held where the shell stands, which is where that write lands. The reading is one function, `permit::rules::standing`, from which `runs_in`, `command_runs_in`, `fired_in` and `acts_in` are read, so `where = "ticketed"`, `where = "operating"`, the checkout `--after` holds and the approvals it reads move together, and a scoped rule judges a git command where its own `-C` names. A change the line does not say where it goes is not followed, and the line stands where it stood before it, the workspace for the first: a variable the line did not assign, `cd -`, a `pushd` with an option or none, a `popd` with an option or no `pushd` before it. §4.1's `where` clause, Mitchell's words of revision 109, keeps them: "the one a leading `cd` names" is read by the same function. The sentence is an agent's to word under the delegation Mitchell ruled on #2439 (2026-10-09, a session), which §11 carries since revision 131 (#2543). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test permit_after` runs `a_call_by_git_c_into_its_worktree_leaves_the_main_checkout_alone`, `a_call_by_a_cd_to_a_variable_it_assigned_leaves_the_main_checkout_alone` and `a_call_by_pushd_into_its_worktree_leaves_the_main_checkout_alone`, and `permit::operation::tests` runs `a_call_ran_in_the_worktree_an_assigned_cd_a_pushd_or_git_s_c_names`.

**133. Revision 133 (2026-10-11).** The registry excerpt's kind comment gains `makala`, the eighth of `Kind::ALL`, as #2629 built it and revision 129 gave the comment `wire`. Mitchell ruled on #2611 (2026-10-10, a session) that the php-fpm comparison gets a kind of its own, consulted as the witness lane consults `witness:` rows, and on #2639 that it is named `makala`, ids `makala:<test path>`, one row per by-design fact of the production server read by `fpm-compare` and the served phpt lane alike. Both read it through one lookup, `Registry::makala`, and one rule, `makala::held`: a differing test an accepted row's id or `covers` names is accepted, counted apart and named; `fpm-compare` also names a row whose test now agrees stale, which the served lane, holding a test to its own expectation, cannot tell. Rejected, by the rulings: reusing `phpt:` rows, which would excuse the CLI and CGI lanes too, a kind per comparison, which writes each fact twice, and masking the differences inside the instrument. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test divergence` runs `a_makala_row_is_keyed_by_its_test_and_an_unknown_kind_is_refused`, and `makala::compare::tests` runs `a_difference_no_makala_row_names_fails_and_one_a_row_names_is_accepted` over `xtask/fixtures/makala`.

**134. Revision 134 (2026-10-11).** §4.1's grammar names `where = "operating"`, settled by Mitchell on #2530 (2026-10-10, a session) and landed by #2617, the clause word for word after the `where = "ticketed"` one: "or `where = "operating"`, which limits it to a checkout where a merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase or am stands that the calling session did not start, the starter being the session whose starting command `permit` admitted (`permit::operation`, #2476)". #2476 built the judgement, `manifest::Where::Operating` judged by `permit::operation::foreign` and the starter recorded by `operation::record` when `permit` admits the start, inert while no row carried it; on 2026-10-09 a settled divergence row staged by pathspec in main's checkout rode into `1bd555d0`, an unrelated merge, because a merge in progress commits the whole index. The same ruling settled the row, `git.operation` after `git.no-amend`, with the argv widened past the drafted `add`, `stage`, `commit`, `rm` and `mv` to `restore`, `reset`, `apply` and the operations' own subcommands, so another hand's `--continue`, `--abort` or `--skip` is refused and the starter's is not; it reached `seams.toml` by `steer` as an addition. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'where = "operating"' seams.toml` finds the row the clause describes, `cargo test -p xtask --test permit` runs `the_git_operation_rule_refuses_staging_where_another_hand_s_merge_stands` and the fixture walks over `xtask/fixtures/rules/git.operation/`, and `permit::adapters::tests` runs `the_git_operation_row_lets_the_starter_conclude_its_own_merge`.

**135. Revision 135 (2026-10-11).** §4.2's instrument list and §7's sensor table name `fpm-compare` and `soak`, as #2612 asked once #2349 and #2350 built them. #2336's ruling (Mitchell, 2026-10-09, a session) set the soak's lane: nightly on dax-build, ten minutes, 64 concurrent clients, over the HTTP-served subset and the bench scripts, against a debug build, plus a 30-second smoke in makala's own tests per commit; #2632 then made a bare `xtask soak` the smoke's 30 seconds and `--nightly` the nightly length, which `lanes.nightly-soak` keeps in CI. The §7 row states what that built: the per-commit smoke is `sapi/makala/tests/soak.rs` in makala's `test` reading, the bare call runs the same length on request, and `dashboard.yml`'s `soak` job runs `--nightly` against `release-da`, the lanes' profile with debug assertions, which arm the request leak sensor. `fpm-compare` has no lane: it runs on request where the pinned php-fpm and php-src are, and measures nothing elsewhere, stated as a skip. Both write one reading at makala's crate. `CONTEXT.md`'s **Instrument** entry lists both in `cli::Command`'s order. **136. Revision 136 (2026-10-12).** §4.5 condition 1 holds a touched crate's phpt subset to no regression against the merge base, and §4.2's **Store** keeps the phpt runs, as Mitchell settled on #725 (2026-09-28, session dax-php-de): "the draft on this ticket, as written, with the runs moved into the store". The draft's three sentences are the `phpt` sentence of `done::Condition::Fresh`'s first paragraph word for word, which §4.5's conditions region renders (revision 119's region, #2309), with the sentence naming which subsets the per-commit lane runs kept after them (revision 63, #2029). `done` diffs the run kept at the merge base's engine tree with the one kept at this tree's itself, `phpt::against::regressed` over the same `against::diff` that `phpt --against` reads, a skip here no longer meeting a test met at the base, and never reads the `@against` reading. The runs move from `target/phpt-runs` to `target/harness/phpt-runs`, so CI's upload of the store carries them and a run CI took can serve as a base; §4.2 says so in one sentence, an agent's to word under the ruling's "§4.2's Store paragraph says so". What the draft retires, stated here and in the two paragraphs it touches: the `porting` key's `phpt` half (condition 1 at length, §7's **An extension whose port is partial**), and #319's `unread` rung, which the comparison holds without one; the missing engine binary's skip stays the one excuse, read where no run is kept here. A subset that holds no test in the pinned tree, as `ext/lexbor`'s and `ext/mysqlnd`'s derived ones do, is kept as a run of none, which holds nothing against any base, so the key's retirement leaves those crates meetable. #725's coordinator held it on 2026-10-08 until CI keeps runs (#2362, closed); while the runners (#2296) are not online, a base run is taken by `xtask phpt --base`, or found where the main worktree's lanes kept one. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `xtask/tests/done.rs`'s `a_subset_is_held_to_no_regression_against_the_runs_kept_at_the_base` fails on a `done` that reads the `phpt` reading, or that lets a test met at the base skip here; `grep -n 'target/phpt-runs' xtask .github` finds nothing.

**137. Revision 137 (2026-10-12).** §4.3 says the compile-identity lane puts each binary's own `PHP_BINARY` and `PHP_BUILD_DATE` out of its dump before comparing, as #2697 built it. Mitchell ruled on #2692 (2026-10-11, a session) that the eight files of the per-commit corpus differing from php's dump in those folded values alone get no registry row: the lane asks each binary for its own two values and puts a placeholder where exactly those strings appear in that binary's dump. `compile_identity::dump::Folded` asks each binary once per run with `echo PHP_BINARY, "\0", PHP_BUILD_DATE;` under the dump's recipe and regimes, never computing either value, and writes the placeholder for the value as the dump escapes it wherever it stands inside a string literal, and nowhere else in the dump; three of the eight (`php_cli_server_ipv4_error_message.phpt` and the two `ipv6` files) fold `PHP_BINARY . ' -S …'` into one literal, so a placeholder only for a literal whose whole value is the binary's own, as #2697's second point worded it, left them differing, and the ruling's words, where those strings appear, cover the part. That reading of the ruling is this revision's, and a person reads it at the merge. A binary that does not answer, such as a test's stand-in, has its dump compared as it stands, and the run says so. Rejected, by the ruling: the row `compile:build-literals`, which would accept each file's whole diff for good. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --lib compile_identity` runs `each_binarys_own_build_literals_alone_are_no_difference`, `a_difference_beside_the_build_literals_still_differs` and `a_binary_that_folds_a_value_not_its_own_still_differs`, with `the_value_is_matched_as_php_prints_it_inside_a_literal_alone` holding a value outside a literal untouched, and `cargo xtask compile-identity --file` passes each of the eight files #2692 lists.

**138. Revision 138 (2026-10-12).** §4.1's account of what `api-check` reads says it holds the `#[mirrors]` marker, the third layer of Mitchell's ruling on #2020 (2026-10-07), "a pass-through `#[mirrors("php_x.h", "sym")]` marker checked against php-src's `PHPAPI` at the pin", built by #2353. `php_macros` declares the attribute and expands it to its item unchanged, and `php_ext` re-exports it as `attr::mirrors`; `api-check` reads every marker under an `ext/*` crate's `src/` and holds it against the tree `oracle.toml`'s `[source]` pins: the header, a path from php-src's root as a dependent extension's `#include` spells it, must be a file there that declares the symbol with one of the three macros on one line. An item with no marker is not held, so the marker lands crate by crate; where the tree is not on the machine the markers are a note in the reading, or a failure where the machine promised the tree, and a tree standing at another commit fails them, as `Source::tree` refuses it. The header path from the root, where the ruling's example writes a bare `php_x.h`, is this revision's reading, since §4.10's rows mirror headers of other extensions and of `main/` as often as their own, and a person reads it at the merge. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: the fixture `xtask/fixtures/api-check/mirrors` fails on a marker naming `php_random.h`'s `static inline` `php_random_default_engine` and on one naming a header the tree lacks, and `cargo xtask api-check` passes ext/random with its five markers.

**139. Revision 139 (2026-10-12).** §4.2's instrument list names `bench` among the subcommands that write a reading, in `cli::Command`'s order after `usage`, and leaves only `fuzz` and `mutants` as named and not built, as #2669 asked. #155 built `xtask bench` on 2026-09-13 (`8727213c4`), the agent's half of §4.2's bench paragraph: one reading per bar, the measurement in `numbers`, the exit 0 whatever the reading says; the store held 62 `bench` readings on 2026-10-11, and the list still called it unbuilt. `CONTEXT.md`'s **Instrument** entry and `Reading::instrument`'s rustdoc carry the same list and say the same. **140. Revision 140 (2026-10-12).** §6's wiring takes four things out of a commit's wait, as Mitchell ruled in a session (2026-10-12) on the measurement of a session, where a hook was running for 14.8 of 18.3 hours and the 29 merges into `main` whose phases the store's readings date spent half their 300 minutes after `test`, in `witness` and `crosscheck` building the lanes' linked engine. First, pre-commit runs no `crosscheck`: its exit was never read (revision 44), so it refused nothing and rendered every carried extension again on each engine commit; `done` still asks a touched default extension for its row, which `cargo xtask crosscheck --ext <name>` takes, and the specs skill's merge check renders every extension once per batch. Second, `witness` is not started once `clippy` or `test` has refused the commit, and not on a merge commit, whose merged tree the merge check's `witness --unmeasured` measures; on a branch, `done` asks the witness of each touched crate before the branch is ready. Third, git reads the file `-F` names only after pre-commit, so the hook reads the committing git's arguments and refuses a missing file before anything runs; twice in that session a mistyped path cost sixteen minutes. Fourth, a commit in a linked worktree, a builder's, renices its hook to 10, so a builder's build yields the cores to a merge in the main checkout, and the specs skill keeps three slots, each under the `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` `.claude/settings.json` sets. Rejected: a `permit` rule on `git commit -F`, since the command that writes the message file is most often the one that commits, so the file does not exist when `permit` judges the call. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test hooks` runs `a_merge_commit_runs_no_witness`, `a_missing_message_file_is_refused_before_anything_runs` and `a_failing_instrument_refuses_the_commit_and_witness_is_not_built`, and `pre_commit_re_stages_the_region_it_rewrote_and_the_commit_is_allowed` holds the sequence with no `crosscheck` in it.

**141. Revision 141 (2026-10-12).** §4.1's grammar names `where = "main"`, settled by Mitchell on #2751 (2026-10-12, a session, through AskUserQuestion), the clause word for word after the `where = "operating"` one: "or `where = "main"`, which limits it to the repository's main checkout, the one whose `.git` is a directory, and never a linked worktree, asked of the command a `deny_pattern` hits (#2751)". #2744 measured four merges into `main` that `git merge` committed itself, so only `pre-merge-commit` ran and pre-commit's renders, tests, witness and crosscheck did not. The same ruling landed two rows, approved as one diff through #2247's path: `git.merge-commits-itself`, which refuses in the main checkout a `git merge` that would commit on its own, so `git commit` finishes it through pre-commit, and `git.no-verify` widened to `git merge`, whose `--no-verify` skips `pre-merge-commit`. A merge in a linked worktree, where a builder merges `main` into its branch, is not the rule's. A `deny_pattern` rule scoped by `where` now asks where the command its pattern hits runs, and no longer where every command of the line runs, so a `git -C <main> merge --abort` beside a merge in a worktree does not place that merge in main (`permit::rules::fires`). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'where = "main"' seams.toml` finds the row the clause describes; `cargo test -p xtask --test permit` runs `a_rule_where_main_judges_only_the_main_checkout` and `a_merge_that_commits_itself_is_refused_in_main_alone`, and the fixture walks over `xtask/fixtures/rules/git.merge-commits-itself/` and `git.no-verify/deny-merge.json`.

**142. Revision 142 (2026-10-12).** §4.4's after-half runs on the `PostToolUseFailure` hook too, as Mitchell ruled on #2762 (2026-10-12, a session, through AskUserQuestion) and approved as the exact `hooks.json` diff. Measured on #2723's build: a `Bash` call that exits non-zero gets no `PostToolUse`. Claude Code fires `PostToolUseFailure` in its place, so such a call's `permit/before` record was left behind, `operation::settle` never ran for a merge that stopped on a conflict, and a write a failing line made to a harness file was never compared. `hooks.json` runs the same `permit --after` entry on `PostToolUseFailure` with matcher `Bash`. `permit --after` answers with the event it was called on, read from the call's `hook_event_name`, since an answer naming another event is not that hook's, and on the failure event it gives what it put back as context and no `decision`, the call having already failed. The put-back itself is the same on both. The sentence states what the ruled ticket built, the delegation §11 carries since revision 131. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test permit_after` runs `a_call_that_exits_non_zero_is_settled_on_the_failure_event`, where a call that writes `oracle.toml` and exits 1 has its record spent and the file put back, and the answer names `PostToolUseFailure`.

**143. Revision 143 (2026-10-12).** §5's Worktrees paragraph says where `built_root` resolves and what `built::judges` holds the build to, settled by Mitchell on #2786 (2026-10-12, a session, through AskUserQuestion), the sentence word for word: "The harness rests on the opposite: `built_root` resolves `--root` to the workspace the binary runs in, the nearest `[workspace]` above its working directory, and `built::judges` holds the build to it, the digest of `xtask/src` compiled into the binary against that workspace's and the manifest's against the calling checkout's, so a binary cargo reused from another checkout is stale wherever their sources differ and never judges as this one's (#2719)." #2719 (t2764, 909b9438d) made `built_root` resolve the workspace the binary runs in, from its working directory up, then `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` as cargo sets it for the process, then the compiled path. The sentence it replaces said the compiled workspace, the mechanism #2719 measured failing when two checkouts share one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. The ticket's draft named one digest; the settled sentence names both that `judges` compares. Revision 40's ruling, a worktree's `target/` its own, stands. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `xtask::built_root` reads `current_dir` first, and `built::judges` takes `manifest_digest(checkout)` and `sources_digest(built_root)`.

**145. Revision 145 (2026-10-12).** A kept phpt run carries the pin's recipe digest and stops counting as this tree's run once the digest moves, as Mitchell ruled on #2718 (2026-10-12, a session): "a kept phpt run carries the pin's recipe digest, and stops counting as this tree's run once the digest moves, as `UNDER_RECIPE` readings do (#1387)". #2725 built it: `phpt::against::Run` records `oracle::Pin::digest` as `recipe`, and `done`'s no-regression comparison takes this tree's run and the base's only under the pin's digest now. A stale run here is unmet as a stale reading is, naming its subset. A stale run at the base names the `phpt --base` call that takes it again, and `worktree::carry_runs` lets the run taken again replace the stale one in main. §4.2's **Store** sentence on the runs says so. Rejected by the ruling: keying only the parts of the recipe phpt reads, and the engine's tree as the only key. The sentence states what the ruled ticket built, the delegation §11 carries since revision 131. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test done` runs `a_reading_under_the_recipe_is_stale_once_the_recipe_changes`, where the kept run is fresh before the pin moves, and after it the run here and then the base's are stale.

**149. Revision 149 (2026-10-19).** The pin's digest leaves out a build's provenance (`image`, `closure`) and nothing else, as Mitchell ruled on #2969 (2026-10-19, a session): "The whole-pin digest stands, except that each build's `image` and `closure` are left out. They are records of what a binary was built from, never an input to a run. They are left out by a declaration on the type, so a key added later is digested by default." #2974 built it: a variant build's `image` and `closure` are `oracle::Provenance`, flattened into `Build`, and `oracle::Pin::digest` is the blob id of the pin's values with the keys `Provenance` declares taken out of each build, so a reading under the recipe and a kept phpt run stay fresh across a re-recorded provenance and go stale on any other change, a variant's md5 among them. §4.2's shape and its **Store** paragraph say so. This partly reverses revision 145, whose ruling rejected keying on only the parts of the recipe phpt reads: the key is still the whole pin, and only provenance leaves it. Offered on #2969 and not taken: a key per subset over the sections that can change its verdicts, whose wider surface risks a false green where one section is misjudged, and scoping a variant's md5 to the subsets that name it, so a variant re-pin still stales every run. The digest is computed differently from this revision on, so every run kept before it is stale once. The sentence states what the ruled ticket built, the delegation §11 carries since revision 131; the ruling is the person's, and a partial reversal of a rejected option is not of an ADR's decisions, so there is none. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test done` runs `changing_a_builds_image_keeps_the_runs_under_the_recipe_fresh`, `changing_a_variant_builds_md5_stales_every_run_under_the_recipe` and `changing_the_oracles_md5_a_closure_keg_or_a_recipe_ini_stales_every_run`, and `cargo test -p xtask --lib oracle::variant` runs `adding_a_closure_to_a_build_keeps_the_pins_digest` and `an_unknown_key_in_a_build_is_refused`; a run that a re-recorded `image` or `closure` stales, or one that survives any other change to the pin, is this paragraph wrong.

Every live store has been read and split since 2026-10-07." #2973 built it: `reading::LEGACY`, `reading::MIGRATED` and `Store::migrate` go with its eight calls, and `Store::land` lands the files of the other store's `log/` alone. A `log.jsonl` a store still holds is read by no history and landed by no retire, and is left where it stands; the console's `storeLog` and the dashboard's `storesOf` no longer read one either. §4.2's **Store** paragraph loses its sentence on a store written before revision 83; ledger row 83 records the split as it was built. **153. Revision 153 (2026-10-19).** Pre-commit runs no witness, the building instruments take a seat, and `test --staged` leaves out an executable whose own paths are not staged, as Mitchell ruled on #2982 (2026-10-19, a session) after an audit of a session. Of that session's 81 builder-hours, the pre-commit hook took 18–19, as much as all model time, and 9.2–9.7 of those were `witness --staged`, which rebuilt `release-da` for every commit that touched php_engine, since `witness --accept` rewrites rustdoc; five worktrees each under `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=3` on twelve cores held the load at 32 at the median and 100 at the most, which cost 7–15 hours. Three changes follow. §6's Wiring drops witness from pre-commit: `done` already asks the witness of each crate a branch touched before it is ready, so a branch is witnessed once and not once a commit, and a merge stays the merge check's. A building instrument (`clippy`, `test`, `miri`, `phpt`, `compile-identity`, `crosscheck`, `fpm-compare`, `soak`, `witness`, `bench`) takes one of `XTASK_SEATS` machine-wide seats, three unless set, before it builds, and waits for one while all are held; what it spawns is marked as its child and takes none, so a test that runs `xtask` under the seated `test` cannot wait on its parent (`xtask/src/seat.rs`). A crate's `[package.metadata.xtask.test]` may name, under `own`, the paths that are an executable's own: under `--staged` such an executable runs only where one of them is staged, and a crate that passes with any left out writes no reading, since `done` asks the whole run; xtask names its five executables that build with cargo or serve the engine inside a test, about two of a run's two and a half minutes. Rejected: witnessing only the staged fences (`--file`, `--item`), which saves the fences' seconds and not the rebuild; incremental `release-da`, which keeps the per-commit build; and a reading of a partial run, which `done` would read as the crate's. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo test -p xtask --test hooks` holds pre-commit's calls to no `witness`, `cargo test -p xtask --test test` runs `staged_leaves_out_an_executable_whose_own_paths_are_not_staged`, and `cargo test -p xtask --lib seat` holds a seat held until it drops; a pre-commit that witnesses, or a `test --staged` that records a reading with an executable left out, is this paragraph wrong.

**154. Revision 154 (2026-10-19).** §4.1's grammar names `where = "checkout"`, and `harness.self-shell` carries it, as Mitchell ruled on #2887 (2026-10-18, a session): "narrow `harness.self-shell` to the checkouts of this repository". #2915 built it. On 2026-10-18 a builder checking a merge sequence against main's registry in a scratch repository under its scratchpad (`cd $S/realcheck && ... >> docs/divergence.toml`) was refused, because the rule matched a harness path by its spelling alone. `manifest::Where::Checkout` is judged by `permit::rules::in_a_checkout`: a line is in a checkout when where it stands, or where any of its commands runs, is at or under one of `track::checkouts`, by canonical path, so `/tmp` and `/private/tmp` are one. Every command is asked, and not only the one a pattern hits, since a redirection is in no command's argv. A relative path the line names that resolves into a checkout through a link is in one. It fails safe, as the ruling says, reading each of these as a checkout: a directory change the line does not say where it goes; a place that is no directory on disk; a change not joined to what follows it by `&&` alone (after `;`, `||`, a lone `&` or a newline, or piped), since a failed `cd` leaves the shell where it stood; a change spelled in the line that `permit` did not read as a command (`xargs sh -c 'cd …'`); a program moved by its own option (`env -C`, `find -execdir`, `patch -d`, a target directory) or by git's environment (`GIT_WORK_TREE`); a link the line makes; and a repository git cannot list. It is narrowed only: inside a checkout every shape is judged as before. The row reached `seams.toml` by `steer` as an addition. `harness.self`, `permit --after`'s put-back and `done`'s hold on a person's files are unchanged. The sentence states what the ruled ticket built, the delegation §11 carries since revision 131; nothing an ADR decided is reversed, so there is none. Check: `grep -n 'where = "checkout"' seams.toml` finds the row; `cargo test -p xtask --test permit` runs `harness_self_shell_judges_a_write_where_it_runs_and_only_in_a_checkout` and the fixture walks over `xtask/fixtures/rules/harness.self-shell/`, whose `deny-divergence.json` is the negative control #2887 names.

**155. Revision 155 (2026-10-19).** §7 says where a red nightly of dashboard.yml's `soak` or `tailcall` job is read, as #2980 asked and built: on the tracker, where dashboard.yml's `red-nightly` job files it under the rule harness.yml's `red-main` has held a red `main` to since #1156, adopted by Mitchell on #1151. Neither job's store is drawn on the site, so before this a red one was seen only by opening the workflow's runs. The rule has one home: `scripts/ci/red-main.sh` became `scripts/ci/red.sh`, whose subject is `main` or `nightly <job>`, and the two differ only in where the verdict before this run is read, the harness run of the push before, or the job's own conclusion in the newest dashboard run that ran it. The revision states what a ruled ticket built and reverses, widens or adds to no ruling, the delegation §11 carries since revision 131; nothing an ADR decided is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `actionlint .github/workflows/dashboard.yml` and `shellcheck scripts/ci/red.sh` pass, `grep -n 'red.sh nightly' .github/workflows/dashboard.yml` finds the `red-nightly` job, and `git grep -n 'red-main.sh' -- ':!data'` finds nothing outside this ledger.

**156. Revision 156 (2026-10-20).** §4.1's grammar names `unless_argv`, which #3068 built for the ruling Mitchell made on #3052 (2026-10-20, a session): `lanes.nightly-flags` lets `--jit` through on `xtask phpt` where `--subset` names one subset, and keeps `--full`, `--jit` with no subset and `xtask bench --jit` denied. One `deny_argv` over two `with` phrases could not let one element through on one phrase alone, so the qualifier maps a denied element to one `with` phrase and the option that bounds it, read by `permit::argv::Argv::denies` through `Argv::unbounded`, the reading `require_argv` has; `Predicates::orphaned_qualifier` names it, and `Manifest::parse` refuses an excuse whose element the row does not deny or whose phrase the row does not name. The row itself is Mitchell's paste on #3052, proposed in #3068's commit; until it lands the fixture walks leave `allow-excused*.json` to `the_nightly_flags_let_the_tier_s_one_subset_reading_through`. The sentence states what a ruled ticket built and reverses, widens or adds to no ruling, the delegation §11 carries since revision 131; nothing an ADR decided is reversed, so there is no ADR. **157. Revision 157 (2026-10-20).** §4.1's derived bullet on the generated clippy configuration names `allow_in_tests`, which #3104 built for the ruling Mitchell made on #3078 (2026-10-20, a session): test code is out of `lints.no-panic`'s reach through clippy's own configuration, not through attributes per module. The qualifier stands on a `deny_lints` rule, `Predicates::orphaned_qualifier` names it, and `Manifest::parse` refuses a lint the rule does not deny or one clippy has no in-tests key for (`manifest::IN_TESTS`; `unreachable`, `todo` and `unimplemented` have none). `generated` renders each listed lint as its `allow-*-in-tests = true` into every `clippy.toml` the rule's scope reaches, and a rule that renders only these keys keeps its `exempt`, since a key lifts a lint inside tests alone and an exempt path is held to none of the rule's lints for it to lift. The row reached `seams.toml` by `steer` as an addition. The sentence states what a ruled ticket built and reverses, widens or adds to no ruling, the delegation §11 carries since revision 131; nothing an ADR decided is reversed, so there is no ADR. **158. Revision 158 (2026-10-21).** §4.2's **Store** says what the engine tree a phpt run is kept under is, which #3109 and #3110 built. **175. Revision 175 (2026-10-26).** §4.4's adapter paragraph and §13 item 8 carry the sentence Mitchell ruled on #3489 (2026-10-26, a session): the module fails open on `Bash` and closed on an `Edit` or a `Write` no judge saw, as the command hook does. This reverses the module's half of §13 item 8's "every function hook fails open", a person's ruling. The reason is #3470's finding: on a Max login with no managed settings, `sec-default@builtin` does not load, and another mod's allow can approve a call a `PreToolUse` hook blocked, so a write the module let fall to the command hook could be approved over that hook's deny. Each `permit` candidate's `$.process.run` gets the command hook's 60 s, raised from 30, so a cargo rebuild is judged and not denied; the module's own 10 s budget does not count time spent waiting on `$`. Also ruled there and built with it: each denial is kept in `$.store` under `denials`, the last 50, for the desk (#3496). The `tool.check` re-assertion #3489 first asked for is dropped: per the 2.1.310 types, `tool.check` runs only after `tool.call`'s `next` and the `PreToolUse` hooks, so it never sees a call the module denied. Check: `claude plugin test` runs `harness-plugins/hooks/harness.test.ts`, whose tests "an Edit or a Write no candidate judged is denied, and Bash handed on" and "a throw before permit judged denies an Edit or a Write, and hands Bash on" fail against the module as it stood before. An `Edit` that falls through the module unjudged is this paragraph wrong.

Where that process is gone, or the pid now names a process with a different start time, the checkout is no session's. There is no timer." ---

## 2. Vocabulary

From HE: a **harness** is everything in an agent except the model, narrowed here to the part a team adds around a product it did not build; a **guide** is a feedforward control that steers before the agent acts; a **sensor** is a feedback control that observes after; **computational** controls are deterministic and fast, **inferential** ones are model-run and slower; **keep quality left** distributes checks by cost, speed and criticality; the **steering loop** is the human iterating on the harness whenever an issue repeats. The full reading of the article, its principles and the claims it attributes to others is `research/harness-research.r1.md` Part A and is not repeated.

From this design: an **instrument** is one `xtask` subcommand that measures or judges something and writes a reading; the **seam manifest** is `seams.toml`; a **reading** is the one-shaped result an instrument writes; a **divergence** is one accepted difference from the oracle, a row in the registry the oracle module owns; a **rendering** is what `xtask status` produces from readings and the manifest. Cadences: **at the tool call** (milliseconds, `permit`), **on save** (seconds), **pre-commit** (under a minute), **per commit** (CI, the 4-minute phpt budget sets the scale), **nightly**, **per revision**.

---

## 3. What the design of record already prescribes

The design never uses the word harness, but most of it is one. Each mechanism, its kind in HE's vocabulary, and where it is specified. Where revision 3 moves the fact's home into the manifest or the registry, the last column says so.

| Mechanism | Kind | Specified in | What it does | Home from revision 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Oracle** | sensor | `CONTEXT.md`; r1 §8.6; r3 §8 | The pinned `php` binary and recipe in `oracle.toml`; one `xtask` module every lane calls, owning `-n`, the 39-entry baseline ini, env, timeout, normalisation; nightly drift re-run. | `oracle.toml` is the only statement of the pin; revisions cite it (§14). The module gains `compare` (§4.3). |
| **Witnesses** | sensor and guide | `CONTEXT.md`; r1 §8.1; r4 §8 | PHP snippet plus pinned `expect` in rustdoc on every kernel and native; `exit=` written only by `--accept`; id is a content hash; coverage rule fails CI. | Unchanged; the id is the divergence key (§4.3). |
| **phpt** | sensor | r1 §8.2; r2 §8; r3 §8; r4 §8 | ~6.5k files per commit through `Capture`, 21.5k nightly, per-extension subsets, `--subset <name>`; nightly `run-tests.php -p` verdict control. | Runner disagreements become divergences of kind `runner` (§4.3). |
| **Compile identity** | sensor | r2 §6.1; r7 §5.7 | `Unit::dump()` byte-identical to php's pre-optimiser dump on 6.5k files per commit, 172k nightly. | A deliberate difference is a divergence of kind `compile`. |
| **Trace dump diff** | sensor | jit r3 §4.2, §7 | Recorded `TRACE start/stop` lines diffed against php's `jit_debug` under `jit.compile=inline`, "within the recorded differences table". | The table's rows become divergences of kind `trace`. |
| **Cross-check** | sensor | r3 §7.1; r4 §7.1; r6 §7.1; design ledger 20 | Each bundled extension rendered as `php --re <name>` and `php --ri <name>` do and held to the oracle's bytes under the recipe, tentative-return row and MINFO table included. | The stub diff was the earlier form; the stubs are read by nothing (revision 41). |
| **Interface locks** | guide and sensor | r2 §4.5; r3 §4.5; r4 §4.5; r7 §9.3 | Generated inventories of public items, diffed by `api-check`; `api.lock` ≤ 400 items; `tier.lock` doubles as `php_jit`'s import allowlist. | The lock set is declared once in the manifest (§4.1). |
| **Dependency direction** | guide and sensor | r3 §4.2; r4 §4.2; ADR-0004 | Crate visibility first; `api-check` fails manifest edges outside the list and `use` paths across lines, and checks the bundle against the `order` its `[[edge]]` names. | Edges declared in the manifest. |
| **Lock-free extensions** | guide and sensor | r5 §10; ADR-0005 | `Mutex`, `RwLock`, `Cell`, `RefCell`, atomics and the once types (`Once`, `OnceLock`, `LazyLock`, `OnceCell`, `LazyCell`, `once_cell::*`) denied under `ext/*` via `clippy::disallowed-types`, and `thread_local!` via `clippy::disallowed-macros`, with reasons. | The list is generated from the manifest into `ext/clippy.toml`. |
| **Budgets** | sensor | r1 §3, §4.1; r4 §4.1; r7 §4.1 | Line ceilings per crate and for the workspace: the total, the new lines, `docs/`, the harness's own prose and the pooled bindings; the lock packages and the clean release build. | Ceilings declared in the manifest; no design document restates one. |
| **`unsafe` audit** | sensor | r1 §10; r4 §10; r7 §4.1 | Four homes: `php_heap`, `ext/*/src/ffi/`, `php_jit::table::code`, `php_jit::enter`; a fifth fails. | Homes declared in the manifest; ADR-0004's two-home sentence is superseded (§14). |
| **Lints** | sensor | r1 §10 | `clippy::pedantic`, `-D warnings`, panic family denied in engine crates. | Unchanged. |
| **Re-entry in signatures** | guide | r2 §10; design principle 9 | Any function that can run PHP code holds a `&mut Request`, as a parameter or in a value whose lifetime names it; the type carries it now. | The lint is retired by revision 117 (#2464). |
| **Request leak gate** | sensor | r2 §5.14; r5 §8; ADR-0005 | Asserted after every request-state drop in tests; names the state type. | Unchanged. |
| **Bundle features** | guide | r4 §4.10; ADR-0004 | One feature per non-default extension on `php_bundle`; `enabled()` in module-startup order, checked against the crate graph. | Unchanged. |
| **Gates G1 to G8** | sensor | r7 §9; jit-research §E.11; llvm-research §G8 | Numbered bars under the measurement protocol (paired reps, A/A control); G1 per commit from the day `php_engine::tier` exists. | Readings of instrument `bench` (§4.2). |
| **Perf bars** | sensor, nightly | r1 §9.2; r3 §8; r5 §9 | Geomean ≤ 1.5× php's interpreter, start ≤ php, compile-on-hit 0, RSS ≤ 1.2×, FastCGI ≥ php-fpm, parse ≥ 25 MB/s, `Cache` hit cost. | Readings of `bench`. Each bar is held to its `[[bench]]` tolerance beside its baseline, which is the ratchet's reading in the store and no manifest value; two established regressions in a row fail it (protocol §12; #1902, #2336, #2659). A bar held to a bound and not a baseline, G1, is held to one plus its row's tolerance (#3130). The absolute targets stay in each reading as the distance to go. |
| **Divergence registry** | sensor and record | r1 §8.5 | One table, each row a phpt name or witness id with a reason and a status; < 20 rows on day 30. | Becomes `docs/divergence.toml`, owned by the oracle module (§4.3). |
| **Schedule gates** | sensor, weekly | r1 §11; r2 §11 | Weekly exit bars measured by the instruments; a missed bar applies the cut order. | Read off the instruments' readings; no section renders the bars (§9, revision 151). |
| **Learnings ledger** | guide | r1 to r7 §2 | Keep/Reject rows with file:line evidence. | Gains a "From this engine" table (§10 rule 1). |
| **ADRs** | guide and record | r3 §10; `docs/adr/*` | Written only on reversal or where a future reader would "fix" a choice; each ends with the sentence that reader needs. | Unchanged; their numbers are carried by manifest rules so they print at the failure (§4.1). |
| **`CONTEXT.md`** | guide | header | Glossary of the seams with an `_Avoid_` list per term. | Gains the harness terms (§4). |
| **Revisions** | maintenance rule | r3 §10 | Files `rN`, highest wins, each opening with "what changed and why" and the check that would show it wrong. | Reopened by §14; this note no longer follows it. |
| **Process rule** | guide, anti-weight | r1 §2.3, §10, §12 | One design document, ADRs on reversal only, no ticket corpus; `docs/` budgeted. | Unchanged; the harness's own files are budgeted (§12 item 6). |

Two observations stand from revision 1. The design is unusually harnessable: strongly typed, eight seams, one executor, one declaration model, one oracle, and its ledger rejects exactly the variety a regulator could not model. And almost every mechanism is a sensor whose guide half is structural; the textual guides (map, ritual, division of labour) are what the harness adds.

---

## 4. The harness's own modules

Five modules, each deep: a small interface, most of the harness's behaviour behind it. All live in `xtask` (budget 14k, r3 §4.1) except the manifest and the registry, which are data at the workspace root and under `docs/`.

### 4.1 The seam manifest: `seams.toml`

**Interface.** One tracked file. Readers: `api-check`, `unsafe-audit`, `budget`, `permit`, `status`, the generator of each `clippy.toml`, and the test that runs one negative-control fixture per rule. Writers: people, in the same commit as the design change that alters a rule; the commit that lands a revision of the design of record is the commit that changes the manifest.

**What it declares, once.** Every rule the design of record states as a sentence today. The list is the acceptance test for the format: a rule below that the format cannot carry is a design error in this section.

```toml
# An excerpt of seams.toml; the file is the statement. One key per line: TOML
# has no statement separator, so `name = "x"; budget = 1` does not parse.
[oracle]
pin = "oracle.toml"                        # the only statement of version, digest, recipe

[budget]                                   # total, new, docs, harness, lock_packages: one integer each;
                                           # clean_build_release_s, api_items, api_private_items, bindings:
                                           # one each where the file states it, and none held where it does not

[[crate]]
name = "php_engine"                        # budget = <lines>, the ceiling `xtask budget` holds
lock = ["api.lock", "sapi.lock", "tier.lock"]
lock_from = { "api.lock" = "php_ext" }
[[crate]]
name = "php_jit"
feature = "jit"
# ... one row per crate of design §4.1, ext/* rows with a ceiling each

[[seam]]
name = "tier"
producer = "php_engine"
consumers = ["php_jit"]
lock = "tier.lock"
allowlist = true
sections = ["face", "kernels"]
adr = "0006"
# ... heap, unit, ops, api, sapi; effects.lock is retired (§14)

[[edge]]
from = "php_bundle"
to = ["ext/*", "php_ext", "php_jit"]
order = "registration"
adr = ["0004", "0006"]
[[edge]]
from = "php_engine::{executor,kernels}"
to = "php_engine::{serve,headers,gpc,output}"
deny = true

[[owed]]
instrument = "phpt"                        # what a crate owes before done (§4.5 condition 1), one row per instrument
crates = "*"                               # a glob or a list of crates
when = "phpt-subset"                       # optional: a predicate the harness derives in code
# ... clippy, test, witness, api-check, budget, unsafe-audit, port-map, crosscheck, miri

```

The rules of the excerpt are the file's own rows, rendered by id, so a row the manifest changes changes here and a row it drops fails the render, naming the id (#2472):

<!-- computed exec cmd="scripts/manifest-rules.sh ext.no-locks unsafe.homes lints.no-panic witness.exit-authored git.no-verify harness.self" inputs=seams.toml,scripts/manifest-rules.sh name=manifest-rules as=fence lang=toml | do not edit; run computed -->
```toml
[[rule]]
id = "ext.no-locks"
scope = "ext/*"
deny_types = ["std::sync::Mutex", "std::sync::RwLock", "std::cell::Cell", "std::cell::RefCell", "std::sync::atomic::*", "parking_lot::*",
              "std::sync::Once", "std::sync::OnceLock", "std::sync::LazyLock", "std::cell::OnceCell", "std::cell::LazyCell", "once_cell::*",
              "std::rc::Rc"]
deny_macros = ["std::thread_local"]
adr = "0005"
text = "Extension state is request-scoped, immutable engine-scoped or process-scoped (ADR-0005, ADR-0010); no lock, cell, once, Rc or thread-local in ext/*. Cross-request caching is api::Cache; a library's object a call into PHP code must outlive is the request's, held by Memory::holding (ADR-0013); a C variable exported from src/ffi/ under php's name is C ABI, not state (#3551)."
# Renders to the generated, committed `ext/clippy.toml` (`disallowed-types`);
# a stale copy is a `budget` failure. The wildcard is expanded from Cargo.lock.

[[rule]]
id = "unsafe.homes"
allow = ["php_heap", "php_process", "ext/*/src/ffi/", "sapi/*/src/ffi/", "timelib_sys", "php_jit::table::code", "php_jit::enter", "xtask::bench::spent::read"]
adr = "0006"
text = "unsafe is permitted only in the homes this rule names; see design §4.1. Every other crate is #![forbid(unsafe_code)]; php_jit, xtask (its bench reader, #3062), and an extension or a host with bindings, are #![deny(unsafe_code)] with the allowance on the named module alone."

[[rule]]
id = "lints.no-panic"
scope = ["crates/*", "ext/*", "sapi/*"]  # every crate a request runs through; php_syntax because the parser runs inside every request (#236), the rest measured clean (#1917)
exempt = ["tests", "xtask", "crates/php_macros"]
deny_lints = ["clippy::unwrap_used", "clippy::expect_used", "clippy::panic", "clippy::unreachable", "clippy::todo", "clippy::unimplemented"]
allow_in_tests = ["clippy::unwrap_used", "clippy::expect_used", "clippy::panic"]  # rendered as clippy's allow-*-in-tests keys (#3078)
text = "Internal invariant failures are InternalError fatals with a message, not panics (design §10)."

[[rule]]
id = "witness.exit-authored"
scope = "**/*.rs"
deny_pattern = '```expect[^\n]*\b(?:exit|signal)=[+-]?\d+'
unless = "xtask witness --accept"
exempt = ["xtask/src/witness/**", "xtask/tests/witness.rs"] # the parser of the fence grammar and its tests carry the shape they parse (#23)
text = "exit= and signal= in a witness are captured by `xtask witness --accept`, never authored."

[[rule]]
id = "git.no-verify"
tool = "Bash"
where = "checkout"   # a scratch repository outside every checkout has no hooks for the flag to skip (#3186, ruled 2026-10-21)
deny_argv = ["--no-verify", "-n", "-c core.hooksPath", "--config-env core.hooksPath"]
with = ["git commit", "git merge"]   # git merge --no-verify skips pre-merge-commit as git commit's skips pre-commit (#2751); -n there is --no-stat, refused all the same
text = "Commits run the pre-commit hook; --no-verify is not available to agents."
remedy = "run the commit as it is, and fix what the hook names"                 # #3627

[[rule]]
id = "harness.self"
tool = ["Edit", "Write"]
where = "checkout"   # as harness.self-shell: a checkout of this repository, never a scratch repository outside them (#3885)
scope = [".claude/**", ".claude-plugin/marketplace.json", "harness-plugins/*/hooks/**", "harness-plugins/*/.claude-plugin/**", "seams.toml", "oracle.toml", "docs/divergence.toml"]
# A skill is an ordinary file, held by review and the [budget] harness
# ceiling (#626); a plugin laid inside one is not, since a project skill's
# plugin loads for every session and its module could approve a call permit
# blocked (#3470). The marketplace says where each plugin's source is, and
# every plugin's hooks/ and .claude-plugin/ are held, so a later plugin is
# covered with no edit here (#3490).
exempt = [".claude/skills/**", "!.claude/skills/**/.claude-plugin/**", "!.claude/skills/**/hooks/**"]
deny = true
text = "Harness configuration, the oracle pin and the divergence registry are edited by a person."
remedy = "propose it on a `for-a-person` ticket; an additive row a person settled lands with `cargo xtask steer`; to discard your change, file it, since `git restore` of these paths is denied too"                 # #3618 ruling 3
```
<!-- /computed in=90b8e6175e1d2f867b9ba3c024443b8c5c6194680a5381adc88a265527f0b2b5 out=17ab53e3e7d1c89c74ac5cf089eb8a939b3623890f6b5b574956e0d758726c63 -->

Each `[[rule]]` has an `id`, a scope or a tool, one predicate, an optional `adr`, an optional `exempt` (paths the rule does not judge, in the scope's grammar; `permit` reads it for every predicate it judges, #23), an optional `exempt_label` (the tracker's labels under which the rule does not judge a call that names an issue, which only a rule `permit` judges at the call may carry, #341, ledger 53), and the `text` a failure prints. A tool is `Edit`, `Write` or `Bash`, the calls `permit` judges, or `Stop`, the hook event, for a rule `done --hook` reads over the reply and `permit` never judges (#32). The predicates are the ones `manifest::Predicates::NAMES` lists, and a rule carries exactly one; the qualifiers, keys that qualify a predicate and are none of their own, are the ones `Predicates::orphaned_qualifier` refuses without the predicate they qualify. This section cites both lists and restates neither (#1909, #2250). `check` names an `xtask` lint, `avoid_from` a glossary file, or `{ file, section }` for one section of it alone (#63), and `generated` the files the manifest renders, each from every rule whose scope reaches its directory (`generated::reaches`). Of the qualifiers, `unless` on `deny_pattern` names the instrument whose work the pattern describes, so that a hit that instrument left behind is that instrument's own and not the call's, judged by the instrument's reading and never by the shape of the call (§4.4). A rule `permit` judges at the call may carry `where = "ticketed"`, which limits it to a checkout whose branch records a ticket, the call's own or the one a leading `cd` names, or `where = "owned"`, which limits it to a checkout another session owns, the owner being the session its owner record names (`xtask worktree add`, `xtask worktree own`) while the Claude Code process the record names runs, a record naming none standing (#3755), asked where each write of the call lands, a write being a change to the checkout's working tree, index, HEAD or operation files and never to the refs, objects or settings its repository shares, or `where = "operating"`, which limits it to such a checkout where a merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase or am stands (`permit::owned`, #2476, #3621), a call that names no session being judged by neither, or `where = "main"`, which limits it to the repository's main checkout, the one whose `.git` is a directory, and never a linked worktree, asked of the command a `deny_pattern` hits (#2751), or `where = "staging"`, which limits it to the merge worktree, the checkout on the branch `staging`, asked of the command a `deny_pattern` hits as `main` is (revision 183), or `where = "checkout"`, which limits it to a checkout of this repository, the main one or a linked worktree as `git worktree list` names them, and never a directory outside them such as a scratch repository, asked where the line stands and where each of its commands runs, by canonical path, and read as a checkout wherever `permit` cannot follow the line (#2887, #2915); a `deny_pattern` may carry `unless_pattern`, which lets a hit through when the command around it, the text between the shell separators on either side, also matches; and a `deny_argv` may carry `unless_argv`, which lets one of its elements through where the `with` phrase its excuse names runs bounded by the option the excuse names, as `require_argv` reads one (`{ "--jit" = "xtask phpt --subset" }`, #3068). Each `[[seam]]` names its lock, and a lock with `sections` is diffed per section; each `[[crate]]` names its ceiling and nothing about `unsafe`; the `unsafe` homes are one rule and appear nowhere else in the file. That is the whole grammar, and `xtask` refuses a manifest with an unknown key.

Two tables beside the rules. `[telemetry]` carries the sensor telemetry's one number, `silent_days` (§7); it is optional, and while it is absent `status` renders no silent column and says which key is unset, since a default in code would be a copy. Each `[[bench]]` is one perf bar's tolerance (protocol §12, revision 147): `bar`, `tolerance_pct` and `nights`, the first and the last night it was derived over, landed by `steer` once the bar's A/A control has two weeks of nights, and until then the 2% floor alone with no `nights` (revision 167); a bar held to a bound reads its bound from its row and from nowhere else, and a bar without one is a series held to none.

**What is derived.**

- `api-check` reads `[[seam]]` and `[[edge]]`: it regenerates each lock from the compiled crate's public items (`lock_from` says which crate for `api.lock`, r4 §4.5), the harness's own included (the `harness` seam, `xtask/xtask.lock`, whose consumers are none: nothing in the workspace imports `xtask`), fails on a diff, tags the seam's reading with the `<lock>-line` rule the manifest carries for it (`api.lock-line`, `xtask.lock-line`), fails a manifest edge not in `[[edge]]`, fails a `use` path crossing a seam, checks the bundle against the `order` its `[[edge]]` names, fails an `ext/*` crate's library dependency on another that no row of design §4.10's list of surfaces names with both crates, writes each extension's `pub` beyond `extension()` to `ext/<name>/surface.lock` and diffs it as an item lock (revision 100), holds each `#[mirrors("ext/x/php_x.h", "sym")]` an extension's sources carry against the pinned php-src, failing a header the tree lacks or a symbol it does not declare `PHPAPI`, `PDO_API` or `ZEND_API` (revision 138), holds `api.lock` to `[budget] api_items` over every line but `__private`'s and to `[budget] api_private_items` over those (design revision 34: the helpers only a macro expansion names, listed and diffed like any line, capped apart so their growth is also a person's decision), and treats `allowlist = true` as "the consumer may import only what the lock names" (r7 §4.2).
- `unsafe-audit` reads `unsafe.homes`. `budget` reads `[budget]` and every `[[crate]]`.
- Each rule with `deny_types` or `deny_methods` renders into a generated, committed `clippy.toml` at each directory its scope names, and the file in a directory carries every such rule whose scope reaches it, reasons included; a `deny_lints` rule's `allow_in_tests` renders there as clippy's own `allow-*-in-tests` keys, so a test may trip the lints it lists while the deny holds every other line (#3078); the files are the ones `generated.committed` names. A stale copy is a `budget` failure (r1 §3 principle 7, applied to a config file). Clippy reads only the nearest file, so a crate is held by its directory's, and a clippy.toml nearer than that would hold it to nothing (revision 1 D.2).
- `permit` reads every `[[rule]]` with a `tool` or a `scope` (§4.4).
- `status --section rules` renders the rule list with its ADR numbers; the map names it by command and carries no region of it (§5, #2806).
- `clippy` reads the rule whose `check` names `silent-return`, `tests.no-silent-return`, beside the `deny_lints` rules: a crate its scope reaches fails its reading naming, as `file:line`, each `return` at a `#[test]` fn's own level, outside a closure, an async block or an item nested in the body, other than `return Err(..)`, in its src test modules and `tests/` alike (#3079).
- One fixture per `[[rule]]` under `xtask/fixtures/rules/<id>/` that must fail, run by `cargo test -p xtask`; a rule without a fixture fails the test. This is Mahout's "a new instrument ships with its negative control" (r1 §2.1) as a structural fact.

**The deletion test.** Delete the manifest and every number and edge reappears in six places: `api-check`'s source, `unsafe-audit`'s source, `budget`'s source, the generated clippy.toml files, the hook's rules, and the map. That is what the inventory found had already happened in the design revisions.

**What is not in it.** The oracle pin (`oracle.toml`, a human decision with its own nightly sensor, §10 rule 5). Divergences (`docs/divergence.toml`, §4.3). Anything decided in prose: the deletion-test arguments, the ledger, the principles.

### 4.2 The reading and its store

**Interface.** Every instrument ends by writing one reading; every rendering reads them; nothing else touches the store. One shape:

```toml
v = 2                                      # the version of the shape; a reading with no `v` is the first, which spells `key` as `tree`
instrument = "witness"                     # api-check | permit | done | track-work | ticket | usage | bench | budget | clippy | test | miri | phpt | port-map | compile-identity | crosscheck | trace-dump | fold | fpm-compare | soak | unsafe-audit | steer | divergence-reason | witness | vocab, the subcommands that write one, in cli order; fuzz | mutants are named here and not built yet
scope = "ext/pcre"                         # a crate, a corpus subset, a gate id, or "workspace"
rule = "witness.coverage"                  # a manifest rule id when one applies
status = "fail"                            # pass | fail | skip
numbers = { run = 212, failed = 3, missing = 1 }
message = "ext/pcre: 3 witnesses differ from the oracle; preg_replace_callback lacks a witness"
adr = ""                                   # from the manifest when the rule names one
key = "9f3c…"                              # the hash of the scope's files and of every workspace crate in its declared dependency closure: its normal, build and dev dependencies, and from those their normal and build dependencies, as cargo's fingerprint follows them
recipe = "5d1c…"                           # an instrument under the oracle's recipe (reading::UNDER_RECIPE): the digest of oracle.toml's values the run was taken under, less each build's provenance; absent elsewhere (#1387, #2969)
commit = "0b62ae0"
at = 2026-09-07T01:05:49Z
session = ""                               # the hook's session_id on a permit reading; empty elsewhere
```

**Store.** Three things under `target/harness/` (revision 83). `<instrument>/<scope>.toml` is the scope's last reading, overwritten on each run. The index, `<instrument>/<scope>/<key>.toml`, is the latest reading of the scope over each key it was taken over, written whole by a rename so two writers need no lock, the newest 32 keys of a scope kept, and a reading taken under an oracle pin kept per pin, `<key>-<pin>.toml`: "the latest reading over a key", which `done` and every `--staged` instrument ask, is one file read. The log, `log/<instrument>.jsonl`, is append-only history, one line per reading and one file per instrument, read by the renderings of history (the permits section, usage, the dashboard) and by no question about a key, save `permit`'s look for evidence of a key the index has let go. The phpt runs `xtask phpt` keeps are in the store beside the three, `phpt-runs/<engine tree>/<scope>.json` with the targets under the pin beside them, so an upload of the store carries them and a run CI took can serve as a merge base's (revision 136, #725); they are no reading, and `done` reads them as condition 1's `phpt` (§4.5). The engine tree is the tracked files under what the binary is built from, as cargo's dep-info beside it names it: each package it links, whole, every host's, and the workspace's manifest, lock and toolchain, each set a run was kept under kept beside the runs, `phpt-runs/built-from/`, and a run looked for under the tree over every set kept; a binary with no dep-info of its own is keyed over every crate directory. So a commit that writes no source of the binary, `seams.toml`, `ext/clippy.toml` or a crate it does not link, keeps every run, at the tree and as the next branch's base, and a binary is behind the tree only where a path its dep-info names, or a linked package's manifest, was written after it (revision 158, #3109, #3110). A run carries the digest of the pin it was taken under, and a run under another pin is stale, as a reading under the recipe is (revision 145, #2725). CI uploads the store: each per-commit job its own, joined for `done` and kept as the run's `harness` artefact, and the dashboard's jobs the log (#2253). The `key` field is what makes a reading fresh or stale without a turn counter: a reading is fresh for a scope when the scope's key still matches and, for an instrument that runs under the oracle's recipe (`reading::UNDER_RECIPE`), when the pin's digest does too: a change to the pin moves what php prints as surely as a change to the crate (#1387). The pin's digest leaves out a build's provenance (`image`, `closure`) and nothing else: those record what a binary was made from and are never an input to a run, and every other value of every section is digested, other platforms' builds and variants no subset names among them. A key is left out only where `oracle::Provenance` declares it, so a key the pin gains later is digested by default (revision 149, #2969). The closure is the graph the manifests declare, read from `cargo metadata --no-deps`, so an optional dependency counts whether a feature turns it on or not, and a key can only count a crate too many, never one too few (#1318, ADR-0011). A crate's key also covers the files beyond its directory that its tests read as the tree holds them, listed once, in `key::READ_BY_TESTS` in xtask's sources: xtask's are the manifest, the pin, the registry, the glossary, `data/`, `scripts/`, `harness-plugins/`, `.github/`, `.gitattributes`, `.gitignore`, `ext/clippy.toml` and four crates' files its tests read, so a commit that changes `seams.toml` alone stales every reading of xtask and touches it for `done` and pre-commit (#3646). `ext/zend_test`'s crash test reads the pin. The files beyond its directory a crate's `build.rs` reads are listed beside them, in `key::READ_BY_BUILDS`: `ops.toml`, which `php_unit`'s and `php_engine`'s read, and `data/func-infos.toml`, `php_ssa`'s. A change to one touches the crate that reads it, and stales the reading of every crate built from that crate as a change to its own files does, since what it generates is compiled into it (#3654). A change to `php_engine` stales the reading of every crate built from it; a change to one leaf extension stales that extension and the crates built from it, and no other.

**Readers.** `status` (§4.6), `done` (§4.5), the steering ledger (§10 rule 6), the CI summary that answers HE's "which sensor never fails" (§7). `permit` writes a reading per denial, which is the steering-loop capture of revision 2 without `$.store` or a second file. The dashboard reads readings through `xtask status --format jsonl --every --log`, never a log line's numbers itself, and names the xtask commit that rendered them. Which reading stands, and what a series sums, stay its own.

`session` is what makes "this session" of §4.6 a rendering rather than a guess: `permit` copies the hook's `session_id` onto every reading it writes, and `status` counts denials per session from the log.

**A bench reading's two halves.** `bench` is the instrument whose reading is not the whole answer, so its halves are named here and not inferred again at each bar. The agent's half is the run, and it is an ordinary instrument: `bench` measures the scope under the protocol this engine inherits — paired reps, the A/A control, status labels, load average recorded (`docs/perf/measurement-protocol.md`; third-engine-design.md §9) — and ends as every instrument ends, in one reading, scoped by the gate id or the bar's name, the measurement in `numbers` and `status` pass or fail against the bar. Its exit is 0 whatever the reading says, which is what `permit --bench` already turns on: a bench number is a claim a person reads with the A/A control (§4.4; revision 16, #61), so the agent states the measurement and never the conclusion (`AGENTS.md`). The one exception is the rule protocol §12 holds a nightly bar to: a bar held to its tolerance that regressed two nights running fails the nightly lane and is filed (revision 147, #2659). The person's half is the sign-off: reading the number beside its control and saying what it means — the perf claim itself, the revisit a missed G1 asks of `jit-design.md` §3, the cut order a missed weekly bar applies (§11). An acceptance criterion that wants both is therefore two boxes and not one: "a fresh passing `bench` reading at G1" is an agent's and is measured under the protocol, "the number signed off" is a person's, and a single box phrased as both can be ticked by neither.

**The deletion test.** Delete the store and nine channels reappear: `jq .summary phpt-last.json`, `jq` over the mutants outcomes, `wc -l` over the locks, `grep -c` over the divergence table, the `context` note, `harness-turn.json`, the `$.store` counters, `harness-steering.jsonl`, and a per-gate CI line. Revision 2 had all nine.

### 4.3 The oracle module owns the comparison and the divergence registry

**Interface.** One handle and four functions on it, plus the comparison pair. `Pin::oracle()` returns the oracle for one run: it asserts the pinned binary's digest once, before the first program, and refuses the whole run on a mismatch, so a lane that runs a thousand snippets hashes the binary once (revision 16). On it, `run` is revision 1's: run this program, SKIPIF or `-r` snippet under the harness regime and return normalised output (r3 §8); `run_under(invocation)` is `run` with the binary named rather than assumed, so a lane that measures the engine runs it under the one recipe and the two are comparable. An `Invocation` names the run, the binary and the program, and carries over the recipe what a `.phpt` section adds and what no section gives; its fields are the region below, rendered from the type, so a field the type gains is on this page when it lands. The flags before the program are where php takes a mode of its own rather than a setting. `-l`, which compiles a program and does not run it, is the compile-identity lane's (#101), so that a corpus written to be run is compiled and never run to measure the compiler. The flag is on the invocation and not in the pin's `[regime.dump]` (#111): a regime is what the pin says php runs under, ini and the arguments no ini reaches (`-e`, #1780), and `-l` is a lane's mode, not a setting; the pin is a person's file with its own review (§11), so a lane that wants a mode of php would wait on a person for each; and `run_under` puts the same flags before the program for the binary under test as for the oracle, which is what leaves the compiler as the only difference between two dumps. Whether php prints its op arrays is the regime, and whether the program then runs is the flag. What each binary folds from its own build is put out of its dump before the two are compared (#2692): both compilers fold `PHP_BINARY` and `PHP_BUILD_DATE` into the op array as php's `zend_try_ct_eval_const` does, so the two dumps of a file that names either differ by construction, and the lane asks each binary once per run, under the recipe and regimes the dump runs under, for its own two values and writes `<PHP_BINARY>` and `<PHP_BUILD_DATE>` where exactly that binary's value appears in a string literal of its dump, whole or folded into a concatenation. A registry row would accept the whole file, since the registry is looked up per file; the placeholder hides the value and nothing else, so a compiler that folds a value other than its own, or folds where php does not, still differs. `oracle::normalise(rules, bytes)` is the recipe's CRLF and trim rule alone, which a stream and a `.phpt` expectation both go through, the stream then also losing its scratch prefix. The module also owns what every lane needs of the pin and nothing else does: the runner, which says whether a lane ran the engine, a control or nothing because the binary is absent; `Source::tree()`, the one answer to where the pinned php-src tree is: the directory `TEST_PHP_SRCDIR` names when it is set, upstream's own variable, and the pin's `[source].path` otherwise, which is the tree on the machine the pin was written on (#36); a test that would skip for a fact this machine has not, the pin's tree or binary, the MySQL server of `ci/mysql/compose.yaml`, the locales the tests name or a main thread whose size reads, fails instead where the machine promised it through `XTASK_PROMISED`, naming what it looked for (#38, #3084, #3180), so a broken provisioning is a red test and never a quiet skip; the words are `tree`, `oracle`, `mysql`, `locale` and `main-stack`, and the tests that cannot depend on xtask, those of ext/mysqli and ext/pdo_mysql through `MysqlServer::with`, ext/standard's and php_compile's, read theirs through the one skip in `crates/php_compile/tests/support/skip.rs`, the lowest of those crates and one each of the others is built from; and the expectation grammar (`--EXPECT--`, `--EXPECTF--`, `--EXPECTREGEX--`) a `.phpt` and a witness fence share. `oracle::compare(id, expected, actual) -> Verdict`, where `id` is a divergence key and `Verdict` is `Same`, `Diverged(Divergence)` when the registry has an accepted row for the id, or `Differs(diff)`. Every instrument that holds the engine to the oracle calls `compare` and never diffs on its own: witnesses (id `witness:<hash>`), phpt verdicts and the nightly runner control (id `phpt:<path>`, kinds `phpt` and `runner`), compile identity (id `compile:<path>`), the crosscheck lane (id `crosscheck:<name>`, kind `crosscheck`, one row per extension whose rendering a person has accepted apart from the oracle's), the trace dump diff (id `trace:<site>`), and the differential fuzzer, whose minimised failure is proposed as a row with status `open`.

<!-- computed exec cmd="cargo xtask status --section invocation --tracked" inputs=xtask/src/oracle/mod.rs,xtask/src/status/invocation.rs name=invocation | do not edit; run computed -->

| field | type | group | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| `regimes` | `&[&str]` | no section gives it | The `[regime.<name>]`s of the pin the run stands under, in order. |
| `ini` | `&[String]` | a `.phpt` section adds it | Ini after the recipe's and the regime's: a test's `--INI--`. |
| `flags` | `&[String]` | no section gives it | Flags before the program's path, where php takes a mode of its own. |
| `args` | `&[String]` | a `.phpt` section adds it | Arguments after the program's path: a test's `--ARGS--`. |
| `env` | `&[(String, OsString)]` | a `.phpt` section adds it | Environment entries over the recipe's, a later one of a name over an earlier. |
| `stdin` | `Stdin` | a `.phpt` section adds it | What the program reads on stdin: a test's `--STDIN--`, or a CGI-shaped test's body. |
| `streams` | `Streams` | a `.phpt` section adds it | How the program's two streams out are taken: to files apart, or through pipes as run-tests.php takes a test's, stderr into stdout where its `--CAPTURE_STDIO--` captures both. |
| `cwd` | `Option<&Path>` | no section gives it | The working directory: the program's own where `None`, which is a witness's; php-src's root for a phpt test and the sections run beside it, as run-tests.php runs them (#952). |
| `raw` | `bool` | no section gives it | Whether what the program wrote is handed back as it wrote it and not normalised: a CGI run's, whose headers run-tests.php parts from the body before it trims (run-tests.php:2530-2533). |

<!-- /computed in=15a10f534340b5436c41bd755105bb0d0b1cb1ff9c1c84be35fcace0f28d770a out=0f403af025fb1822890e1582d53a55da45f59c26199a988cd5503283858ffbf5 -->

**The tally.** The phpt and witness lanes, and the compile-identity lane when it comes, share one tally (`tally::Tally<J>`): it owns the runner ladder (a control is a skip and not a reading of the engine, an absent binary is a skip, the engine is pass or fail), the rule that nothing run is not a green reading, the print loop over every non-pass item and the reading it writes; a lane contributes its judgement variants and how to judge one item, and nothing else (revision 16). A `lane` is a scheduled run of a corpus (§7), never the tally.

**The registry.** `docs/divergence.toml`, one row shape:

```toml
[[divergence]]
id = "trace:nested-entry-return"
kind = "trace"                             # witness | phpt | runner | compile | crosscheck | trace | wire | makala
reason = "A trace rooted at the entry frame of a nested execute stops with Return one opline earlier than php's (jit-design.r3 §4.2)."
status = "accepted"                        # open | accepted | fixed
since = "2026-09-06"
```

The two rows the JIT note carries in prose and the runner disagreements revision 4 §12 allows in the first weeks are rows here, and the "target zero" of the runner control is the count of kind `runner`. A row's `cause` says whether it is parity debt: `debt` by default; `php-ub` where php's behaviour is undefined or it crashes and the engine does something defined (#2220); `by-design` where the design of record chose the difference, its reason citing the section or ADR; `oracle-build` where the oracle's build or platform makes it, its `retires` naming what it retires at, `oracle-rebuild`, `pin`, `fixture` or `platform` (#2460, #2462). The day-30 target counts the rows whose cause is `debt`, and `status --section divergence` counts each other cause on its own line after the total. A row may pin the difference it was ruled on by `oracle` and `engine`, the `sha256:` digests of both sides' lines, named both or neither: `oracle::verdict` accepts its difference only while both stand and names the side that moved otherwise, and an accepted `trace:dump/<kernel>` row must name them, which `divergence-reason` holds (#3291's ruling). A re-pin is a changed value, a person's edit, as a new acceptance is. A row whose cause a regime of the pin proves names it by `proven_at`: the witness lane, which alone holds a lane's id it can run again under a regime, runs each fence the row holds once more under the oracle in that regime beside the fence's own, and fails the fence, naming its id and the regime, where that run is not the linked engine's, so a row of an optimizer's cause cannot mask a difference the engine causes; a row naming `proven_at` that holds an id no fence is refused at load (#3365's ruling, revision 174). A Markdown rendering for people is a computed region in `docs/divergence.md` whose command is `xtask status --section divergence`; the region is the view, the TOML is the truth (CM-spec:3).

`oracle::verdict(registry, id, met, expected, actual)` is `compare` with the caller's match standing where byte equality stands, for an expectation that is a pattern rather than bytes (`--EXPECTF--`, `--EXPECTREGEX--`); `compare` is `verdict` with `expected == actual`, so one place decides what the registry means and the count of comparisons that consult it is still one.

**What the recipe's environment is.** The pin's `[recipe.env]` is the witness regime's: `TEST_PHP_EXECUTABLE` there names the pinned oracle, which is what a witness runs. A phpt lane runs a different binary, and the corpus spawns the binary under test by that name and by `TEST_PHP_EXECUTABLE_ESCAPED`, so the runner sets both over the recipe's and under the test's own `--ENV--`. The pin does not move for this: its value is right for the regime it states.

**What a crate row's oracle variant is.** Beside `[oracle]`, the pin carries `[variant.<name>]`: the pinned tag built with the main oracle's configure line plus the variant's `flags` and nothing else, so it differs from the oracle only where intended, with one table per platform its binary is pinned for (`darwin-arm64`, `linux-x86_64`, `linux-aarch64`; #1782, #1890). Every platform's table carries `path`, the install prefix #1889 builds to (`/opt/dax-php/oracle/<variant>/bin/php`), and the binary's `md5`; a relative `path` is the pin's directory's. A Linux table also carries `image`, the base image's manifest digest, which #1889 pins and rebuilds from, and the lane checks the binary by its md5; the macOS table, built on the maintainer's machine, names no image. A `[[crate]]` row's `oracle = "<name>"` runs that crate's `crosscheck`, `witness` and `phpt` under the variant's build for the platform the lane runs on, and every crate without the key under the main oracle. A row naming a variant holds in its phpt subset, beside its own directories, every test of the pinned tree whose --EXTENSIONS-- names an extension the main oracle does not load and the variant does (php -n -m of each pinned binary). The test leaves the subset it stands in rather than being skipped there by both sides. A test the lane cannot build for, needing another feature or a host, is named outside the target with that reason (#3309, #3312). A variant is not a second recipe: `Pin::variant` is the pin with the variant's binary standing for `[oracle]`'s, so the recipe and its regimes are the main oracle's, an entry of the recipe's environment that names the main oracle's binary naming the variant's instead, and the binary's md5 is asserted once per run as the main oracle's is. An unknown variant fails that crate's readings, not the manifest: a row naming a variant the pin does not carry fails the crate in the three lanes, naming it, where refusing `Manifest::load` would stop `permit`, `done` and every lane over one row (revision 80, #1948).

A row whose one reason holds for many ids of its kind names them in `covers`, and `compare` reads an id in `covers` as it reads the row's own; the row counts once against the day-30 target, which counts reasons, not tests (revision 62, #1220). The fixture that fails without it is a `phpt:` id listed only in `covers`, read as `Differs`. A `trace:` row's `covers` may name `witness:` ids. A covered fence is held to its pinned `expect` against the engine alone: the oracle still runs and its signal, status or bytes are recorded in the reading, but they are not judged. This is how a witness pins the engine's side of a row where the oracle crashes or prints bytes php does not define. Such a fence's id answers `Registry::pinning` with the row and `Registry::accepted` with none, so the engine's miss is a failure and never `diverged` (revision 111, #1728).

**Who writes a row.** A person, with a reason (§11). The fuzzer and `compare` may add a row with status `open` and an empty reason; `done` fails while an `open` row touches the scope.

**The deletion test.** Delete `compare` and four instruments each decide alone whether a difference is accepted and against which list; three lists existed.

### 4.4 `xtask permit`: the judge at the tool call

**Interface.** Input: one intended tool call, as the JSON the shipped hook receives on stdin (`tool_name`, `tool_input` with `file_path`, `old_string`, `new_string`, `content` or `command`). Output: the hook's JSON, either `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"<text> (ADR-0005)"}}` or allow with `additionalContext` carrying the notes; exit 0 whatever it decides, since the decision is in the JSON (Hooks-ref, PreToolUse decision control). The one non-zero exit is the refusal to decide at all: a build whose compiled digest of what it judges by is not the tree's, or one that cannot read the manifest (revision 13). Every denial, every note and every refusal is a reading with `instrument = "permit"` and the rule id.
