A build whose digest is not the tree's writes a `skip` under `permit.stale-build` naming the candidate it hands the call to, since that candidate judges it; a call no candidate can judge writes a `fail` under `permit.unjudged-call`.

**Implementation.** The `[[rule]]` rows of the manifest with a `tool` or a `scope`, evaluated in order: the region-body rule of revision 2 (an `Edit` whose `old_string` lies in a computed region body, CM ADR 0005), `witness.exit-authored`, `witness.capture-authored`, `unsafe.homes`, `ext.no-locks`, `api.lock-line` as a note, `git.no-verify`, `lanes.nightly`, `computed.force`, `harness.self`, `harness.self-shell`. A `deny_pattern` over an `Edit` or a `Write` is asked of what the write adds to the file it leaves, never of the text the call types alone: an edit can finish a hit its own text does not hold, and a pattern over a block, such as `witness.capture-authored`'s fence from its `expect` line to its close, is never whole in an edit inside the block (#510, e7b94f35). Argument parsing for `Bash` is conservative, splitting on unquoted whitespace and `;`, `&&`, `|`; pre-commit remains the sensor of record for what a shell can hide. Path to crate is one function over the manifest's `[[crate]]` rows. **The after-half** (#1671). `permit` judges a call before it runs, and `permit --after`, the `PostToolUse` hook on `Bash` in `harness-plugins/harness/hooks/hooks.json` (#1517) and the `PostToolUseFailure` hook beside it, which a `Bash` call that exits non-zero gets in its place, its answer naming that event and carrying no `decision` (#2762), judges what a `Bash` call did to the files `harness.self` holds, against a record `permit` took before it: the record is keyed by the hook's `session_id` and `tool_use_id`, under `target/harness/permit/before/`, and is taken before any build decides whether it judges, so a call with no record is never put back. It holds tracked files only, since `.claude/` also holds Claude Code's untracked state, written between the two hooks on a person's act, and a new untracked file under the scope is not held; a file the rule's `exempt` covers, `.claude/skills/**` (revision 70), is not held either. The checkout held is the one the call ran in, its `cwd` moved by the line's leading `cd`, `pushd` and `popd`, a variable the line assigned before them read as its value, or by git's `-C` where every command past them is git and names one directory, at the top of its worktree, and never the hook's own where the call ran elsewhere, nor every worktree of the clone (#2597, #2607). What the call wrote is kept as a blob the denial names before the file is put back. `steer`'s write stands when the call's line runs `xtask steer` and steer's reading of that call, stamped over the file it left, names every addition; a TOML comment written before steer in the same call is the one write it cannot tell apart, and it stands, since a comment binds nothing. After the call, a harness.self file whose blob moved is put back only where the call's line can be read to write it: a redirection into it, a writer handed its path, an interpreter whose program names it, or a program run by its path whose bytes name it; and never one that a merge, cherry-pick, revert or rebase in progress in that checkout has staged, which is kept and named for the operation's commit to carry (#2597), nor one history carries: `HEAD` moved during the call and the call's line runs no git command that moves it, its blob for the file moved with it, and the file stands at the blob `HEAD` holds now, as another session's merge commit leaves it, which is kept and named and recorded nowhere (#3220). A change the line cannot write is kept and named, since a person's edit made while a call runs is lost by putting it back; until a person commits or restores it, that change is one of `done`'s unmet conditions (#1931). That holds in main's checkout and in a checkout no session owns, where persons edit. In a linked worktree the call's session owns by its owner record (revision 179), a harness.self file that moved during the call and now differs from `HEAD`, with no steer reading or approval covering it, is put back and the call denied, naming the blob the write is kept as, whichever of the session's calls the change is caught in: the denial names that call and says the write may have come from a background call (revision 185).

**What an `unless` permits.** A `deny_pattern` row that names an instrument (§4.1) refuses an authored line and not the line itself, so the judge needs evidence of whose hand wrote it, and the evidence is the instrument's reading and the tree it is stamped with (§4.2, §13 D.18). A hit is that instrument's own, and permitted, when every match already stands in the file as the instrument last saw it: each match in what the call would write is in the file on disk, the file lies in a crate the manifest names, and that crate has a reading of the named instrument whose stamp is the crate's key now. Where no such reading stands, a match is the instrument's own when the block around it, its line and the lines on either side up to a blank one, stands in the file as it was at one of the last commits to change the file whose crate a reading of the instrument was taken over, the commit's tree hash read from its objects, and the reading found in this workspace's store or in the store of another worktree of the clone (#1448): a merge makes a state of the crate no branch measured, and the line a branch's `--accept` captured is still the branch's capture; a block typed by hand stands in no commit an instrument measured. `witness --accept` writes the fence line and constructs its reading after, so the line it captured is inside the tree the reading is over. A hit neither kind of evidence covers is denied whatever the store holds. That is where an authored line is refused — at the call, before what it would write is in any tree — and equally where a line a hand left in the tree is refused, by `permit --diff`, the call pre-commit makes over the staged diff (§7), the crate carrying no reading of the instrument or one stamped with an older tree. Evidence the judge cannot read is no evidence: a file it cannot read, a path no `[[crate]]` row covers, a store or a git that answers with an error each leave the hit denied, where the row stood before the qualifier. The reading's own status is condition 1's to hold (§4.5) and is not read here: a failing `witness` is the instrument's to print, in its own words, and saying it again in the row's would name the wrong problem.

**What a person's approval admits.** What only a person does stays the person's decision, and typing it becomes an agent's act once the person approved it in the session (#2247). The approval is the person's answer to an `AskUserQuestion` call that names the file and whose chosen option's preview carries the change as a unified diff, the option's label saying it applies it ("Apply …", "Approve …"), since the person reads the label as much as the preview. It lifts `harness.self`'s denial of an `Edit` or a `Write` that realizes exactly that diff from the file as it stands, and `harness.self-shell`'s denial of a `Bash` line (#625) where every harness file the line can write has such an approval pending, `permit --after` then holding what the line wrote to the diff and putting back any other write; the denial becomes a note naming the call that asked. The answer is read where the model cannot write it: the record Claude Code keeps for an `AskUserQuestion` call, which the command hook and `--after` read from the session's transcript at the hook's `transcript_path` and the function hook from the engine's own messages, each adapter its own evidence and nothing else. The model's text, what a command printed, a file the agent wrote, the model's own input, another session's transcript and an answer the dialog gave for an absent person are none of it, each a fixture that must stay denied. A call in a subagent is admitted by nothing, since the hook hands it the main session's transcript and a subagent cannot ask the person. An approval is not counted: once the change stands its diff no longer applies. An approval in a GitHub comment is not one, since agents post through the person's `gh` account. `permit` itself records each change an approval admitted (#2281), one entry per change in the main worktree's store, which outlives a worktree, with the question and its options, the answer and notes, the file, the diff and its hash, the file's blob before and after, the session, the two calls' ids, the time and the commit the checkout stood on: an `Edit` or a `Write` when it is admitted at the call, a `Bash` line once `--after` finds what it wrote standing as approved. The commit that carried a change is the one `git log --find-object` names for its blob after. The transcript the answers are read from is guarded beside the files: a shell line that writes under `~/.claude/projects/`, in the shapes `harness.self-shell` reads, is denied by `harness.transcript` (#2282), which no approval lifts, and the function-hook modules that hand `permit` the engine's answers are a person's files under `harness.self` (#2283).

**Adapters, two.** The shipped command hook, always on: `PreToolUse` with matcher `Edit|Write|Bash`, command `xtask permit` reading stdin, in `harness-plugins/harness/hooks/hooks.json`, a person's file under `harness.self`. The function hook, `harness-plugins/harness/hooks/harness.ts`, a person's file under `harness.self` as well (#2283), on unless `functionHook` in the plugin's `userConfig` is set false: a `tool.call` handler on `Edit`, `Write` and `Bash` that writes the event in the command hook's stdin shape (D.16) to `$.process.run(["xtask", "permit", "--above-the-command-hook"], { stdin })`, returns `{ deny }` or `{ ...result, context }`, and calls `$.ui.invalidate("prompt.context")` after a `Bash` call that ran an instrument. It judges the call before the command hook does, and the flag says so: a denial ends the call there and is recorded there; an allow goes on to the command hook, which judges the same call and records the notes, so a note is one reading and not two. It adds nothing the command hook does not do except the invalidation, the denials it keeps in `$.store` as `{ rule, adr, tool, at }` for a desk to draw, and in-process latency. The module fails open on `Bash` and closed on an `Edit` or a `Write` no judge saw, as the command hook does (#3489): a walk no candidate judged and a throw before `permit` judged both deny the write, where no managed settings seat the built-in guard and another mod could otherwise approve what the command hook denies (#3470), and each candidate has the command hook's 60 s, so a rebuild is judged rather than denied (revision 2 C.8 item 8; revisions 11, 13 and 175). Each walks its candidates, every built `xtask` that stands and then `cargo run`, and takes the first that exits 0: `permit`'s exit is 0 whatever it decides, so a non-zero one is a build that never judged — one handed a flag it does not know, one that cannot read the manifest, or one whose digest of what it judges by, `seams.toml` and the `.rs` files under `xtask/src` written into the binary by `build.rs` and compared with the tree by `built` (revision 16: a swap file or a `.DS_Store` there is not a source and does not move the digest), is not the tree's — and neither adapter passes a call on that build's silence. The built two are walked in orders that are not equal, the command hook release first and the module debug first, so one stale build does not silence both layers at once, as it did on 2026-09-08. A built candidate's stderr is dropped and the cargo candidate's kept: a refusal from a binary the walk passes over is not a message for the model, and cargo closes the list, so its stderr is the one a person needs when every candidate refused (revision 16). `cargo run` closes both lists, and its build re-runs `build.rs`, so the candidate that rebuilds carries the digest of what it is about to read and a list cannot end in nothing but refusals for want of a rebuild. A call in another checkout of the repository whose manifest the build was not compiled against, a worktree laid before a `seams.toml` change, is not refused: the build hands it to that checkout's own `xtask`, the same walk over the worktree's built binaries and then `cargo run` there, rooted at the worktree, and returns its decision, so each tree is judged by the rules it carries; a hand-off no candidate judges is the call unjudged (revision 60). The command hook is two entries over that one walk: on `Edit` and `Write` a list no candidate judged ends in a deny, since a write no judge saw does not proceed, and on `Bash` the call is handed on, since an agent repairs a broken harness with `Bash` and denying all three deadlocks the repair (revision 13). A third caller is pre-commit, which runs `permit` over the staged diff as a whole. There a harness file is a note, since a person commits those, except in a commit from an agent's session, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` set: one that stages a harness file is refused unless a steer reading covers the staged content, passing, written and stamped over the staged blob, or the commit is a merge taking that file whole from one side's commit, and pre-merge-commit asks the same of a merge git commits itself. A commit without the variable, a person's, is admitted as before (revision 185).

**What the seam does not cover.** The seam is only as good as the set of tools that cross it: the matcher is `Edit`, `Write` and `Bash`, so a GitHub MCP server attached later would not be judged at all, and `xtask ticket` is the closer only for what reaches the hook. The tracker is `gh` and nothing else, no trait and no second adapter, until a second tracker exists to make a seam real (#28).

**Cost.** `cargo run -q -p xtask -- permit` after a warm build is a process start and a manifest parse, tens of milliseconds; an `xtask` binary installed by `cargo install --path xtask` is the same without cargo. The bar: p99 under 100 ms at the tool call on the developer machine, measured by `permit --bench`, a measurement and not a gate: its reading carries pass or fail against the bar and its exit is 0 whatever the reading says, since a bench number is a claim a person reads with the A/A control (AGENTS.md; revision 16, #61). If it is missed, the manifest's rules are compiled into the binary at build time and the parse goes away; the interface does not move.

**What leaves the harness.** `rules.ts` and its tests. The function hook is enabled (revision 10), so the adapter, its tracked `tsconfig.json` and the declaration file stay. Pre-commit copies the declarations Claude Code ships into `harness-plugins/types`, a tracked file, whenever a commit stages a path under `harness-plugins/`, and runs `claude plugin validate --strict` and `claude plugin test` on each plugin there, each directory with a `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`; `tsc -p harness-plugins`, at pre-commit and in CI's test job, holds the module to the committed declarations and fails loudly when they are absent (revision 16, #53, #62, #1736). How a build of Claude Code hands its declarations over is that build's and has moved once already, so the contract is the copy and the type-check, and pre-commit says by name when this Claude Code handed over none. The `types-version` region that would make the pin a sensed fact is postponed (#62).

**The deletion test.** Delete `permit` and the rules are evaluated twice, in Rust after the write and in TypeScript before it, on a surface whose events "may change between releases without notice" (FH-dts:1-6).

### 4.5 `xtask done`: the definition of done

**Interface.** `xtask done [--base <ref>] [--hook]` prints the conditions of done that are unmet for the changes since `--base` (default: the merge base with `main`, the checkout's branch where it has one and `origin`'s otherwise), exits 1 if any, 0 if none; with `--hook` it reads the Stop hook's stdin, exits 0 when `stop_hook_active` is true, and otherwise prints `{"decision":"block","reason":"<the list>"}` (Hooks-ref, Stop).

**The conditions**, from revision 1's map item 6, now checked rather than stated. The list is rendered from `done::Condition`, the first rustdoc paragraph of each variant, and `done::judge` has an arm for every variant, so the definition is what `done` judges and cannot lag it (#2309):

<!-- computed exec cmd="cargo xtask status --section conditions --tracked" inputs=xtask/src/done.rs,xtask/src/status/conditions.rs name=conditions | do not edit; run computed -->

1. For every crate with a changed file, a fresh passing reading exists for `clippy`, `test`, `witness`, `api-check`, `budget` and `unsafe-audit`, for `port-map` where the crate is the one `api.lock` lives with, and for `crosscheck` in both of its columns where the crate is an extension the binary carries; `witness` is not asked of the harness, and fresh means the reading's `key` is the crate's key now and, under the oracle's recipe, its `recipe` the pin's digest now. For `phpt`, a touched crate's subset is held to no regression against the base `done` measures since: every test met at the merge base is met here, read from the run kept at the merge base's engine tree and the run kept at this tree's, a comparison a skip does not meet. The absolute reading is still written and rendered, the port's meter as the crosscheck is, and the `porting` key's `phpt` half goes with it, since a partial port no longer fails for the tests it has not reached. The missing engine binary stays the one excused skip (#132). The subsets held are those of a crate the binary under test carries, or one whose row names an oracle variant, which the per-commit lane runs (revision 63, #2029). A crate whose library or binary sources name `debug_assertions` is asked for `clippy` twice, at its name and at `<crate>/release`, the release profile's lint on its library and binaries (#2392). For a change that moves `php_heap`'s key (ADR-0011), `done` reads a fresh `miri` reading of `php_heap` and holds `undefined` to zero (`unsupported` is reported, not held, under the permissive provenance #2415 ruled).
2. Every kernel or native added in the diff has a witness: the `witness` reading's `missing` is zero for the crate.
3. If a `.lock` differs from its regenerated form, the lock file is in the diff, read from the `api-check` seam readings and the diff's paths.
4. No `open` divergence touches a changed scope: a touched crate's name or a changed path in an id the row names or covers, or a row the diff proposes.
5. `permit` over the diff denies nothing: every changed file is judged as the `Write` that would put its content in place.
6. `seams.toml`, `oracle.toml`, `docs/divergence.toml` and the plugin's own files, what the manifest's `deny = true` rule covers, are unchanged in the diff, or the diff is by a person (the hook cannot tell, so the Stop hook reports it as a note, and pre-push, run by the person, passes).
7. The ticket the branch records (`branch.<name>.ticket`, the record `xtask worktree add --ticket` writes) has no unticked acceptance box, or was withdrawn, read from the `ticket` reading over its number and never from the tracker (#31, #221); a branch that records no ticket, and a detached `HEAD`, are not asked.
8. A change `permit --after` kept, one no call's line could write, is unmet until a person commits it or restores it to `HEAD`, read from the sensor's record in the store and not from the diff (§4.4, #1931).
9. A ticket a merge since the base names, and that `xtask ticket merged` left open, for want of a ready record or because its close did not go through (#2804), is unmet until a close replaces that reading, the merge at `HEAD` being read where the base is `HEAD`, as on `main` (#2358); a merge that lands part of a ticket, one with no ready record, a box unticked and an open ticket blocking it, is a partial landing and is not named (#2394).
10. For a diff that changes what decides in a file rustc is built under, the toolchain file, `.cargo/config.toml`'s rustflags, profiles and deployment target, or the workspace manifest's profiles, a fresh passing `fold` reading exists for `sapi/cli`: the release `serve_frame` reaches no request field through an address held apart from the request's register, which LLVM's `--cgp-max-address-users-to-scan` folds for every profile (design §10 Profiles, #3372).

<!-- /computed in=5c804bb2e1bfbb4bceb727c2864da744800be082fbe75c88961b88b313911b1e out=a4760458e951634f93ac78a8903358b92341509b94698d3ab77f39cc43f2e215 -->

**Condition 1 at length.** `crosscheck`'s two columns are the `--re` reading under the crate's name and the `--ri` reading under `ext/<name>/ri`, one sensor rendering one extension twice and a touched extension held to both (#361, #273). `test` is the crate's own tests, `cargo test -p <crate>` as a reading (#191), and it is narrowed for nobody: every crate has tests, the harness included, and a crate whose tests do not run is not done. It is a reading rather than a line in the pre-commit script because every other thing this condition asks is stamped with the crate's tree, so it is fresh or it is stale and a session that changed a file after running the tests is caught; a bare `cargo test` in a hook answers only for the moment it ran. What it costs is the run itself, `cargo test -p xtask` above all, and the instrument carries the `--crate` and `--staged` narrowing the others have, so a session pays for the crate it touched and CI pays for the workspace. A crate the manifest marks `feature =` is outside that build, and unless its row names an oracle variant its corpus is the nightly lane's and `done` does not ask it for a reading no lane run writes (#133, §7). `port-map` is asked of that one crate and of no other, because it writes exactly one reading and that is the scope it writes it at (`port_map::home_of_the_lock`, design §4.4); asked of every touched crate it would hold each of the others to a reading nothing ever writes, so its `[[owed]]` row names the crate by `when = "lock-home"`, as `phpt`'s names `phpt-subset` and `witness`'s names `engine`, every crate but the harness, the one way a row narrows (#148, #3206, revision 172). That crate is the one to ask because the lock is in its tree: an unmatched row is the join's failing half, what the lock carries or does not carry is what makes a row one, and a change to `api.lock` therefore stales the reading and `done` says it a turn before the per-commit lane does. The join's other input is wider than its home and this condition does not reach it: an extension declaring its first native leaves rows resolved without touching `php_ext` (design §4.4, §11), and that number is the port's schedule and no condition of done, measured by the lane per commit like any other. Fresh means the reading's `key` matches the crate's key now (§4.2) and, for an instrument that runs under the oracle's recipe, its `recipe` matches the pin's digest now. One skip excuses what is not met, and it is the only one: the `phpt` skip the missing engine binary causes, read where no run is kept at this tree's engine (revision 136, #725), which says the engine has no binary to run the corpus through (#132). There is none until `crates/php` reaches `serve`, so while it is absent every crate that owns a subset is unmeetable, #100's twenty `ext/` crates among them. Three ways were open — build `crates/php` and `php_sapi` far enough for the corpus to run, serve a `.phpt` in process the way `witness` serves a snippet, or excuse the skip — and the third is taken, with its cost stated rather than hidden: a crate is done with its corpus unrun, which is the price of the alternative, every extension port after #100 unmeetable from its own scope. The skip is held to its cause and not to its numbers, having none worth holding, since nothing ran at all. `engine_absent` on the reading is how the lane says the binary is what stopped it and not that the subset was empty, which it alone can tell from a subset of a crate not yet created and from one whose every test skipped; `failed`, `diverged` and `borked` are zero beside it, so nothing the lane did read counted against the crate. `witness` had a rung of the same shape while the binary was why its reading was a skip, held to its numbers, something run and nothing failed or missing (#106), and has none now: the linked engine serves every snippet, `php-linked` in a process the lane starts since revision 84, so its reading is a pass or a fail on its own merits (#119). This one goes away by itself when the binary exists. A `.phpt` the compiler refuses with its own parse error, the `cannot read yet` refusal `php_compile` raises for a construct it does not read, is tallied `unread` by the lane, and since `done` holds a subset to no regression against the merge base it needs no rung of its own: a test unread here that was met at the base is a regression, and one unread at the base was met nowhere and is held to nothing, so #319's rung is retired (revision 136, #725). With no run kept at this tree's engine, the missing binary's fresh skip is the one excuse the comparison takes. A second rung is the manifest's rather than a lane's. An extension whose port is not finished carries `porting = true` on its `[[crate]]` row, and while it does, its `crosscheck` readings are the port's meter rather than a condition of done: the numbers are printed and the row is met whatever they say (#383). Its `phpt` reading was the meter too until revision 136 (#725), when the subset came to be held to no regression against the merge base whatever the key says, since a partial port no longer fails for the tests it has not reached; what follows of `phpt` is the reason it had one. Everything else a touched extension is held to stands unchanged — `clippy`, `test`, `witness`, `api-check`, `budget`, `unsafe-audit` and `vocab`, and the witness on every native it declares. The reason is the shape of those two sensors and of no others: both measure one extension against the whole of the oracle, every declaration `php -n --re <name>` renders and every `.phpt` under `ext/<name>`, so an extension whose port has forty natives of six hundred fails both from its first commit to its last, and held to their status they stop every commit that touches it however green its own work is. #325 landed `sleep()` and `array_unique()` with every reading of its own passing and could not close; #340 and #337 stood on the same line. Two other shapes were open and are refused. A regression floor, a pass count per crate that may not fall, is the honest sensor and the number moves with every port commit, so it is a manifest write per commit and `steer` refuses a changed value by construction: it puts a person in the loop of every port commit, which is the cost this rung exists to remove. Chartering the port as fog with the two rows standing leaves no sensor at all. What holds the key honest: the readings are still asked for and still have to be fresh, so a session that touches the crate runs both lanes, and `xtask status --section crosscheck` and `--section phpt` render every row with its numbers whatever the key says, under `--uncut`, and for a failing row of a `porting = true` crate without it, so a port that goes backwards is visible in the console and in CI's log; and the key names one crate, so a crate without it is held to both readings the day it is created. This rung does not go away by itself, as the binary's does: it is deleted, one extension at a time, and the day the key goes the extension is held to the oracle's bytes forever after, which is the port's completion certificate.

**Condition 2 at length.** A native is a `fn` under `#[php_function]`, or a method with a body in a `#[php_class]` impl, at any indentation and visibility; a body-less member and an `implementation =` alias are not subjects (design §8, revision 34). A subject is covered when a fence of its own, or for a method a fence on its class's header or member impl, enters it while the linked engine runs that fence (revision 58). The entry a macro expansion builds records itself on the way in, in every build, and records nothing until the witness runner switches it on for a request or a host's binary finds `PHP_WITNESS_TRACE` set (revision 85). A spelling in a fence's text covers nothing: a fence covers what it ran. A crate that declares no attribute keeps the subject count it had. A kernel is a function of a crate's `ops` module, as the manifest's witness.coverage scope names it (design §8, `php_engine::ops`), `pub` or `pub(crate)` at indentation zero, covered by a fence in its own doc block; a crate the attributes anchor is counted by its kernels beside its natives.

**Condition 7 at length.** A ticket has no tree of its own, so the reading is stamped over the workspace and is fresh while the workspace's tree hash still matches: every write after the read stales it, the ones an instrument makes included, so on a branch with a ticket `xtask ticket read` is the last act of a turn that wrote anything, one tracker call per such turn, outside the hook. `done` itself is computed from git and the store alone and reaches the tracker nowhere, the Stop hook included. What this does not catch, accepted: a body edited at the tracker while the work stands still is a green `done` until the next edit of the work. Condition 7 holds the branch's ticket to the `ticket` reading over it: met when the reading passes with every box ticked, or with `withdrawn` set, the instrument having settled the ticket either way; held while a box is unticked, while the ticket carries no box and is not withdrawn, or while the reading fails (#221). The one exception, standing from #31: a close the tracker refused with every box ticked is met, the boxes being the condition and not the close. A branch that records no ticket, and a detached `HEAD`, are not asked and nothing here says the word, the way `xtask` is not asked for a witness. In practice this is the Stop hook's and pre-push's condition: the record is the clone's own configuration, which a push does not carry, and a pull request's checkout is detached, so CI's `done` is not asked.

**Callers, three.** The Stop command hook, pre-push, and CI per commit. One instrument, one list for a checkout: the Stop hook asks it of each checkout the session's own calls wrote in, pre-push and CI of the checkout they run in; a session that wrote in none passes. A caller passes a base only where it knows one the branch cannot say: the Stop hook passes nothing and is measured since `main`, pre-push passes the remote's sha per ref, and CI passes the ref a pull request targets, its checkout being a detached merge commit on no branch, and nothing on a push. A caller also names, with `--not-run`, an instrument its run did not take: CI names the two the oracle job writes readings for, `witness` and `crosscheck`, while that job is skipped (#2477). A missing reading of one is then a note that it did not run, never unmet and never met, the head line says the pass is over what ran, and the reading counts `not_run`; a reading of it the store does hold is judged as any other.

**The reply, at the Stop alone.** With `--hook`, `done` also reads `last_assistant_message` for a session the store says closed a ticket (a `ticket` reading of a close carrying the hook's session), and holds the turn once when a sentence of it matches the `deny_pattern` of a `[[rule]]` with `tool = "Stop"`, the sentences named and a reading of `done` over the rule written. A sentence naming a ticket other than the one closed does not fire. This one is a heuristic beside the structural conditions on the close: a false positive costs one turn, since the second Stop passes and the recovery is to file the ticket and stop again; a false negative is the state before it (#32).

**The deletion test.** Delete `done` and the definition is as many sentences as the list above renders, in the map, plus a file protocol between `turn.step` and `stop-gate.sh` that checked one of them and only inside Claude Code.

### 4.6 `xtask status`: the one renderer

**Interface.** `xtask status --section <name> [--format md|json|jsonl] [--every [--log <file>|-]] [--uncut] [--tracked] [--session] [--session-id <id>]`. Its sections are the ones `xtask status --help` lists, each a rendering of the readings and facts it names. `rules` is the manifest's rules, each with its ADR number, the predicate that judges it and its reading; the text its failure prints is in `--format json` and under `--uncut`, so the map carries the rules without it. `locks` is each seam's lock with its `items`, what the tree's lock lists, counted as `api-check` counts them, beside the `api-check` reading, and `--section api-check` names it too. `--tracked` renders a section against the tree alone, as a tracked region carries it: no reading is read and a table drops its `reading` column, which could only say `no reading`, so the bytes are the same on any checkout and only a tracked file moves them (revision 6, #630, #2377); a section whose rows are this checkout's alone (`phpt`, `track`, `usage`, `permits`, `git`, `oracle`, `corpus`, `parity`, `tickets`) is refused. `--session` prints the map file and the local facts, as one block or as the JSON object `{map, facts}` whose `facts` the function hook's `prompt.context` hands over (§5), lays the local file down where it is missing or its template moved, takes the session's id from `--session-id` or the hook's stdin head, and appends this session's denials after the local facts, nothing when it has none; the `permits` section itself is rendered by name and no region of the local file, since `computed run` has no session to hand `status` (revision 180). `--session` also appends the week's ticket closes in one line, `status --section tickets`, which reads the `ticket` log of the main worktree's store, where a merge writes a close, and of the checkout's; `invocation` and `conditions` read the source of `oracle::Invocation` and of `done::Condition`, the two sections that render a type.

`budget`, `phpt`, `witness` and `mutants` render the rows a session acts on (a fail, a skip, no reading, not yet created, a budget within a tenth of its ceiling, a phpt subset whose comparison fails) and count the passing rest in one line naming `--uncut`, which renders every row; `--format json` renders every row.

It reads the manifest, the registry and the store, and what the repository and the pinned tree hold on this machine (the `git`, `oracle` and `parity` sections), only ever reading them: never the tracker or the network, never a build or a run. It computes nothing else; a section that needs a command of its own is a missing instrument, not a shell one-liner in a region.

With `--every`, a section that keeps one reading per scope renders every reading instead, in the log's order, and picks none; with `--log <file>`, or `-`, it renders a log it is handed, another store's or the published history, in place of the checkout's store. Under `--format jsonl` each row carries `v`, the version of its shape, which changes only when the shape does; a reader refuses a `v` it does not know.

A field a row may omit, whose absence a reader of the earlier shape already handles correctly, is added under the same `v`; a field removed, renamed or retyped, or a meaning changed, is a new `v`.

---

## 5. Context layer: what an agent reads first

**The map.** `AGENTS.md` at the workspace root, at most 100 lines outside its computed regions (revision 12; the figure is OpenAI's, and revision 7 raised it to 150 of the whole file only because the regions crowded the prose). What a person writes is what the ceiling holds; a region's body is rendered, and it grows because the harness does, one line of `instruments` per instrument and one of `layout` per crate. It reaches the model by the runtime's own load: `CLAUDE.md` holds `@AGENTS.md`, so a session has the map with the plugin or without it (revision 182). The function hook's `prompt.context` (`harness-plugins/`, enabled by `.claude/settings.json` as `harness@dax-php` from the repository's own marketplace) carries the local facts alone, the `facts` of `xtask status --session` with the session's id; the `SessionStart` command hook (matchers `startup`, `resume`, `clear`, `compact`) wires the clone, lays the local file down and renders it, and prints nothing. The map is named `AGENTS.md` because that is the name every runtime reads, and `CLAUDE.md` imports it rather than repeating it. Its prose items are decisions and stay prose:

1. Read `CONTEXT.md` first; the `_Avoid_` lists are binding in code, rustdoc and commit messages.
2. The design of record is `third-engine-design.md`, and `jit-design.md` for the compiled tier; the `rN` files are records and are not read for anything current.
3. `docs/adr/` is the list of reversals; read the ADR before touching what it names.
4. Definition of done: what `xtask done` checks, in one sentence each (§4.5).
5. What an agent must not do alone (§11).

Its regions, all tracked, each one command:

| Region | Command | `inputs=` |
|---|---|---|
| `design-of-record` | `ls third-engine-design*.md jit-design*.md harness-design.md \| sort -V` | the same three globs; `*.md` would take in the map itself, whose closer sums change on every render |
| `adrs` | `scripts/adr-index.sh` | `docs/adr/*.md,scripts/adr-index.sh` |
| `crates` | `xtask status --section crates --tracked` | `seams.toml,xtask/src/status.rs,xtask/src/status/graph.rs` |
| `seams` | `xtask status --section locks --format md --tracked` | `seams.toml,crates/**/*.lock,xtask/xtask.lock,xtask/src/status.rs` |
| `instruments` | `xtask --help` | `xtask/src/cli.rs` |
| `layout`, `layout-ext`, `layout-sapi` | `tree src=crates depth=1`, `tree src=ext depth=1`, `tree src=sapi depth=1` | (implied) |

Every region that runs `xtask status` passes `--tracked`, so it renders against the tree alone, with no `reading` column (§4.6). The manifest's rules are no region of the map: it names them by command, `cargo xtask status --section rules`, so a rule the manifest gains costs the map nothing (#2806).

**The local file.** `.claude/harness.local.md`, gitignored, rendered only by name (CM discovery skips dot-directories and ignored files; revision 2 A2.1). Every region is `xtask status --section <name>`, `volatile`, and there are eight: `budget`, `phpt`, `witness`, `port-map`, `divergence`, `git`, `ci`, `tickets`. `port-map` is here for the reason the first five are (#148): its reading moves with the store and not with a tracked file, and a fact renderable only by name is one a session does not read. `ci` and `tickets` are the seventh and eighth (#3619): whether CI on `main` runs and passes, and the tracker's week beside the store's closes, are standing facts that ask GitHub, so they are read where the local file renders and never at a prompt, and what GitHub cannot be asked is rendered as unknown, never as green. `bench` is not among them (#193). Its reading moves with the store as the others do, but a bench number is a claim a person signs off beside its A/A control (§4.2, §11), and one rendered in front of every session is one that gets cited without it. It is renderable by name, with `ticket`, `track`, `usage`, `bundle`, `oracle`, `permits` and `mutants`; `usage` (#210) is by name for the second reason, being an answer to what a session spent and not a fact about the tree, and so is `permits`, the judge's telemetry, whose one line a session acts on, the rules it fired itself, `--session` still appends as "permits this session" (#3620 D1). `mutants` has no instrument yet, and a section no instrument writes renders "no reading" in front of every session; it joins the others when its instrument does. That is the test for any further section, whoever proposes one: a section is in the local facts when it is a standing fact about the tree, and rendered by name when it is an answer to a question a session chose to ask. Not `inputs=../target/harness/**`: `computed` answers exit 2 to a glob that matches nothing, and a fresh clone has no store. `xtask status --session` lays the file down from its template when the clone has none, and again whenever the file, each region's body and sums taken out, differs from the template: a clone keeps its file across sessions, and laid only when missing, the main checkout's carried `mutants` and lacked `port-map` long after both moved (#3620 D1). The template is a constant, so the renderer computes nothing by writing it. The `computed trust` grant is the person's (§11). The session hook runs `computed run .claude/harness.local.md` before `status --session`, treating exit 1 as "written", exit 2 as a broken marker and any other failure as "computed did not run", each said in one line of the hook's output; it goes through `cargo run`, since once a session the rebuild is cheap and a stale built binary once answered without `--session`. That three-arm reading of `computed`'s answer is otherwise one function, behind `xtask regions <file>...`, which pre-commit, `new-ext` and `worktree add` use; the session hook's copy is the one that stays, being a person's file (revision 16, #43).

**Worktrees.** Every ticket is worked in a worktree on a branch cut from `main`, `t<n>`, laid by `xtask worktree add <branch> --ticket <n>`; `xtask track` answers what the two halves and `done` ask: the branch, the base, the main checkout whose `target/harness` is the one store, and the ticket the branch records. `add` cuts the branch from `main` (`origin`'s where the repository has only that), lays the worktree beside the checkout, at `<parent>/<checkout's name>-worktrees/<branch>`, which for main's checkout `~/Projects/dax-php` is `~/Projects/dax-php-worktrees/<branch>`, and never under `.claude/`, whose files are the harness's own (revision 56). Where one goes is `worktree::home`'s alone, and only `add` asks it: a worktree that exists is found through git's own record, `git worktree list` for `retire`, `track` and the console and its `.git` for `permit`, which judges a call in any checkout of the repository against that checkout's manifest, so a worktree laid under `.claude/worktrees/` before revision 56 is judged and retired as any other. `add` then makes three facts about it measured rather than guessed: it builds `xtask` there, so an `Edit` from the first tool call is judged by a built binary and not by a cold `cargo run` inside the hook's timeout (§12 item 8); it lays `.claude/harness.local.md` down through that build and renders it through `xtask regions`; and a grant covers every repository under the granted path (#74, §11), so the one that covers the worktrees is the grant over `<checkout's name>-worktrees`, made once per checkout: where `computed` answers untrusted, `add` prints that line as a person's and waits on none. The worktree's `target/` is its own, and `add` seeds it from the clone's `target/debug` with `cp -c`, a clone-on-write copy where the volume supports one: the registry dependencies are then blocks the two directories share and cargo rebuilds only the workspace's own crates, measured at 10 s of seeding against a cold dependency build, with `xtask` then built in 7.6 s. What it must not do is give two worktrees one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: cargo keys a workspace member's artifact by its path under the workspace root and judges the slot fresh by mtime, so two worktrees share one slot per crate and a checkout whose files predate another worktree's build runs that worktree's binary — measured on cargo 1.95.0, `cargo run` in one worktree executing the other's code with the other's `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` baked in (#251). The harness rests on the opposite: `built_root` resolves `--root` to the workspace the binary runs in, the nearest `[workspace]` above its working directory, and `built::judges` holds the build to it, the digest of `xtask/src` compiled into the binary against that workspace's and the manifest's against the calling checkout's, so a binary cargo reused from another checkout is stale wherever their sources differ and never judges as this one's (#2719). Seeding is best-effort; where `cp -c` cannot, the worktree starts cold and nothing else changes (revision 40). What the clone already carries it reports rather than sets again: `core.hooksPath` is repository config, `.claude/settings.json` and `oracle.toml` are tracked, and the php-src tree the pin names is found through the oracle module (§4.3): `oracle.toml`'s `[source].path` is where the tree is on the maintainer's machine and stays so, and a machine that is not the maintainer's, a worktree on another box or the runner, sets `TEST_PHP_SRCDIR` to a checkout at the pin's tag, which `Source::tree()` reads first and holds to `[source].commit`. A branch the repository or `origin` has, and a directory that exists, are refusals that change nothing, as `new-ext` is. `xtask worktree retire <branch>` is the other half, and it is where the checkout keeps its evidence: a worktree's readings live in its own `target/harness/` and removing the worktree removes them, so the worktree's log is landed in the main checkout's store, the one `xtask track` names, each reading in its log and its index (revision 83), and only then is the worktree removed, which keeps a merged agent's denials in the `permits` section the console reads (§4.2). The scope's files beside the log stay with the worktree: each is a scope's latest run over a tree `main` does not have, and copying one in would hand `done` a reading that is fresh for nothing (#35). The branch is kept; deleting it is a person's line, printed. Merges into `main` go through one more worktree, the merge queue's (revision 183): `xtask worktree queue` lays `_merge` down beside the others on the branch `staging`, owned by the calling session and seeded from main's `debug`, `release` and `release-da` builds, and `xtask worktree land` asks `done` there since main's tip, lands its log in the main checkout's store as `retire` does while the worktree stays, and moves `main` only by a fast-forward to the commit `done` judged, refusing with `main` untouched where `done` is unmet. Both halves are held by `cargo test -p xtask --test worktree` against a scratch repository whose `xtask` is this one, built at its own HEAD.

**A track's plan.** What a track works next is the tracker's and no session's. `xtask track next` names it: of the open tickets labelled `ready-for-agent`, those no open ticket blocks, that are the track's (carry its `track:` label, or, for `main`, no `track:` label at all), and that no branch of the repository records, the widest by the census, the number breaking a tie (#618); `xtask track work --track <name> --model <m>` lays a worktree down per ticket and runs the frontier until none is named. A ticket closes when its work is on `main` (revision 98): a session ends at `xtask ticket ready <n>`, which holds the boxes and the successors as a close does and records them on the branch, `branch.<name>.ready-<n>`, one record per ticket so a branch carrying several keeps each (#2358); the branch is left for /specs to merge (#1749), and the merge commit's hook closes the ticket. `xtask track chains` prints the same open tickets whole, each under the one that blocks it, with its label, its branch and what it waits on on every line, so a session sees the frontier and what stands behind it in one call rather than a walk over `blockedBy`; it decides nothing and writes no reading (revision 26). What fills that frontier is the track's chart on the tracker, one issue labelled `frontier:chart` whose open tickets are its sub-issues. It is not the map: the map is `AGENTS.md`, which the head of this section names, and the two were one word until `CONTEXT.md` settled them apart (revision 22); one is where a session starts, the other where the track is going. The `/frontier` skill is the only thing that writes it: `chart <track> <days>` lays it down at one row of design §11's table, Destination from the row's exit bar, the work not yet sharp enough to ticket sketched as fog, and files the first batch; `work <track>` files the next batch when the frontier empties, indexing each close since the last batch into the route walked and graduating the fog those closes made sharp. The chart indexes and never restates, the detail staying in the ticket, so a session loads it cold in one read. A batch is three to five tickets, each one concern of the row sized to one session, and a ticket is of one of three kinds: a build, a fact outside the tree that a decision waits on (`frontier:research`), or a question the design of record does not make (`frontier:decision`). The kind is a label, and the label is the one thing `track work` changes between tickets, since it chooses the skill the session starts on: `/build <n>`, `/research <n>`, `/grill-design <n>` (#81, revision 181). With `--fill` an empty frontier starts a `/frontier` session; without it the run ends there. Planning measures nothing and decides nothing: the row's exit bar is read from the instruments, the next row is charted by a person, and a question the design is silent on is a person's (§11).

**Per-directory guidance.** `ext/AGENTS.md` with the extension template rules (one dependency, `extension()`, state on the value, no locks, tests through `Capture`, witnesses in rustdoc, phpt from every `.phpt` under `php-src/ext/<name>`, as run-tests.php walks the directory, revision 67) and two regions, `extensions` (`tree src=. depth=1 dirs`; `ls -d */` fails on an empty `ext/`) and `bundle-order` (`xtask status --section bundle --tracked`). `xtask new-ext <name>` lays the topology down. What the template is exercised on is the fixture at `xtask/fixtures/new-ext`, whose own `seams.toml` carries the `[[crate]]` rows for `ext/demo` and `ext/other`: `cargo test -p xtask --test new_ext` lays `ext/demo` down there and holds it to `budget`, `unsafe-audit` and `api-check` untouched, so the shipped manifest gains no row for a name nobody ships and design §4.1's table stays the twenty ported extensions. The fixture's facade is empty by its own line, so the generated `ext/<name>/tests/capture.rs` is held there as text; its execution is held where a real extension stands instead, `cargo test -p php_ext_<name> --test capture` over the twenty #100 laid down. The topology and the three instruments are therefore held in isolation and the capture test is not, which is what `new-ext`'s rustdoc says too.

**The glossary as a sensor.** `xtask vocab` checks public item names and rustdoc prose in `crates/*` and `ext/*` against the `_Avoid_` lists, scoped so that `Result` is not `result`. A lock is not read: its lines mirror php's own names (`sapi_name`, `set_exception_handler`), which the engine copies on purpose. It writes a reading like any instrument.

**What else is computed.** `data/INDEX.md` gains a `files` region. Design documents carry no numbers of their own: they cite `seams.toml` and `oracle.toml` (§14), and a fact of theirs that changes is a region, rendered from the manifest, the tree or the code, as `harness-design.md`'s and `third-engine-design.md`'s are.

---

## 6. Constraint layer: what is mechanically forbidden

The constraint table of revision 1 stands, with its column of where each rule now lives:

| Constraint | Mechanism | Cadence | Declared in |
|---|---|---|---|
| An extension cannot reach the engine | crate visibility (`ext/*` depends on `php_ext` only) | compile | `[[edge]]` `ext/* → php_ext only`, ADR-0004 |
| A native cannot re-enter without saying so | `&mut Request`: running PHP code needs one, as a parameter or in a value whose lifetime names it | compile | design principle 9, ADR-0002 |
| Shared structures cannot hold per-request values | `MapPtr<T>` newtype | compile | design r2 §5.14 |
| An extension cannot hold a lock | generated `ext/clippy.toml`; `permit` at the edit | at the tool call, on save, per commit | `ext.no-locks`, ADR-0005 |
| A process forks through one home, and every engine thread is counted | generated `disallowed-methods` in each clippy.toml; `#[expect(clippy::disallowed_methods)]` at the home | on save, per commit | `fork.threads`, ADR-0010 |
| No `unsafe` outside the homes `unsafe.homes` names | `forbid(unsafe_code)` at crate roots; `unsafe-audit`; `permit` | at the tool call, compile, per commit | `unsafe.homes` |
| No panic reaches a request | panic family denied; `catch_unwind` at the boundary | on save, per commit | lints (unchanged) |
| No interface growth without a lock line | `api-check`; `permit` note | at the tool call, per commit | `[[seam]]`, `api.lock-line` |
| Only a macro expansion names `api::__private` | a `deny_pattern` over `ext/*` sources; `permit` at the edit | at the tool call, per commit | a manifest rule a person adds (design revision 34) |
| No hand-authored exit status in a witness | `witness --accept` the only writer; `permit` | at the tool call, pre-commit | `witness.exit-authored` |
| No hand-authored stream in a fence marked `capture` | `witness --accept --streams` the only writer; `permit` | at the tool call, pre-commit | `witness.capture-authored` |
| No second registry | bundle features checked against requirements | per commit | `[[edge]]` `php_bundle`, `order = "registration"` |
| No committed generated code, except the generated config | `budget` path check; each generated clippy.toml regenerated and diffed | per commit | `[[crate]]` |
| No process weight | `docs/**` and the three living documents under `[budget] docs`; `.claude/**` and `harness-plugins/**` hand-written under `[budget] harness`, less the desk's `harness-plugins/desk/**`, tests included, under `[budget] harness-desk` | per commit | `[budget]` |
| An agent does not touch the harness's own configuration | `permit` | at the tool call | `harness.self`, `harness.self-shell`, `harness.transcript` |

The strongest rows are still the ones with no path to take. Every row below them has a twin: a rule `permit` evaluates before the write has a lint or an `xtask` check after it, so no function hook is ever the only sensor for a constraint (revision 2 C.8 item 8), and the column says which.

**Wiring.** Pre-commit runs `cargo fmt --check` first, and the instruments that only read the tree at once, a refusal among them ending the hook before anything is built (#1252). `xtask regions` renders the region files by name and says which it wrote so the hook re-stages them (revision 16), the gitignored `.claude/harness.local.md` not among them (#1312). Under `--staged`, which pre-commit passes, it first refuses a region the commit would carry, one it would write or one of a file the commit stages, that reads a path whose working copy the commit does not stage, since a region rendered from the working tree would carry that change (#2236). The region files are six, `AGENTS.md`, `ext/AGENTS.md`, `data/INDEX.md`, `docs/divergence.md`, `harness-design.md` and `third-engine-design.md`, in pre-commit's list and CI's `computed check` alike, and `computed check` refuses a hand-edited region; both run before anything that reads the map, so xtask's map tests read from the index the map the commit carries (#1416). Witness is not run here: `done` asks a branch the witness of each crate it touched once, before it is ready, and the merge check's `witness --unmeasured` measures a merge (revision 153); crosscheck is not run, and `done` asks a touched extension for its row (revision 140). Before anything runs, a `git commit -F` whose file is missing is refused, and a commit in a linked worktree runs at `nice` 10 (revision 140). The sequence is the hook's own, rendered from it, the render and the check among its lines (revisions 64, 66):

<!-- computed exec cmd="grep -E '^(step|spawn|gather)( |$)|^if (rendered=|! computed check)' scripts/hooks/pre-commit" inputs=scripts/hooks/pre-commit name=pre-commit as=fence | do not edit; run computed -->
```
step cargo fmt --check
spawn $X api-check --staged
spawn $X budget --staged
spawn $X unsafe-audit --staged
spawn $X vocab --staged
spawn $X permit --diff
gather
if rendered=$($X regions --staged $regions); then
if ! computed check $regions >/dev/null 2>&1; then
step $X clippy --staged
step $X test --staged
```
<!-- /computed in=aae374e3ba25329d0ec7b14b840c950af04829dbf8f49ba513a86498aadc5825 out=2e3abed831cc5964ea5020bffd271112cdac94dc26e003c21a1fa988dd83b0a7 -->

Pre-push: `xtask done`. Post-commit, on a merge commit, and post-merge: `xtask ticket merged`, which closes each open ticket the merge commits name as `#n` with the successors its ready record names, one record per ticket of a branch (#2358), and closes nothing off `main` (revision 98). The hooks are held by `cargo test -p xtask --test hooks`, which runs them in a scratch repository with a stand-in `cargo` (#52). The plugin:

```
.claude/
  harness.local.md                    gitignored; §5's local regions
  settings.json                       enables harness@dax-php and desk@dax-php from the repository's marketplace; the function-hook flag
.claude-plugin/marketplace.json       the repository as a marketplace of two plugins
harness-plugins/
  harness/.claude-plugin/plugin.json  name, version, hooks; a person's file
  harness/hooks/hooks.json            PreToolUse → xtask permit; Stop → xtask done --hook; SessionStart wires the clone and renders the local file; `modules` names the function-hook module; a person's file
  harness/hooks/harness.ts            the function-hook module: permit before the command hook, the map through prompt.context, the refresh after an instrument ran
  desk/.claude-plugin/plugin.json     the desk, the mods that draw what the harness keeps; a person's file
  desk/hooks/hooks.json               `modules` names the desk's module; a person's file
  desk/hooks/desk.tsx                 the desk's module: the for-a-person queue, /decide's pane, the counsel and the ruling post (#3496, #3831)
  types/                              the declarations Claude Code ships, copied in by pre-commit; tracked, shared by both plugins
  tsconfig.json                       against the declarations in types/, over every plugin's hooks/; tracked, so the test job typechecks the modules
```

No `rules.ts`, no `scripts/`. The command hooks and the function-hook module are one plugin, `harness@dax-php`, and the desk a second, `desk@dax-php`, both loaded from the repository's own marketplace by `.claude/settings.json`; the desk is its own plugin so the judging module stays small and the desk has its own switch, and it shares the hooks worker, so three crashes still unload both (#3490). Every plugin's `.claude-plugin/` and `hooks/` are a person's files under `harness.self`, as `harness-plugins/*/` names them, so a later plugin is covered with no edit to the rule. The module was outside it while a function hook only failed open beside a command hook and was never the only sensor (revision 9); since it hands `permit` the evidence of the person's approval, a module edited to forge that evidence would get past its own layer, so it is guarded as `hooks.json` is (#2283). The declarations are generated and tracked: pre-commit copies the ones Claude Code ships into `harness-plugins/types`, and the type-check fails loudly when they are absent (#62, #1736).
