## 7. Feedback layer: the sensors and when they run

| Sensor | at the tool call | on save | pre-commit | per commit | nightly | Reading's scope |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `permit` | yes | | over the diff | over the diff | | rule id |
| type check, clippy | | crate | touched crates | workspace | | crate |
| `test` | | crate | touched crates | workspace | | crate |
| witnesses | | crate, on request | touched crates | all crates | oracle drift | crate |
| phpt | | `--subset` on request | | `Zend/tests` + `tests/` + `sapi/cli` + default extensions' subsets, 4 min | 21.5k; `run-tests.php -p` control | subset |
| compile identity | | file, on request | | 6.5k files | 172k | corpus |
| crosscheck (`php -n --re <name>` and `php -n --ri <name>` renderings against the oracle's bytes, design §7.1) | | `--ext` on request | | default extensions, both columns | all features, both columns | extension, one reading per column |
| `fpm-compare` (makala against the pinned php-fpm over the HTTP-served subset, #2349) | | on request, where the pinned php-fpm and php-src are | | | | `sapi/makala` |
| `soak` (many clients against one makala, each response held to the request served alone, #2350) | | the 30-second smoke, a bare call | | the 30-second smoke, `sapi/makala/tests/soak.rs` in makala's `test` | `--nightly`: ten minutes, 64 clients, over the HTTP-served subset and the bench workloads, against a debug build, `release-da` | `sapi/makala` |
| `api-check` | | | yes | yes | | seam |
| `budget`, `unsafe-audit` | | | yes | yes | | crate |
| Miri (`php_heap`), `cargo careful` | | | | Miri | careful | crate |
| fuzz | | | | libfuzzer smoke | differential | target |
| `bench` | | | | G1 from the day `tier` exists, the tier bound and `opcache.jit_buffer_size=0`; `Cache` hit cost; parse throughput | full protocol | gate or bar |
| `phpt --jit force`, `--jit exit-fuzz` | | | | every witness (G5) | `Zend/tests` + `tests/` | subset |
| threaded dispatch (`cfg(dispatch = "tailcall")` on the dated nightly `scripts/ci/tailcall.sh` pins, #2970) | | | | `cargo +nightly-<date> check -p php_engine --config "target.'cfg(all())'.rustflags=['--cfg', 'dispatch=\"tailcall\"']"` | `tests` and `Zend/tests` with `--engine` on its `release-da` build | per commit, none: the job's exit; nightly, subset, in a store of its own |
| trace-dump diff (`jit-design.md` §4.1) | | `--kernel` or `--program` on request | | seven kernels under `jit.compile=inline` | | kernel |
| `fold` (`serve_frame`'s accesses to the request, #3372) | | on request | | a diff that changes `rust-toolchain.toml` or `.cargo/config.toml` (§4.5 condition 10) | | crate (`sapi/cli`) |
| `port-map` | | | | unresolved rows | | crate |
| mutants | | | | `--in-diff`, reported not gated | `php_heap` full | crate |
| leak gate | | | | in tests | | state type |
| `vocab` | | | yes | yes | | crate |
| `done` | | | | yes | | workspace |

**What CI's corpus covers now** (revision 17). The test job has the corpus: php-src checked out sparse and shallow at the pin's tag, by the patterns `xtask status --section corpus` renders from `phpt::ROOTS` (`Zend/tests`, `tests`, every `sapi/*/tests` and every `ext/<name>` whole, the directories the nightly lane walks and every `[[crate]]` row's subset stands under), cached by the pinned commit and a digest of those patterns, pointed at by `TEST_PHP_SRCDIR`, refused when its `HEAD` is not `[source].commit` (#37) and when it holds fewer `.phpt` files than `[source].phpt_files` counts (#1713). The job promises the tree and not the binary: the pinned php is a macOS keg the ubuntu runner cannot have, so the tests that spawn it skip there, stated, and the witness lane's readings are the maintainer's machine's (§13 D.20). What runs against that corpus per commit today is the runner control, `ext/pcre@control` under the pinned oracle where a machine has it, and the subset resolution of every corpus lane; the phpt rows above stand as the plan for the day the engine executes a script, and the row changes when a lane does.

**What the workspace's `test` row holds, and what it cannot** (revision 49, #389). The row runs and always has: CI's `xtask` job runs `cargo test -p xtask` and the per-commit job runs `cargo xtask test` over the workspace, on every commit pushed. What it cannot reach is a test that returns for want of the pinned binary. The runner promises the tree and not the binary, as the paragraph above says, so every test that spawns the oracle returns at its first line there and the readings those tests hold are the maintainer's machine's alone; an oracle the runner can have is a person's, and #424 is where that decision is filed. Until it lands, a test of the harness may not assert as an equality a number another crate's commits move: it goes red on one machine at that crate's commit, with no lane to say so, and is paid for by whichever session next touches `xtask`. What such a test asserts instead is read off the reading it has just taken — the counts, the status they imply and the message from the counts — and a **floor** stands beside it, a constant an advance never moves and a regression falls through, raised by hand beside a commit that wants it raised or not at all. The two that carried the pinning shape are `xtask/tests/compile_identity.rs`, whose `REACHED` is the files of the fixture corpus the linked compiler has come to, and `xtask/tests/crosscheck.rs`, whose `FLOOR` is the scopes whose rendering is the oracle's; each carries the negative control that shows its floor firing. This says nothing about `done`, which reads a crate's own readings and not the harness's tests, and nothing about a regression floor on a port, which §4.5 condition 1 refuses for a reason that stands: that one is a manifest value moving per commit, and these are a constant in the harness's own tree, moved by the session that touches it or never. What the row's reading does say is how many tests returned (#3085): a test that skips for a fact the machine did not promise, through `Promise::or_skip` or, beyond xtask, `crates/php_compile/tests/support/skip.rs`, appends its name to the file `XTASK_SKIPPED` names, one per test executable, and `skipped_unpromised` joins the reading's `passed`, `failed`, `ignored` and `filtered` as the count of the distinct tests that did, its message saying it and `done` noting it beside a passing reading.

**Which subsets a lane runs has no home yet.** The per-commit row above names its four groups in this table and nowhere a command can read, so `xtask phpt` names no lane of its own: it runs the subset it is given, or every subset of the corpus under the whole-corpus flag, and a bare call says so rather than keeping a second copy of this row. The manifest is where the row belongs, and until it has one the lane is composed by whoever calls the instrument (revision 12).

**What the default build is, and where it is stated.** The per-commit row's default extensions are the design of record's: enable and disable live on `php_bundle` alone, one feature per non-default extension, and the default features match php-src's default `./configure` set as far as ported (design §4.10, and §8's `ext/<name>` row, per commit for the default extensions and nightly for the rest). At the pinned tag that set leaves four of the twenty out — `ext/mbstring`, `ext/pdo_mysql`, `ext/openssl` and `ext/curl`, each an `--enable` or `--with` whose `config.m4` gives no default — and those four are the ones `seams.toml` marks `feature =`. So the manifest's `feature` rows are the statement the lane filters on and `done` asks by (§4.5 condition 1), and they are the one the harness reads: widening the lane to every crate the bundle builds was the other way through #133's friction and is refused here, since it would run per commit what the design schedules nightly. `php_bundle`'s `default` names those sixteen and not the four (#159), so the two files name one default build and a reader is not choosing between them: the four are features a command line turns on and no build starts with, and the bundle's own comment says so. `new-ext` still lists every crate it lays down in `default`, and the pruning stays the person's act that comment reserves, done once per non-default extension as it is ported (design §4.10); so a crate laid down after this reads wider until that act, and what the harness reads is the manifest throughout. The witness lane does not stand on the rows (revision 43, #314): the linked engine the harness builds carries every extension feature, so a `feature =` crate's witnesses read pass or fail on their merits under the linked runner, and the rows filter the binary and the phpt lane alone. It resolves no binary either (revision 48, #401): every snippet is served by the linked engine whether or not `target/*/php` stands, since that binary is the default build and a fence held to whichever rung happened to run reads pass or fail by the tree's state and not by the code beside it. The one exception is a fence marked `process` (revision 61, #1062): pcntl's, posix's that change the process, and any other whose snippet forks, sends or takes a signal, or sets the process's ids, session or limits, is served by the `php` binary, which the lane builds itself, so the fence reads pass or fail by the code beside it and never by which binary stood in the tree. The linked engine is `php-linked` since revision 84: `sapi/cli` built with the bundle's `linked` set, every extension feature, `pcntl` among them since the CLI carries it, in a process of its own, so a snippet that forks forks it and never the harness.

**What the per-commit lane measures** (revision 63, #1261). The per-commit phpt lane measures every extension the binary under test carries, not the bundle's default set alone: the rows whose `feature` the `php` binary turns on (openssl, bcmath, calendar, pcntl, posix, sockets, bz2 at this revision; under ADR-0008 the features `sapi/cli` turns on), beside the rows with no `feature`. The binary the lane runs is that build, the oracle is built with those extensions, and parity is every test the oracle runs (third-engine-design.md §1, #1161); an extension the binary serves and the lane never measures is a gap the dashboard reads as nothing. A `feature` row the binary does not turn on stays out of the lane and in the nightly, unless the row names an oracle variant: the variant is the statement that php loads what the feature builds, so the lane runs that subset on the engine the harness builds with the row's feature in a target directory of its own, where the variant's php is on the machine (#2029). The paragraph above still says which features are php's default `./configure` and so which `php_bundle` starts with; it no longer says what the lane runs.

**Which build a correctness lane runs** (revision 82, #1994). Every correctness lane (witness, the per-commit phpt lane, Zend/tests, `tests/*` and the nightly full run) runs the `release-da` profile: release with debug assertions and overflow checks, without thin LTO or a single codegen unit. The bench lane and the shipped binaries are plain release. The profile is `Cargo.toml`'s `[profile.release-da]`, and the harness names it once, in `xtask/src/oracle/profile.rs`: `phpt` looks for the engine's binary under that profile's directory first, then release's, then debug's, passing over one the tree has moved past as it passes over any (#475), and the cargo line a stale binary's skip names builds it; the witness lane serves its fences through `php-linked` and builds a `process` fence's host. Every binary the harness builds, `php-linked` and the hosts' own, is built in the lanes' profile, whatever profile the harness itself was built in (revision 92). CI's oracle job builds the harness, the hosts and the tree's test in it, and runs the nightly lane through that harness too.

**A `--ri` row that names a linked library's version** (revision 89, #624). A `--ri` row whose value is the version of a library the engine links is met by construction. The engine links, or vendors, the version of that library the pinned keg links, as #717 ruled for PCRE2 10.48, and an oracle bump moves those versions with it. No registry key per row, and no `porting` key held for this reason. Each library whose version differs from the keg's when its extension first renders such a row is a build ticket of its own, filed as it is reached.

**Which build a port renders** (revision 103, #635). A port's `--re` and `--ri` renderings are held to the pinned keg's bytes, the build the pin names and CI's oracle job pours on the `dax-oracle` runner (#809, #2253); no library version or driver list is normalised, and a row that names a linked library's version is met by linking that version (#624). So `xtask crosscheck`'s floor rises onto one of the six extensions whose columns differ between a macOS and a Linux build (`ext/standard`, `ext/pcre`, `ext/pdo`, `ext/pdo_sqlite`, `ext/dom`, `ext/iconv`; measured on #424) as it rises onto any other: when the engine's rendering is the keg's bytes.

**An extension whose port is partial.** The `phpt` and `crosscheck` rows above run per commit for every default extension whatever its port has reached, and both measure one extension against the whole of the oracle. An extension whose `[[crate]]` row carries `porting = true` is run by both lanes at the same cadence and rendered with its numbers by `xtask status`, and what the key changes is `done` alone, which reads its `crosscheck` readings as the port's meter and not as a condition while the key stands (§4.5 condition 1, #383); its `phpt` subset is held to no regression against the merge base whatever the key says (#725). So the two manifest keys the lanes are read through say different things and neither is a copy of the other: `feature =` says which extensions a lane reaches, and `porting` says what the readings of one it reaches bind. The key is one crate's, added by a person and deleted by a person; a crate that carries neither is run per commit and held to what the run says.

**What the agent sees.** The tool result, as before; and beside it the rendering. `permit`'s note or denial arrives as the hook's `additionalContext` or `permissionDecisionReason`, with the ADR number from the manifest. The map's numbers are `status` renderings, refreshed by the session hook, by the function hook's invalidation when enabled, and by the next `computed run` on the local file otherwise. `done`'s list is the Stop hook's `reason`. A person sees the same renderings in the terminal and, per CI run, the uploaded `target/harness/`.

**Sensor telemetry.** The CI summary is `xtask status --section permits` and a query over the log: readings per instrument, failures per instrument, and the instruments with no failure in fourteen days, which are the candidates for "silent" (HE, open questions; Sensors, appendix). The negative-control fixtures of §4.1 are what separates silent from respected for the rule sensors; for the phpt runner it is the nightly control; for `permit` it is a fixture tool call per rule.

**The threaded dispatch's lane** (revision 150, #2970). The `match` loop on the pinned stable toolchain is the shipped dispatch (design §5.8); `cfg(dispatch = "tailcall")` (#2905) is live code that no build ships, and the lane in the table holds it: without one it would stop compiling, or stop holding the corpus, with no reading going red. `<date>` is a dated nightly pinned in one place the lane reads, `scripts/ci/tailcall.sh`'s `TOOLCHAIN` line, which both workflows run and neither spells, so moving the date is a one-line change. A nightly that breaks the check is moved past, not followed. The lane builds in a target directory of its own, so the cfg never invalidates the shipped build's. The per-commit check writes no reading and is harness.yml's `tailcall` job, whose failure is the run's; the nightly subsets are dashboard.yml's `tailcall` job, held to the targets the oracle job keeps, their store uploaded apart from the stores `publish` folds and the runs `phpt` keeps put back as they stood, so no reading or kept run of the threaded build is read as the shipped engine's.

**Where a red nightly is read** (revision 155, #2980). Two nightly jobs draw nothing on the site: dashboard.yml's `soak`, whose store `publish` does not fold, and its `tailcall`, whose store is kept apart so that no reading of the threaded build is read as the shipped engine's. A red nightly of either is read on the tracker. dashboard.yml's `red-nightly` job runs `scripts/ci/red.sh nightly <job>` for each, under the rule harness.yml's `red-main` holds a red `main` to (#1156): two red nightlies of the job in a row file one `ci:red-soak` or `ci:red-tailcall` ticket, a third comments on it, and the first green after them comments that the job is green again and closes nothing. A job that ran out of its time is red, a soak that hung among them; a nightly in which the job was skipped is passed over.

**The trace-dump diff** (#2953). `xtask trace-dump` runs each of the seven kernels under the recipe and `[regime.jit-inline]`, by the oracle and by `php` built with the tier's feature, the build the phpt lane's `--jit` and the witness lane's jit fences run, with the kernels' memory limit, `jit.mode=force` and `jit.compile=inline` after the regime on both sides, two directives php ignores. It holds what the regime's `opcache.jit_debug` prints, as the regime pins it and with no mask of the instrument's own (#3155's ruling): a program's `---- TRACE` lines, a root's or a side trace's start, its stop or abort, and compiled or blacklisted; its `TRACE n exit m` lines, which php writes on each pass through an exit that has no side trace yet; and its `---- EXIT m/k blacklisted` lines, an exit blacklisted where its side's recording aborted past its limit or where it grows no side and is spent. They are held through `oracle::compare` under the registry key `trace:dump/<kernel>`, and both sides' lines are kept in the store under the kernel and the pin's digest, as `crosscheck` keeps the oracle's bytes. So an exit the engine places where php places none, revision 26's division guard among them (`jit-design.md` §4.2), is a difference the diff shows, and one the design chose is a by-design `trace` row a person accepts rather than a line the instrument hides, pinning both sides' kept lines by digest (§4.3), so a pin bump or an engine change that moves either is a difference nobody ruled on. One reading per kernel, scoped by its name and stamped over the workspace; `xtask/fixtures/trace-dump` holds the control, engine lines a line apart from the kept oracle lines, which fails it.

**The fold sensor** (#3384). Every profile passes LLVM's `--cgp-max-address-users-to-scan` at the value design §10 Profiles names (#3372), and an LLVM that keeps the option's name and changes its meaning builds, runs and passes every corpus, slower. `xtask fold` builds the release `php`, disassembles each instantiation of `php_engine::tier::serve_frame` and fails where one reaches a request field through an address held apart from the request's register, `add xN, <request>, #C` and then a load or store of one register through `xN` at an offset `[<request>, #C + offset]` could encode: the shape #3345 found, matched by its form and never by an offset. The request's register is the one the prologue copies `x0` into. A field whose address is also handed to a call escapes, and no limit of CodeGenPrepare's folds it, so its accesses are counted as `escaped`, named with the call, and not held: at e61851ebe that is the frame table's first word, which `Frames::grow` is handed, reached the same way with the option at the ruled value and a hundred times it, until #3345's change lands. `done` asks for a fresh passing reading at `sapi/cli` on a diff that changes the toolchain file or `.cargo/config.toml`, and the harness keys a crate's readings over the toolchain file and that file's codegen tables (`key::BUILT_UNDER`), so one taken before such a change is stale. An `aarch64` Mac alone, whose `objdump` is LLVM's; the reading is a skip elsewhere. Its negative controls are `xtask/fixtures/fold/unfolded.s`, an excerpt of #3345's build without the option, and `main-unfolded.s`, of e61851ebe's, beside `folded.s`, this ticket's build with it. CI's `done` names `fold` `--not-run`: its runners are no aarch64 Mac, and the reading is taken where one is.

**Mutation testing and concurrency** stand as revision 1 had them: `cargo mutants --in-diff` per commit on `php_engine` kernels and `php_compile`, reported; full nightly on `php_heap`; `Cache` under Miri's data-race detection on a `std::thread` test, with loom priced as revision 1 D.4 says.

---

## 8. The behaviour harness

Unchanged in substance from revision 1. The behaviour specification is the oracle, the test bodies are php-src's, and a witness is an approved scenario (Approved) whose approval is the oracle's bytes, "captured and never authored" (r4 §8). What the harness protects is the choice of snippet: the `port-map` join says the minimum each native's witness must cover, mutation on the diff says whether it pins the branches touched, and human review of witness diffs is the inferential sensor. Revision 3 adds one thing: the registry. A witness that differs from the oracle is a `Differs` verdict until a person writes the row, and `done` will not pass while the row is `open`. Revision 48 adds what serves one: the engine linked into the harness, in this process, and never the engine's own binary (§7), so a witness is a claim about the kernel or native beside it and never about a build's enabled set. Revision 84 moves that engine out of the harness's process and keeps the rest: `php-linked`, `sapi/cli` built with the bundle's `linked` set, serves each snippet with a fresh engine as the harness once did, started by the lane and handed one snippet after another, so the harness links no engine and an edit under `crates/` or `ext/` rebuilds that binary and never the one every hook runs. A snippet that ends the binary is that witness's failure alone, and the next is served by a binary started again.

Revision 50 adds the one case where the oracle cannot be made to run the snippet at all. A branch whose state no PHP snippet can build — not one that is hard to write, but one the language cannot express here — is held by a unit test over the copied algorithm, and the box that asks for a witness is ticked by that test, under three conditions. The commit says in its own words why no snippet reaches the branch: which construct the compiler does not read, or which release php does not make. The test holds php's algorithm and not the engine's convenience: the text, the code and the order php's own source produces, cited by file and line at the pinned tag. And the fence is owed as soon as the branch is reachable, the ticket that makes it reachable named in the commit where one exists and filed where none does. `witness.coverage` is untouched: the kernel or native carries its fence wherever the oracle can run one, and this is the narrow case where it cannot. The one instance today is `zend_objects_destroy_object`'s `Attempt to destruct pending exception` (`Z/Zend/zend_objects.c:157`), held by `api::request::tests::a_destructor_served_on_the_pending_throwable_is_the_core_fatal`; what the third condition means where php's own algorithm is what keeps the branch out of reach, rather than this engine's compiler, is #458.

Revision 55 adds what a witness runs under. A witness is approved against the pin's `[recipe]`, and that does not change: the recipe is what a fence stands on when it names nothing, and its authority is that a person wrote it. What a fence may do beside that is name one `[regime.<name>]` of `oracle.toml` — the same block, in the same order, that `compile-identity` names as `dump` and the tier lanes name as `jit-tracing`, `jit-off` and `jit-inline` — whose ini stands after the recipe's for the oracle run and for `php-linked` alike, and whose args stand after the recipe's on a binary's command line; `php-linked` has none and takes `-e` as a host does, building with extended info (#1822), so a fence under a regime of any other argument is marked `process` (#1780, #1887). The reason is that a behaviour whose entry the recipe pins away is otherwise outside what a witness can pin at all: the recipe passes `short_open_tag=0` and php registers that entry `PHP_INI_SYSTEM|PHP_INI_PERDIR` (`Z/main/main.c:820`), so no snippet asks the oracle what `<?` opens while it is on, and `short_open_tag` is only the first such entry to be found. A regime is a named, reviewed, reusable row of the pin, so the fence carries the name and the pin carries the values: a fence that would set an ini of its own is refused, since it would let a fence approve itself against a pin nobody wrote. A name the pin does not carry is read off the pin before anything runs, and the block is one the lane cannot run, named beside the regimes the pin has.

Revision 87 adds a fence's locale (#1701, #1678). An `expect` line may name `locale=<name>`, repeatable, for a snippet that needs a locale. Before the fence runs, the oracle and then the engine each try `setlocale(LC_ALL, <name>)`; where neither host takes any named locale, the witness is a `skip` naming it, counted as skipped in the crate's reading and never a pass or a fail, as a `.phpt`'s `--SKIPIF--` dies on `!setlocale(…)`; where either host takes one, the fence runs and is held as any other.

Revision 106 adds what writes a fence's streams (#801). A fence's streams may be captured as its status is: `xtask witness --accept --streams` writes the oracle's stdout and stderr, after the recipe's normalisation, into each fence that asks for it; every other fence's body is its author's and the oracle judges it, so `--accept` leaves it as its author wrote it and `--accept --bodies` rewrites it only where the oracle does not meet it (revision 171); a value that differs between machines is a wildcard the author writes, and the capture refuses to write one it can see differs. A fence asks with `capture` on its `expect` line, which stays once written. The capture runs the oracle twice, from two scratch directories whose paths differ in length, and a line or status the two runs differ on, or a line the fence grammar would not read back as written, leaves the fence unwritten and `unpinned`, naming the line. What it writes is printed fence by fence and held as any fence is.

Revision 171 adds what writes a plain fence's body (#3455, as Mitchell ruled on #3429). `xtask witness --accept --bodies`, scoped as `--accept` is, writes each plain fence the oracle does not meet from the instrument's own two runs of the oracle, the recipe, its normalisation and the second run apart that a capture stands on, and leaves a fence the oracle meets byte for byte. Each authored line whose wildcards the oracle's line still meets in both runs, by the matcher the judge reads a fence with, is kept as written, and the oracle's line is written for the rest. A line the two runs differ on that no kept line meets, a status the fence pins and the oracle does not give, and a fence a `trace:` row covers, whose body is the engine's side of the row, are refused: nothing is written, and the verdict's note names why. A `capture` fence and `exit=` are written as `--accept` writes them. What it writes is printed fence by fence, as a capture's is, for the commit to name. No script rewrites a body from a php run of its own.

Revision 111 adds the fence that pins the engine's side of a `trace:` row (#1650, #1728). A `trace:` row's `covers` may name `witness:` ids. A covered fence is held to its pinned `expect` against the engine alone: the oracle still runs and its signal, status or bytes are recorded in the reading, but they are not judged. This is how a witness pins the engine's side of a row where the oracle crashes or prints bytes php does not define. The fence comes to `covered`, counted on the crate's reading, whose message carries the oracle's status or signal and each stream's length and blob id; `--accept` captures a bare `exit` from the engine's run, the one the fence is held to, and a `capture` on such a fence writes nothing, since what it writes is the oracle's streams. A fence no accepted `trace:` row covers whose oracle ends on a signal it does not pin is a bork, as it was; revision 178 adds `signal=<n>`, which `--accept` writes onto a bare `exit` where the run ended on a signal, and which a run meets by ending on that signal alone.

Revision 61 adds the one fence the linked engine does not serve. A fence whose `expect` line carries `process` says its snippet changes the process it runs in: it forks, sends or takes a signal, or sets the process's ids, session or limits. The harness runs its lane on threads, and in such a process a `fork()` copies one thread of many with whatever the others held, a signal lands on whichever thread the kernel picks, and a limit or an id set is the harness's own; design revision 35 builds pcntl for the single-threaded `php` binary alone for the same reason. So such a fence is served by the `php` binary in a process of its own, under the recipe exactly as the oracle is, and held by the same rule. The binary is `php`, `sapi/cli`'s host (ADR-0008) as every other lane builds it (revision 85), which the lane builds with cargo at the run's first such fence; a request of it records what it entered only where `PHP_WITNESS_TRACE` is set, so the binary the phpt lane runs and the one this fence is served by are one build. Coverage is observed as for any fence (§4.5 condition 2, revision 58): every request the binary serves appends the natives it entered to the file `PHP_WITNESS_TRACE` names, a forked child's request its own beside its parent's, and the lane reads them as it reads the linked engine's. The mark is on the fence and not on the crate, because posix's and the engine's fences are mostly of the other kind; every fence of `ext/pcntl` carries it. What kept the harness unforked was that the linked engine did not carry `pcntl`; since revision 84 the linked engine is `php-linked`, a process of its own that carries it, so a fence that forks and is not marked forks that process and never the harness, and the linked runner's special case for a child coming back into the lane (#1054) is gone.

Revision 110 adds a host's own lines (#1975, ruled on #2468). A host test that holds the oracle's bytes becomes data (a command line, an environment, files laid in a scratch directory, and the normalisations it names) that an xtask instrument runs against the engine's host and the oracle's host from `oracle.toml`, asserting the md5 first, as `witness` does for fences. No `seams.toml` row is needed: the pin stays xtask's alone (§4.3), the `harness` seam keeps `consumers = []`, and `sapi/*` gains no edge. Stdout, stderr, the exit status and the scratch directory's files after the run are held equal, under the normalisations the line names and none by default, and a pinned host that is missing or whose md5 is not the pin's fails the line and is never a skip. The data is a host crate's `tests/lines.toml`, whose grammar is `xtask/src/witness/host.rs`'s; the instrument is `witness`, which judges a crate's lines among its fences and writes the crate one reading over both. Its negative control is `xtask/fixtures/witness/host-line-unmet`.

---

## 9. Architecture fitness

The fitness functions are the manifest's numbers and edges: `api_items`, the lock set, the edges, the ceilings, the `unsafe` homes, plus the perf bars and the gates. The deletion test per crate stays inferential and per revision. The weekly exit bar (r1 §11, r2 §11) is read off the instruments' readings, its pass or fail what they show; "measured, not negotiated" (r1 §12) is what the readings say rather than a judgement, which is what makes it true when an agent reads it.

---

## 10. Maintenance layer: keeping the harness honest

**Rule 1: a ledger row for every repeated failure.** The design's ledger gains a "From this engine" table, Keep/Reject shape, one row when a sensor catches the same class of agent mistake twice; the evidence column is a query over the log. The consequence is a change to a guide or a sensor, in HE's order: a type or visibility change first, then a manifest rule with a `reason`, then a hook, and last a sentence in the map.

**Rule 2: ADRs stay reversals**, and reach the agent at the moment they matter: every manifest rule that an ADR decided carries the number, and every failure text prints it.

**Rule 3: the revision is a harness change.** Each revision of the design of record lists what the manifest, the registry, the instruments or the locks gain or lose, and the commit that lands it. From revision 3 that section is checkable: the manifest diff in the same commit is the list.

**Rule 4: the map is regenerated, not edited.** `computed check` in CI over the six region files, `AGENTS.md`, `ext/AGENTS.md`, `data/INDEX.md`, `docs/divergence.md`, `harness-design.md` and `third-engine-design.md`; a hand edit into a region body is refused at pre-commit (CM ADR 0005) and denied at the tool call by `permit`'s region check.

**Rule 5: the oracle pin is a human decision with a nightly sensor.** `oracle.toml` is the only statement of the pin; the drift job re-runs witnesses against the installed php; an agent reports drift and never moves the pin. Design documents cite the file and never the number (§14).

**Rule 6: a denial that repeats is a ledger row.** `status --section permits` at the weekly bar; a rule that fired repeatedly across sessions gets a row under rule 1; a rule that never fired in two weeks is either respected or silent, and its fixture says which.

**Rule 7: one living document per subject.** This note is one file at its current state with a ledger on top; a revision edits the sections it changes and adds one ledger entry with what changed, why, and the check. Git is the dated record. The `research/harness-research.rN.md` files are the record of the two revisions before the rule and are not edited. §14 asks the design of record to take the same shape.

---

## 11. Division of labour

**Human-only.** What the harness's own three files say: the oracle pin and its recipes (`oracle.toml`); every ceiling, edge, `unsafe` home, forbidden type and rule of `seams.toml`; every reason on a divergence and every flip of a row to `accepted` (`docs/divergence.toml`). A person's hand as well as a person's decision is what any change to something one of them already carries takes — one changed ceiling, one widened edge, one dropped rule, one reworded reason — that being the half of a write which can lower the bar the work is measured against. `permit` denies the agent the `Edit` and the `Write` on all three, and a shell's write to them (`harness.self-shell`, #625). What only a person does stays the person's decision, and typing it becomes an agent's act once the person approved it in the session, through their answer to an `AskUserQuestion` that carries the change, which `permit` admits exactly (§4.4, #2247). What a person has settled on a ticket and an agent may then type without asking is the addition, below. ADRs, and what a revision of the design of record says; the deletion-test arguments. The wording is a person's only where the decision is unsettled: a sentence a person has settled on a ticket is an agent's to land, with the ledger row and the ticket named in the commit. A revision that states what a ruled ticket built, and reverses, widens or adds to no ruling, is an agent's to word and land, with its ledger row naming the ruling and the ticket; the person reads the ledger row at the merge, and a sentence the person reverts becomes a ticket of theirs. A deletion-test argument, an ADR, and a sentence answering a question the design is silent on stay a person's. `.claude/skills/**` is exempt from `harness.self` (#626); the rest of `.claude/**`, the hook JSON, the function-hook modules (#2283) and the plugin manifest stay a person's, and a session's transcript is written by Claude Code alone (#2282). Reviewing witness diffs after `--accept`. Sign-off on a perf claim, with the A/A control read by a person, the second half of a `bench` reading (§4.2). Licence decisions. Applying the cut order when a weekly bar is missed. `computed trust` once per checkout and once over its worktrees' directory. `computed run --force`. Charting a track at the next row of design §11, `/frontier chart <track> <days>`, and closing a ticket `/frontier work <track>` ruled past the row's destination. Answering a question the design of record is silent on, and the revision or ADR that then states it. Enabling the function hook (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_FUNCTION_HOOKS` in `.claude/settings.json`, the `functionHook` option, the workspace trust dialog). Reading `status --section permits` and writing the ledger row. Choosing which prose becomes a region.

**Agent-default.** Running the protocol's paired reps against the A/A control for a `bench` reading and writing it, the measurement stated and the conclusion left to the sign-off (§4.2). The run is the agent's and the claim on the number is a person's, so a criterion that wants one is two boxes, the reading an agent produces and the sign-off a person gives, and never one box that is both: a criterion phrased "measured under the protocol, and the reading is a person's to sign off" names nothing an agent can run and closes for nobody (#128). Porting bodies against `port-map` rows. Writing kernels and natives with witnesses; running `--accept`; adding the lock line in the same commit. Running `xtask done` before proposing a change and the per-crate subsets it names. Regenerating the extension template with `new-ext`. Running `computed run` on a stale region and re-staging. Proposing a manifest rule or a divergence row in a commit message or a PR description, never in the file, which is what an agent does while nothing is settled. Landing one that is settled: a row a person has ruled on a ticket reaches `seams.toml`, `oracle.toml` or the registry through `cargo xtask steer <file> --from <proposal>`, the sanctioned writer of the three, which applies a proposal only where it adds — every value the tracked file carries must stand unchanged in the proposal, at the path and in the order it stood in, or nothing is written and the path that would have moved is named. A key a table or an array-of-tables row does not carry is an addition like a whole row is, so `porting = true` on `[[crate]]` `ext/standard` lands the way `[regime.dump]` landed (#387, ledger 51); what that widens, an `exempt` on a rule that denies the agent among it, is named in the reading, in what the instrument prints and in the commit's diff, which is how a person reads about it in the same commit. Both halves are measured: #152 is the addition, the `[regime.dump]` block the maintainer settled on the ticket, labelled `ready-for-agent`, and an agent landed; #160 is the change beside it, the `xtask` ceiling from 14000 to 18000 by a person's hand, `steer` refusing a value the row already carried. An addition through the instrument, a change by hand. The judgement is at the write and not in the label, and stays there: `ticket read` holds a criterion naming one of the three to a note that says which instrument writes it, not to a refusal, and still fails a criterion naming a denied path no instrument writes at all (#153). Whether a row adds or changes is read from the file it would replace, exactly, and a criterion's prose cannot say it; a heuristic guessing would refuse tickets an agent can in fact work, which is the failure #153 exists to stop. Proposing a rule's fixture with the rule. Working a track's frontier: filing a batch from the chart with `/frontier work <track>`, putting a decision ticket to the sources with `/grill-design` and closing it on what they say, filing the `for-a-person` ticket a silent question makes. Closing a chart sheet the cap has filled, with the comment naming the sheet that continues it: a sheet carries no acceptance criteria, so `ticket.close` does not judge its close (`exempt_label`, ledger 53), while a ticket's close is still the instrument's alone.

**Shared, with the sensor as arbiter.** Interface growth: the agent adds the lock line, the person reviews the lock diff. The manifest: the agent proposes, the person commits, `cargo test -p xtask` and the fixtures arbitrate.

**On a track, the design of record answers.** A decision ticket is a question a build ticket waits on, and on an unattended track nobody sits at the table to answer it, so the sources are asked in a person's place: `/grill-design <n>` works the ticket's question tree in rounds and puts each question, in order, to the three design documents, then the ADRs under `docs/adr/`, then php-src at the tag `oracle.toml` pins, the first that decides it answering it. A resolution is a passage with the citation that names it, `third-engine-design.md §5.6`, `ADR-0006`, `Zend/zend_vm_def.h:1234 at php-8.5.9`, recorded on the ticket and closing it; the session's own preference lives in the recommendation beside the question and nowhere else, and where the two differ the passage is what is recorded. A source is silent when no passage decides the question as asked, when this document or the design marks the point open itself (§13) or when two sources disagree, since which one moves is a person's call. A silent question is a person's: it is filed `for-a-person`, naming the sentence the design needs and the section it belongs in, and wired to block the decision ticket and every build ticket under it, so that none of them sits in the frontier `track next` reads and the decision ticket is left open. An autonomous track therefore stalls on a person and never decides for one; that ending is the edge of autonomy, not a turn that failed. The sentence itself is written into the design by a person, a revision being this list's first paragraph.

---

## 12. Gaps

Items of revisions 1 and 2 with their outcome, then what revision 3 opens.

1. **No agent map file.** Closed on paper by §5; still to build.
2. **No local wiring of sensors.** Closed by §4.4 and §6 on the shipped command hook, which is certain; the function hook is an addition that costs nothing when it does not load.
3. **No rule for repeated agent failures.** Rules 1 and 6, evidence from the reading log.
4. **No negative controls for the instruments.** Closed for every manifest rule by the fixture-per-rule test (§4.1); the phpt runner keeps its nightly control; `bench` has the A/A control; `crosscheck`, `compile-identity` and `witness` still need one fixture each that must fail.
5. **No concurrency exploration for `Cache`.** Unchanged; revision 1 D.4.
6. **No harness for the harness's own size.** Closed: `[budget] harness` holds the hand-written lines of `.claude/**` and `harness-plugins/**` together, generated types (`*.d.ts`) excluded, and `xtask` keeps a row of its own; the desk plugin, `harness-plugins/desk/**` with its tests, is held apart under `[budget] harness-desk` where that row stands (#3491). What the row measures is how much prose and configuration a person writes for an agent to read, so the plugin's TypeScript counts against it and so does a skill under `.claude/skills/**`, which a session reads exactly as it reads the map. Revision 20 raised the ceiling rather than scoping the skills out, and the number is `seams.toml`'s and not this document's (§14).
7. **No statement of what an agent may run.** Closed by `lanes.nightly` and §11.
8. **The function hook fails open on `Bash` alone.** Narrowed three times, not closed. The command hook beneath it fails open too when no candidate judges at all, and a stale release build opened exactly this hole on 2026-09-08, for both adapters at once because both resolved the binary in the same order, leaving the twin of §6 as the only thing holding (revision 11). Revision 13 narrows what is left to `Bash`: an `Edit` or a `Write` that no candidate judged is denied, the two adapters no longer resolve in one order, and a build stale in its rules or its own code refuses rather than judging quietly. Revision 175 narrows the module to the same line: an `Edit` or a `Write` it could not judge, whether no candidate judged or the hook threw, is denied in the module, so on a login where another mod's allow outranks a `PreToolUse` block there is no unjudged write left to approve. What remains is a `Bash` call that no judge saw and writes a file, which is handed on so that a harness too broken to judge can still be repaired; pre-commit is its twin. A person's file that parses but does not validate narrows the instruments that read what failed, and permit judges by what loaded; only a manifest that does not parse leaves a Bash call unjudged, and every refusal names the error's first line.
9. **A function hook cannot hold a turn.** Moot: `done --hook` is the command Stop hook.
10. **No test harness for `($, e, next)`.** Moot for the rules, which are Rust; the module is exercised only by loading the plugin, as revision 2 said. The command hook it sits over is held by `cargo test -p xtask --test permit`, which runs the command out of `hooks.json` (revision 11).
11. **`computed` discovery skips dot-directories.** Unchanged; the pre-commit and session commands name the local file.
12. **The one-hook-per-event constraint.** Moot: one adapter, one event.
13. **The manifest's grammar is new** (revision 3). Closed by revision 5: the manifest was written before day 1, the example was not TOML, and four predicates were missing; both are fixed in §4.1.
14. **`permit`'s latency at the tool call** (revision 3). The bar and the fallback are in §4.4; measured on day 1, when `permit` exists. The hook shapes it reads and writes are recorded (D.16).
15. **The design of record is still seven files** (revision 3). Closed by design ledger 8: `third-engine-design.md` and `jit-design.md` are living documents; the map's item 2 names them.
16. **Two schedules for divergences** (revision 3). The registry counts the `debt` rows of every kind against one target, and each other cause on its own line (revision 116, #2460); whether the trace and runner kinds should have their own targets is a question for the JIT note's next revision.
17. **Four tests the phpt runner disagrees with the oracle about** (revision 12). Run under the pinned oracle as the binary under test, which is the runner's own control, `sapi/cli/tests` leaves four differing: `017.phpt`, an interactive shell; `022.phpt`, a spawn over a unix socket; `php_cli_server_ipv4_error_message.phpt`, a listening server; and `std_streams.phpt`, which wants a terminal on a stream it did not capture. The other subsets run agree: 165 of 165 in `ext/pcre`, 88 of 88 in `ext/json`, 49 of 49 in `ext/ctype`. They are the runner's difference from upstream's, not the engine's, and their home is a row of kind `runner` each; the rows are not written, because an open row is a gate `done` holds shut (§4.5 condition 4) and the reason on it is a person's to write (§4.3, §11). Until then this item is where they are recorded. The same paragraph names the runner's one difference by construction: `SKIPIF` and `CLEAN` are answered by the oracle, where upstream answers them with the binary under test.

---

## 13. Open questions and things not verified

**D.1 to D.6, D.8 to D.15** of the earlier revisions stand as written there; the ones that matter for this revision are D.2 (where the clippy list lives; now generated, so the answer is the manifest's), D.3 (rustdoc and the `expect` fence: answered by measurement, the `=` on the fence line trips `rustdoc::invalid_codeblock_attributes` under `-D warnings` while the doctests still run, so the crates that carry witnesses allow that lint at their root, design §8), D.5 (the engine binary under `run-tests.php`, a lane control until the CLI flags exist), and D.10 (what in Function Hooks is stable; now only the fifteen-line adapter depends on it).

**D.7, settled.** Every check is an `xtask` subcommand. The shipped command hooks and pre-commit are the whole local harness in month one. The function hook is one optional adapter.

**D.16 The hook JSON shapes, answered.** Exercised on Claude Code 2.1.263 with a headless run (`data/pre-day-1.md` §4). PreToolUse stdin carries `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `prompt_id`, `permission_mode`, `hook_event_name`, `tool_name`, `tool_input` and `tool_use_id`; Stop stdin carries the same head plus `stop_hook_active` and `last_assistant_message`. A deny is `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"<text>"}}` on stdout with exit 0: the tool never ran and the model saw the text. A hold at Stop is `{"decision":"block","reason":"<text>"}`: the model continued on the reason and the hook fired again with `stop_hook_active` true, on which `done --hook` must pass. Day 1 runs `permit` against these recorded shapes as fixtures.

**D.17 `$.process.run` and stdin, answered.** The function-hook noun takes `stdin` (`ProcessRunInit` in the declarations Claude Code ships, `harness-plugins/types`), so the adapter passes the event on stdin, the shape the command hook reads, and no argument-length limit bounds a `Write`; `permit --json` stays for a caller with the call in hand. The test `a_large_write_on_stdin_is_judged_whole` judges a `Write` of over three megabytes whose lock sits at the end of the file (revision 10).

**D.18 The key as freshness.** `git rev-parse HEAD:<path>` gives a tree hash of committed content only; for the working tree `done` must hash the crate's files itself. A content hash over the crate's sources in byte-order sorted path order, the `computed` snapshot shape (CM-spec:100), is what `key` holds. Cost: the same as `computed check` over the same inputs, seconds.

**D.20 The runner has the tree and not the binary.** The pin names one php, a macOS keg, and the ubuntu runner cannot have it, so `XTASK_PROMISED` promises the tree alone in CI and every test that spawns the oracle skips there. Whether the pin should name a php per platform, each with its digest, so the witness lane and the runner control run in CI too, is open; what would settle it is a second `[oracle]` entry the oracle module selects by platform, with the drift lane holding both digests. Until then a witness reading is taken on the maintainer's machine.

**D.19 `parking_lot::*` in `deny_types`.** `clippy::disallowed-types` matches fully qualified paths, so a wildcard is not a clippy feature; the generator expands the manifest's wildcard into the paths the lock packages actually contain, read from `Cargo.lock`. The expansion walks the package's public modules from its `lib.rs` with syn, inline and file modules alike, and names each type at the path a public module makes it reachable at; a `pub use` glob or a `pub mod` at a `#[path]` is refused naming where, and a type behind a `#[cfg]` is rendered `allow-invalid`, denied where the build has it (#1910). Unverified that the expansion is complete for a type a macro defines, or for a crate that re-exports a lock under another name; the `permit` regex and the review of `api.lock` are the twins.

---

## 14. What this design asks of the design of record

Design ledger 8 (2026-09-07) took items 2, 3, 5 and 6 below in full and item 1 in part; design ledger 9 took item 1 in full, since `seams.toml` and `oracle.toml` now exist and the design cites them. Item 4 is the harness's own and is unchanged. The list is kept as the record of what was asked.

1. **The numbers move to `seams.toml`.** §4.1's crate map, §4.2's dependency graph, §4.5's lock set, §10's `unsafe` homes and lint list, and §12's budgets are stated once in the manifest, and the revision cites it. The inventory found the lock set enumerated four ways (`heap`, `api`, `sapi`, `ops`, `effects` in r2; plus `unit` in r3; `tier`, `heap`, `unit`, `ops`, `api` in r7): the manifest's set is `heap`, `unit`, `ops`, `api`, `sapi`, `tier`; `effects.lock` is retired, its content being `ops.lock`'s effects column. ADR-0004's consequence "`unsafe` is permitted in `php_heap` and `ext/*/src/ffi/` only" is superseded by r7's four homes and should say so in a one-line amendment.
2. **The oracle pin is cited, never stated.** r1 §8.6 and §9.2 say 8.5.9; r6, r7 and the JIT notes cite php-src 8.5.7; r6 §7.1 says the oracle and the stubs are pinned to one tag. `oracle.toml` is that statement and the documents point at it.
3. **One divergence registry.** r1 §8.5's table, r4 §12's runner list and JIT revision 3's recorded differences table are one file, `docs/divergence.toml`, with kinds, each the lane that reads a row, and causes; the day-30 target counts the rows whose cause is `debt` (revision 116).
4. **`xtask` gains `permit`, `done`, `status`, `vocab`, `new-ext`**, and the oracle module gains `compare`. The 14k budget stands; revision 3's estimate for the five is under 3k.
5. **The revision rule of r3 §10 is reopened.** "Revisions are files; the highest `N` is the source of record" costs every reader seven files and lets stale sentences survive (`xtask opgen --check`, deleted in r2 and still standing in r1's text; the two `unsafe` lists). The ask: `third-engine-design.md` and `jit-design.md` as living documents with the numbered "what changed and why" ledger each revision already writes, git as the dated record, ADRs unchanged. The `rN` files stay as records. This is not an ADR reversal, since no ADR made the rule; it is a decision of the design and its owner's to make.
6. **A "Harness changes" section per revision** (rule 3), which from now on is the manifest and registry diff in the landing commit.
